# AI-FluxMux — STATUS

> **New chat? Read `BRIEFING.md` first** — it has the specific open items, key file map, and testing instructions.


Current version: **0.3.1 beta** (see `FluxMux.Updates.Core/AppVersionComparer.cs` → `FluxMuxAppInfo`).

## ✅ RESOLVED: YaRN mid-chat switch "failure" — see `YARN_SWITCH_DIAG.md`

The YaRN switch was **never a bug** — it's a ~7.3-min cold prefill of a ~157K-token prompt on a
GPU+CPU offload profile after KV cache is destroyed on switch. Three fixes landed (10/27):
(1) Hail Mary middle-cut now drops the **oldest** turns (was dropping the wrong end — a no-op when
needed), preserving system + initial brief + last task + recent turns; (2) honest "turn too large"
messaging when even a trim can't fit the turn; (3) live prefill progress indicator with ETA.
**Full suite: 1717 passed, 0 failed. Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT
deployed** — close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Context fixes: YaRN switch + per-turn forward-prune + Hail Mary (10/26)

**Full handoff + resumable plan: `PLAN_CONTEXT_FIXES.md`.** All steps implemented + verified
10/26. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** — user must close
the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

- **A. YaRN profile-switch state refresh** — `FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` switch path now refreshes
  `local_vram_context` + `local_extend_context_into_ram` from the managed fields when they differ
  (previously only stamped at launch). Fixes the "switching to a YaRN profile no longer smooth"
  regression.
- **B. Per-turn forward-prune throttle (new `Services/ForwardPrune.cs`)** — a per-turn fill
  throttle that trims each turn's own waste (dedupe + collapse repeated tool loops + truncate
  oversized older tool results; NO image strip) on every local turn from the first, using the
  model's measured token count. Distinct from conventional compaction (summarize older turns) and
  Hail Mary (one-off emergency rear-prune). Gated on a new per-profile toggle
  `LocalForwardPruneEnabled` (default on), read live each turn so it toggles mid-chat without a
  reload. Exposed in the Quick Select panel ("Context management" section).
- **C. Hail Mary measured-count + pulse-clear** — `RearContextPrune` now uses the model's measured
  token count (the over-counting estimate made the button a no-op on Qwen). When the armed
  rear-prune is consumed, the gateway re-measures, updates the context gauge (so it visibly drops),
  and fires `ReportHailMaryApplied` → `MainViewModel` clears `HailMaryQueued` (stops the
  persistent pulse) on both success and no-op.
- **C2. Hail Mary middle-cut calibration** — The middle-cut loop in `RearContextPrune` now uses a
  calibrated estimate (scaled to the measured token count) for the stop condition, instead of the
  raw 4-chars/token estimate which over-counts for dense tokenizers like Qwen. This prevents the
  loop from stopping too early. Hail Mary remains a one-off emergency rear-prune (image-strip +
  middle-cut only); the forward-prune stages (dedupe, loop-collapse, result-truncate) belong in
  the per-turn `ForwardPrune.cs` task, not in Hail Mary.
- **D. Terminal logging + turn progress** — The gateway's `Log()` now also fires a `ReportLog`
  event, wired through `RuntimeService.GatewayLogLine` to the UI diagnostic terminal
  (`AppendDiagnosticEntry("GW", line)`). This means Hail Mary results, forward-prune results,
  context measurements, and route decisions all appear live in the terminal. Additionally,
  `[turn]` progress markers are logged at each pipeline stage with cumulative ms since turn start,
  so the user can see exactly where a delay is occurring. **Log noise reduced:** Hail Mary now
  only runs when actually armed (the `WasArmed` flag), so it no longer logs a no-op on every turn.
  The per-turn log is now just 2 lines: `[turn] start · model=... · context=...` and
  `[turn] sending to local model · measured X tokens · cumulative Y ms · timeout Zs`.
- **E. Forward-prune speed fixes** — (1) No re-measure network call after pruning (was adding a
  full extra llama-server round-trip per pruned turn). (2) Dedupe stage now uses a cheap
  fingerprint (role + content length + FNV-1a hash + tool-call count) instead of full
  `ToJsonString()` on every message every turn; full serialization only on collision candidates.
- **Tests:** +6 `ForwardPruneTests` + 1 `HailMaryCompactionTests` (measured-count rear prune).
  Full suite: **1713 passed, 0 failed**.
- **Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors, 1713/1713 tests pass),
  **NOT deployed** — user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.
- **Note:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` was restored from the 13:54 source backup during this work
  (the live file had drifted from git HEAD, which predates the Hail Mary button). The forward-prune
  checkbox was re-added on top.

## Permanent invariants (always in effect)

- **Cross-system / DPI / resolution safety (user-stated, 10/17):** This app must work on different systems with different display resolutions, DPI scaling, and monitor configurations. **Consequences for any layout/sizing work:**
  - Do NOT hardcode pixel dimensions that assume a specific screen size or DPI (e.g. a fixed `Width="800"` that overflows a 1366×768 laptop at 100%, or is tiny on a 4K monitor at 200%).
  - Prefer **proportional / star sizing** (`*`, `1*`, `2*`), `Auto`, `MinWidth`/`MaxWidth` floors, and `LayoutTransform`/`UiScaleFactor` where already used — these scale with the actual available space and DPI.
  - If a pixel value is unavoidable (e.g. a minimum readable width), it must be a **floor** (MinWidth) that only kicks in when space is genuinely too small, never a fixed size that competes with the available space.
  - Always consider: small laptop (1366×768, 100% DPI), large desktop (1920×1080, 100%), 4K (3840×2160, 150–200% DPI), and multi-monitor with mixed DPI. A layout that only works on the developer's machine is a bug.
  - **Specifically for the graph/terminal split:** the `1,Auto,2` star split broke (huge right margin) — likely a `GridSplitter` interaction, NOT a DPI issue. When re-introducing the 1/3 default, use a mechanism that is DPI-safe: a proportional `GridLength` (e.g. `Width="33*"` on Column 0 with `Width="67*"` on Column 2, or keep `*,Auto,*` and nudge the sash via code-behind using `Bounds.Width * 0.33`) rather than a fixed pixel `Width`. Verify at multiple DPI scales before considering it done.

## Hail Mary picker "no slider/tickbox" (10/26)

**Report:** the Hail Mary Compactor button on the Diagnostics top bar shows no picker (no prune-strength radio "slider" or "strip images" tickbox) when clicked.

**Diagnosis:** the picker popup (`HailMaryPickerPopup`, `Views/MainWindow.axaml` ~3158) is fully present in source AND in the deployed `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` (verified: `HailMaryPickerPopup`, `HailMaryStopToBoolConverter`, `HailMaryButton_Click`, `HailMaryPickerArm_Click`, `HailMaryAggressionStop`, `HailMaryStripImages` all present; XAML well-formed: Popup/Border/StackPanel balanced). The picker is **uncommitted** work (last commit 10/24). No crash/XAML-exception in the 6h log window.

**Root-cause hypothesis:** the button sits in the Diagnostics panel's **top row** (`Grid` at line 3112, `ClipToBounds="True"`, inside `DiagnosticsPanelBorder` line 3105 `ClipToBounds="True"`). The popup uses `Placement="Bottom"` anchored to the button. A `Popup` is normally rendered in a top-level overlay, but inside a `ClipToBounds=True` container it can be clipped/hidden on some Avalonia compositor paths — which would make the button appear to "do nothing."

**Hardening fix applied (10/26):** set `StaysOpen="True"` on `HailMaryPickerPopup` so a stray outside-click can't instantly close it (the picker already closes explicitly via the Arm/Cancel handlers + `HailMaryButton_Click` toggle). This makes the picker robust regardless of the overlay/clipping behavior. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` (artifacts\verifyhm\) → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**; `StaysOpen` + `HailMaryPickerPopup` confirmed present in the compiled DLL. If the picker still does not appear after deploy, the next step is to move the popup out of the clipping container (e.g. anchor it to a non-clipped parent) or render it via `Flyout`/`Window` overlay.

**Deploy state: NOT deployed** — user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart, then click the Hail Mary button to confirm the picker (3 radios + 1 checkbox + Armed/Cancel) appears.

## What works (recent)

- **Graph X-axis: tighter spacing, right-justified end label, taller plots** (10/26): Closed the vertical gaps around the X-axis on the TTFT + Throughput graphs and right-justified the axis-end label so the plots gain height in the available space. (1) **X-axis number gap:** the `TtftXAxisLabelCanvas` / `ThroughputXAxisLabelCanvas` had `Height="16"` + `Margin="2,2,2,0"` (a 2px top gap above the numbers + 4px of unused canvas below them). The Y-axis code-behind applies a buffer (~half the Y-label height, ~5.5px) as a top/bottom margin on the plot border so the top/bottom Y-axis numbers can sit centered on their ticks without clipping — this buffer pushes the plot's outer bottom edge up by ~5.5px, creating a gap between the plot and the X-axis numeral row. Fixed by reducing the canvas to `Height="11"` (matching the 9pt numeral height) + `Margin="0,-6,0,0"`: the negative top margin pulls the numeral row up into the buffer zone so the numbers sit ~6px closer to the plot's bottom edge. (2) **Axis-title row:** the X-axis title (e.g. "Tokens") was `FontSize="11"` + `HorizontalAlignment="Center"`; changed to `FontSize="10"` + `HorizontalAlignment="Right"` so it right-justifies at the axis end and the row is 1px shorter. (3) **Right-justified end label:** added `IsAxisMax` to `XAxisLabelItem`; the view model now flags the final (axis-max) label in all three `UpdateAxisLabels` branches (stress-test fill-levels, no-fill-levels evenly-spaced, and per-turn data-range). `PositionAxisLabelSet` in the code-behind right-aligns that label (its right edge at the axis end) instead of centering it under the tick. The no-fill-levels branch now also populates the `XAxisLabels` collection (previously only the `XAxisLabel1-6` properties), so the right-justification applies there too. (4) **Inter-graph gap:** the inner graph grid's `RowSpacing` was `6` (6px blank above *and* below each Auto row → ~12px of empty vertical space between the TTFT X-axis title and the Throughput title). Reduced to `0` so the two graphs sit flush together and the title row sits flush against the numeral row (no gap between numerals and title, or between the TTFT title and the Throughput title). The plot areas reclaim all the freed height. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (new `IsAxisMax` property; flag set in 3 branches; no-fill-levels branch now fills the collection), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (2× X-axis label canvas `Height` 16→11 + `Margin` 0,-6,0,0; 2× X-axis title `FontSize` 11→10 + `HorizontalAlignment` Right; inner graph grid `RowSpacing` 6→0), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (`PositionAxisLabelSet` right-aligns `IsAxisMax` labels). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `TelemetryGraphViewModelTests` 36/36 + `ContextFillStress` 11/11 pass. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **DIAGNOSIS (10/26): VRAM-only → YaRN switch "times out" — root cause found, NOT a launch bug.**
  - **Symptom:** Switching from a VRAM-only context variant to the YaRN extended-context variant of the same model makes the next chat turn "time out."
  - **Evidence (from `C:\AI_Workbench\Logs\Proxy_Context_Events.jsonl` + `Proxy_Cloud_Handshake.log`):**
    1. On the VRAM-only profile the turn 400s: `local_context_filling ... n_ctx=262144 n_prompt=236858 status=400` — the prompt (~236K) already fills the 262K VRAM window.
    2. User switches to YaRN (393K) and retries the SAME turn. The gateway forwards the full ~236K prompt to the freshly-loaded YaRN model, which is now **RAM-spilled** (GPU+CPU, `local_vram_context=163840`).
    3. llama-server must **prefill ~236K tokens from RAM spill** before the first byte. That prefill exceeds the **Long-load first-byte** hang deadline (600s). The gateway aborts: `request_timeout` + postmortem "llama-server was still prefilling a large (RAM-spilled) prompt and did not start a reply in time. ... Long-load first-byte hit 867s quiet (the limit is 600)."
  - **Key insight:** The switch itself WORKS (YaRN loads, `local_phase=ready`, later turns stream fine in 2–15s). The "timeout" is the **first turn after the switch** — a huge prompt that the VRAM-only profile could not fit, now being prefilled from RAM spill on the YaRN profile. It is a prefill-timeout, not a launch/switch failure.
  - **Why it feels like "the switch broke":** the user's intent is "switch to YaRN so this big turn fits." The turn now *fits* context-wise (393K > 236K) but the RAM-spill prefill is slower than the 600s hang clock, so the turn is killed anyway.
  - **Candidate fixes (decide with user):**
    - (A) **Raise the long-load first-byte deadline** for RAM-spilled large-prompt turns (e.g. scale `ThinkingFirstByteSeconds` with prompt size / spill), so a legitimately-slow prefill isn't aborted. Touches `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (~1537) + `LocalStreamHangPolicy`.
    - (B) **Compact the forwarded history on the first post-switch turn** so the prompt is smaller and prefills faster (the VRAM-only profile already 400'd on it; the YaRN profile should compact it down before forwarding).
    - (C) **Better UX:** when a turn 400s on context-fill and the user switches to a larger-context variant, warn that the first retry may be slow (RAM-spill prefill) rather than silently timing out.
  - **Status:** diagnosed only. No code changed yet. Awaiting user decision on which fix to implement.
  - **REGRESSION CONFIRMED (10/26):** User reports this switch used to work in seconds. Diffing current `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` against ALL 10/24 backups (`0.3.2_0943/0947/1717/2245`) shows **none** of them contain the two new YaRN compact changes — they all landed in the 10/26 build. The two regressions:
    1. **Auto-compact trigger capped at VRAM-resident context** (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` ~2297): when `local_extend_context_into_ram` is true, the compact trigger is forced down to `local_vram_context` (163840) instead of 80% of the YaRN window (314572). So a ~236K prompt now triggers forward-compaction on the YaRN profile.
    2. **Port-rules `filling=true` at VRAM boundary** (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` ~2360): same condition forces `filling=true` when `promptTokens >= local_vram_context`.
    - **Why it times out:** the ~236K prompt is one big over-full turn (tool-pair-locked), so `TryCompactPayload` bails ("too few turns, or one over-full turn") and the Hail Mary prune saves 0 tokens (log: "rear-context prune could not drop any turns"). The prompt is forwarded **uncompacted** to the RAM-spilled YaRN model → 236K prefill from RAM → exceeds the 600s long-load first-byte deadline → `request_timeout`. Before the regression, the prompt was forwarded as-is and prefilled in seconds.
  - **Stress-test "clear context" RULED OUT:** `ClearLocalModelContextAfterStressTestAsync` (MainViewModel ~9818) only POSTs a 1-token "Clear" prompt to the daemon port when the stress button is clicked, and swallows all errors. It cannot affect a later retry. The "rejoin proactive prune" (`_firstForwardAfterStartup`) also never fired (log: always "9 tokens, no proactive prune needed").
  - **Fix decision (user):** revert the two YaRN VRAM-cap compact changes (restore 10/24 behavior: forward the prompt as-is, let YaRN handle the spill) OR make the compact actually shrink the over-full turn. Awaiting user choice.
  - **FIX APPLIED (10/26):** Reverted both YaRN VRAM-cap compact changes in `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs`:
    1. Removed the auto-compact trigger cap at `local_vram_context` (~line 2297) — trigger is back to `triggerPercent` of the full YaRN window.
    2. Removed the port-rules `filling=true` at `local_vram_context` (~line 2339) — `filling` is back to `PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact` only.
    - Restored 10/24 behavior: the prompt is forwarded as-is; YaRN handles the RAM spill. The first turn after a VRAM→YaRN switch no longer triggers a futile compact that can't shrink the over-full turn.
    - **Also fixed a pre-existing XAML break** in `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (unrelated to the revert, from the 10/26 GPU-pill/Hail-Mary work): the `<Border>` opened at line 3163 inside `HailMaryPickerPopup` was never closed — added `</Border>` after the inner `</StackPanel>` (now line 3201) before `</Popup>`. Without this the whole project failed to build (AVLN1001 Border/Popup mismatch).
    - **Verified:** `dotnet build` (separate output dir `artifacts\testrun\`) → Build succeeded, 0 errors. Full test suite → **1675 passed, 0 failed**.
    - **Deploy state: compiled + tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed.** User must: close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.
    - **ADDITIONAL FIX (10/26):** Also reverted the **rejoin proactive prune** (`_firstForwardAfterStartup` block, ~lines 534-585) — a new 10/26 feature that runs `HailMaryCompaction.Salvage` on the first forward after gateway startup when the prompt is ≥50% of context. User reported that after launching YaRN, even a *new* chat hangs with no response. The rejoin prune is the only new code in the forward path that could interfere with the first forward after a profile switch. Reverting it restores 10/24 behavior (no proactive prune on first forward). **Verified:** build 0 errors, full suite **1675 passed, 0 failed**. Deploy state: compiled + tested, NOT deployed.
    - **⚠️ UNPROVEN — see `REVERTS_2026-10-26.md`:** All three reverts above (VRAM-cap compact trigger, port-rules `filling=true`, rejoin proactive prune) are a *hypothesis*, not a confirmed root cause. They restore exact 10/24 behavior, but we have not yet deployed and observed the fix in the running app. The doc file describes, block-by-block, what each reverted piece did, why it's a suspect, what was confirmed absent in 10/24, test impact (none), and what remains unknown after deploy. **Do not treat these reverts as the solution until a post-deploy test proves otherwise.** If the VRAM→YaRN switch or the "new chat hangs" symptom persists after deploy, the cause is elsewhere (likely the YaRN llama-server itself) and these reverts should be re-examined.

- **GPU settings: pill toggle, three-group layout, heading hierarchy, batch switcher rename, section backgrounds, UI prefs inline, system apps listed** (10/25): Reworked the GPU & VRAM section. (1) Replaced the checkbox + status-label toggle with a new **`GpuPillToggle`** control (two-segment pill: "Discrete GPU" | "Motherboard GPU"; active side filled with accent + bold, inactive muted; non-switchable = greyed static badge). The pill is the single source of truth for GPU state, so "active" and "intended" can't disagree. (2) Three groups in order: **user-facing apps** (switchable, listed individually, split by current GPU into "GPU / dedicated VRAM" and "iGPU / shared RAM"), **Protected apps** (pinned like llama-server, listed individually, greyed at 0.55 opacity, static pill, no toggle), **System & background processes** (listed individually, greyed at 0.55 opacity, static pill, no toggle). (3) Headless/no-VRAM processes that are neither pinned nor system are excluded from the user-facing lists. (4) iGPU section always visible with a muted placeholder when empty. (5) Section headers standardized to `FontSize=13, SemiBold, Opacity=0.85`; uniform `Margin="0,10,0,0"` between sections. (6) Batch switcher renamed to **"Batch Windows affinity switcher"** with a tooltip advising only eligible apps/processes are switched (pinned + system protected); Apply button stays adjacent. (7) Added `ToggleGpuAppCommand` to the ViewModel (replaces the old code-behind `OnGpuAppToggle_Click`). (8) **Crash fix:** the pill's `ApplyState()` was hitting a `NullReferenceException` on the `x:Name`-generated fields when called before the XAML tree was populated. Fixed by looking up the named controls via `FindControl` at call time (null-safe no-op if not yet available). (9) **Section backgrounds:** each GPU section now sits in a rounded Border with a distinct background tint — VRAM apps use `FluxMutedBackground`, iGPU apps use `FluxTintBlueBackground`, Protected apps use `FluxTintAmberBackground` (with rows at 0.55 opacity to signal non-switchable), System & background uses `FluxMutedBackground` (rows at 0.55 opacity). (10) **UI Preferences:** the "UI enforce clean width" checkbox is now inlined on the same row as the UI scale and UI theme dropdowns. (11) **System & background apps listed individually:** instead of an aggregated single-line summary, system/background processes (dwm, explorer, csrss, etc.) are now listed as individual greyed rows with a static pill, matching the Protected apps styling. Added `SystemGpuAppRows`/`SystemGpuAppsCountText` to the ViewModel. **Changes:** `Controls/GpuPillToggle.axaml` + `.cs` (new control), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (new section layout with tinted Borders, pill bindings, heading hierarchy, batch switcher rename/tooltip, UI prefs inline, system apps individual list), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (new `ProtectedGpuAppRows`/`HasProtectedGpuApps`/`ProtectedGpuAppsCountText`/`SystemGpuAppRows`/`SystemGpuAppsCountText` properties, rewritten `RefreshGpuAppRows` with three-group logic, new `ToggleGpuAppCommand`). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\gpupill\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Updater dialog: progress bar / status text no longer overflow the dialog** (10/25): In `UpdaterForm` (the WinForms progress dialog shown by the standalone `FluxMux.Updates.exe` updater), the `_statusLabel` and `_titleLabel` were `AutoSize=true` with no width cap. Long status lines (from `Console.WriteLine` → `FormTextWriter.FlushLine` → `SetStatus`) grew wider than the progress bar, the `GrowOnly` `FlowLayoutPanel` widened, and the form overflowed the dialog. Fixed by setting `AutoEllipsis=true` and `MaximumSize=(contentWidth, 0)` on both labels so long lines truncate instead of expanding the dialog. - **GPU process list: added PerformanceCounter warm-up** (10/25): The "Programs using graphics memory" table was not populating because the `PerformanceCounter.RawValue` property returns 0 on the first read (the counter needs to be initialized first). Added a `counter.NextValue()` warm-up call before reading `RawValue` in both `CollectWindowsGpuLocalUsageCore` (for "GPU Process Memory" / "Local Usage") and `ReadGpuAdapterMemoryByKey` (for "GPU Adapter Memory" / "Dedicated Usage" and "Shared Usage"). This ensures the counters are properly initialized before reading their values, so the GPU process list should now populate correctly. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (added `_ = counter.NextValue();` warm-up calls in two methods). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Hail Mary button: pulse animation + removed "queued" text + GPU scan cooldown** (10/25): Three changes. (1) **Removed the "Hail Mary compaction queued..." text** — since the button now acts immediately (token-driven middle-cut), the static "queued" text is no longer needed. The `HailMaryOutcome` text (which shows the actual result) remains. (2) **Added a pulse animation** to the Hail Mary button while compaction is underway. When `HailMaryQueued` is true, a `DispatcherTimer` (80ms interval) pulses the button's opacity between 0.5 and 1.0 using a sine wave. When `HailMaryQueued` is false, the pulse stops and the opacity resets to 1.0. The pulse is driven by `StartHailMaryPulse()` / `StopHailMaryPulse()` in `MainWindow.axaml.cs`, hooked up via the `OnVmPropertyChanged` handler watching `HailMaryQueued`. (3) **GPU scan cooldown** — added a 30-second cooldown to `ScanGpuVramFlyoutAsync` to avoid redundant scans at startup when the Settings tab selection, GPU/VRAM section expand, and popup open all trigger the scan in quick succession. The `_lastGpuVramScanUtc` timestamp is updated after each successful scan; if a scan completed within the last 30 seconds, the new scan is skipped. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (removed "queued" TextBlock, added `x:Name="HailMaryButton"`), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (added `_hailMaryPulseTimer` / `_hailMaryPulsePhase` fields, `StartHailMaryPulse()` / `StopHailMaryPulse()` / `OnHailMaryPulseTick()` methods, hooked up in `OnVmPropertyChanged`), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (added `_lastGpuVramScanUtc` / `GpuVramScanCooldown` fields, added cooldown check in `ScanGpuVramFlyoutAsync`). **Note:** The "using apps table" not populating is a separate pre-existing issue — `CollectGpuProcessUses()` returns empty when both Windows Performance Counters ("GPU Process Memory") and `nvidia-smi` compute apps are unavailable. **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Hail Mary middle-cut is now token-driven (retroactive compaction)** (10/25): The old middle-cut kept a fixed number of recent turns (8, or 4 when over context) and dropped the rest. This was wrong for long chats: in a 500-turn chat, keeping 8 turns barely dents the prompt, so the cut couldn't free enough space and the turn still 400'd. The new `MiddleCutOlderTurns` is **token-driven**: it drops the oldest (least useful) turns one at a time until the estimated prompt tokens fit under the target (`contextTokens - headroom`), keeping at least `MiddleCutKeepTurns` (8) recent turns as a floor for conversational context. This scales with chat length — in a 500-turn chat it drops hundreds of turns; in a 12-turn chat it drops only what is needed. It still respects tool-pair safety (never splits a tool result from its call) and never drops the system prompt or the last real user task. The `Salvage` method now passes `target` (the token target) and `minKeepTurns` to `MiddleCutOlderTurns`. The Hail Mary button path in `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` no longer needs the over-context keep-turns override — the token-driven cut handles it. Log line: "middle-cut: dropped N older turn(s) to fit T tokens (kept the last real user task + M most recent turns)". **Changes:** `Services/HailMaryCompaction.cs` (rewrote `MiddleCutOlderTurns` to be token-driven, updated `Salvage` call site), `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (simplified the Hail Mary button path). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Rejoin-aware proactive compact** (10/25): When FluxMux restarts and Cline rejoins a chat, Cline re-sends the full conversation history, so the context gauge immediately shows "nearly full" even before any new turns. The regular auto-compact only fires when the prompt exceeds the trigger threshold (default 80% of context), so a rejoin at 50-79% left the history packed with no compaction. Added a `_firstForwardAfterStartup` flag to the gateway host. On the first non-probe forward after startup, if the forwarded history is already >= 50% of the context window AND the regular `TryApplyForwardCompact` did not already compact, the gateway now runs the non-destructive Hail Mary prune pipeline (stages 1-4: dedupe, loop-collapse, result-truncate, image-strip) proactively. This shortens the history before the first turn so the operator starts with a cleaner, shorter context. The flag is set to false after the first forward (or immediately if the history is < 50%), so it only fires once per gateway session. Probes are exempt. Log lines: "rejoin: first forward after startup carries N tokens (>= 50% of M context) — running proactive prune" / "rejoin proactive prune: <diagnostics>" / "rejoin: ... (< 50% ...) — no proactive prune needed". **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (added `_firstForwardAfterStartup` field + rejoin-aware proactive compact block after `TryApplyForwardCompact`). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Hail Mary button: fixed no-visual-feedback + button displacement + diagnosed "chat termination"** (10/25): Three issues. (1) **No visual feedback when pressed** — the button set `HailMaryQueued=true` (a static "queued…" text) but the gateway applies the middle-cut on the *next* forward and reports back asynchronously; there was no result shown. Added a `HailMaryOutcome` string property + `SetHailMaryOutcome()` helper (auto-clears after 8s via a `System.Threading.Timer`, marshals to UI thread). On click it shows "Armed — will cut on the next turn you send"; when the gateway reports back via `HailMaryOffered`, `ApplyHailMaryOffer` now sets `HailMaryOutcome` to the actual result (e.g. "middle-cut: dropped N turns…" or "could not drop any turns") and clears `HailMaryQueued`. A new outcome TextBlock in the Diagnostics row (Grid.Column 5) binds to `HailMaryOutcome` via the existing `StringToVisibility` converter. (2) **Button displaced upward** — the button and the queued TextBlock shared a `StackPanel Grid.Column="5"`; when the text appeared the panel grew and pushed the button up. Removed the StackPanel; the button and both TextBlocks are now direct Grid children of Column 5 (all `VerticalAlignment="Center"`), so they overlap in the same cell instead of stacking. (3) **"Unexplained chat termination"** — log analysis (`c:\AI_Workbench\Logs\Proxy_Cloud_Handshake.log`) shows the button IS clicked and the pipeline IS running, but the stage-5 middle-cut drops **zero** turns every time: `hail-mary (button): middle-cut could not drop any turns (too few turns or tool-pair-locked)` (5× this session). The middle-cut keeps the most recent 8 messages; when the forwarded history has ≤8 non-system messages (or they're tool-pair-locked), `keepStart < 1` and nothing is dropped. The turn then succeeds via YaRN/other stages — the "termination" the user saw is likely a separate issue (e.g. a long stream completing, or a context-overflow 400 on a *different* turn), NOT the button. The button is now visibly honest: it tells the operator when the cut actually dropped turns vs. when there was nothing to cut. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (removed StackPanel, added outcome TextBlock), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (added `HailMaryOutcome` property + `SetHailMaryOutcome`, updated `HailMaryShrinkTurn` + `ApplyHailMaryOffer`). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

Switched the button to `FontFamily="Wingdings, Segoe UI"` with content `] Hail Mary Compactor` — Wingdings code `0x5B` (`]`) is the praying-hands glyph and renders as a crisp single-color (white, matching the button Foreground) symbol instead of a colored emoji. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (line ~2972–2973, the `hailMary` button's `FontFamily` + `Content`). - **Fixed graphics memory table not populating + updated VRAM pressure dialog wording** (10/24): (1) Fixed the graphics memory table not populating by replacing the converter-based filtering with two separate observable collections (`VramProcessRowsVram` and `VramProcessRowsRam`) that are populated in `ApplyVramStatusResult`. The root cause was that the `GpuKind` property was being dropped in the `CollectGpuProcessUses` method when projecting the results, so all processes had `GpuKind.Unknown` and didn't match either filter. Fixed by setting `GpuKind: GpuKind.DiscreteVram` in the `CollectGpuProcessUses` method. (2) Updated the VRAM pressure dialog wording from "the loaded model profile's Context is too small" to "the loaded model profile's context is about to spill from VRAM to RAM. This will reduce performance. If your endpoint offers manual compaction (e.g. Cline's Compact button), you can try that, but be aware it may be aggressive (Cline typically cuts context down to ~20%). The Hail Mary option is a more targeted last resort that keeps your most recent turns. Or switch to a model with larger VRAM-based context, or switch to a cloud model." **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (added VramProcessRowsVram, VramProcessRowsRam, updated ApplyVramStatusResult), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (bound ItemsControls to new collections), `Services/LocalReloadRouting.cs` (updated dialog wording), `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (fixed GpuKind in CollectGpuProcessUses). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed TTFT graph headroom and bar value clearing** (10/24):
  - **TTFT graph headroom:** The TTFT (log-scale) graph was not making enough space for bar value labels above the bars. The headroom formula in `GetEffectiveYAxisRange` was linear, but the TTFT graph uses log scale. Fixed by computing the headroom in log space for log-scale graphs: `log(yMax) = log(yMin) + (log(dataMax) - log(yMin)) / (1 - headroom)`. This ensures the tallest bar sits at the correct screen position, leaving enough room for the label. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (updated `GetEffectiveYAxisRange` to use log-space headroom for log-scale graphs), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (removed the 2x headroom hack for log-scale graphs).
  - **Bar value clearing:** When switching to stress test mode, the bar value labels were not cleared immediately. The labels' `IsVisible` was controlled by the code-behind, which only updated on a timer tick (up to 50ms delay). Fixed by adding a `StringToVisibilityConverter` that converts empty strings to `false` (Collapsed) and non-empty strings to `true` (Visible), and binding the labels' `IsVisible` to the `BarValueText1-6` properties using this converter. This ensures the labels are hidden immediately when the text is cleared. **Changes:** `ViewModels/StringToVisibilityConverter.cs` (new converter), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (added converter to resources and bound `IsVisible` for all 12 bar value labels), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (removed `IsVisible` assignments from `PositionBarValueLabelSet`).
  - **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Updated X-axis label to "Chat Turn"** (10/24): Changed the telemetry mode X-axis label from "Turn" to "Chat Turn" for both the TTFT and Throughput graphs. This makes it clearer that the X-axis represents chat turns (user/assistant exchanges), not some other kind of "turn." **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (updated `SetSeries` calls to use "Chat Turn" instead of "Turn"). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed scroll flicker** (10/24): The bars had a visible "flicker" or "pause" just as they were about to scroll. **Root cause:** The smoothstep easing (`t*t*(3-2t)`) started and ended very slowly (derivative=0 at t=0 and t=1), which caused a visible stall at the start of the scroll cycle. **Fix:** Changed the easing to linear (`offset = t`), which moves at a constant speed from start to finish. This eliminates the "flicker" and produces a smoother, more predictable glide. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (changed `AdvanceScroll` easing from smoothstep to linear). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

(2) Fixed remnant bar value text lingering when switching between stress test and telemetry modes. **Root cause:** `SetSeries` and `Clear` did not clear the `BarValueText1-6`, `BarValueX1-6`, `BarValueY1-6` properties, so the code-behind still positioned the old labels. **Fix:** Added clearing of all bar value label properties to both `SetSeries` and `Clear`. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (changed default `DiagnosticsRowHeight` from `1.8*` to `1.5*`), `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (added bar value label clearing to `SetSeries` and `Clear`). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Doubled default diagnostics panel height** (10/24): The diagnostics panel now defaults to `1.8*` (roughly double the old `0.45*` default), so the stress test button no longer needs to trigger a resize. The panel is always large enough to show the live telemetry graph with room for the bars and axes. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (changed default `DiagnosticsRowHeight` from `0.45*` to `1.8*`, removed the stress test resize logic in `OnStressTestRunningChanged`, removed the 8-second post-test expansion/collapse), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (removed the `ResetDiagnosticsPanelHeight` call). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed Y-axis labels + scroll lag + Y-axis baseline + label headroom** (10/24): Four issues: (1) Y-axis scale numbers showed wrong values (e.g. "0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1" instead of the actual data range), causing bar values to appear misaligned with the axis. **Root cause:** `UpdateYAxisDataRangeLabels()` used `_axisMaxY`, which is not updated in scroll mode (only set in `SetAxisRange` for the stress test). **Fix:** Use `_maxY` in scroll mode instead of `_axisMaxY`. (2) Bar value labels and X-axis labels lagged the bars during the scroll animation. **Root cause:** `DriveScrollAnimation()` was called AFTER the label positioning, so labels were positioned based on the old scroll offset. **Fix:** Reordered the timer tick to call `DriveScrollAnimation()` FIRST, then position the labels. (3) Added Y-axis minimum offset so the Y-axis starts at a non-zero baseline (180 for tok/s, 200 for TTFT) to make small variations more visible. **Turn-1 conflict fix:** At turn 1, the data max is often below the offset (e.g. 128 < 180), causing a negative range and mismatched labels/bars. **Fix:** Created `GetEffectiveYAxisRange()` which computes the effective [yMin, yMax] range (falling back to yMin=0 if the offset >= yMax) and is used by BOTH the label computation and the bar positioning (linear + log-scale), so they always agree. (4) **Label headroom:** When a bar's value label would be forced to overlap the top of the chart (or the bar itself), the Y-axis now rescales to ensure headroom is available. **Fix:** Added `_labelHeadroomFraction` + `SetLabelHeadroomFraction()` to the ViewModel. The code-behind computes the headroom from the label height and chart height (2x for log-scale graphs to account for log compression) and sets it on the graph. `GetEffectiveYAxisRange()` uses this headroom to compute a larger Y-axis max (rounded to a multiple of 10) so the tallest bar leaves room for its label. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (new `_yAxisMinOffset` + `_labelHeadroomFraction` fields + `SetYAxisMinOffset()` + `SetLabelHeadroomFraction()` + `GetEffectiveYAxisRange()` + updated `UpdateYAxisDataRangeLabels()` + updated bar Y calculation for linear and log-scale), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (call `SetYAxisMinOffset(200)` for TTFT and `SetYAxisMinOffset(180)` for tok/s), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (reordered timer tick + compute/set label headroom in `PositionBarValueLabelSet`). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed stress test residual text + panel collapse** (10/24): Two issues: (1) When switching back from stress test mode to normal telemetry mode, residual bar value labels and axis text from the stress test remained visible. **Root cause:** `Deactivate()` only sets `IsActive=false` but does NOT clear the points, so `HasPoints` stays `true` and `IsPlotVisible` stays `true`. **Fix:** Changed `Deactivate()` to `Clear()` in the stress test end logic (`MainViewModel.cs` line 9413-9416), which wipes all points and labels. (2) When clicking "Run Stress Test," the diagnostics panel collapsed instead of expanding. **Root cause:** The GridSplitter at Row 2 can override the RowDefinition's Height with a pixel value, breaking the `DiagnosticsRowHeight` binding. **Fix:** Added `ResetDiagnosticsPanelHeight()` in `MainWindow.axaml.cs` which clears the pixel override and re-applies the star-based height. Called from `OnVmPropertyChanged` when `StressTestRunning` changes to `true`. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (Deactivate→Clear), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (new `ResetDiagnosticsPanelHeight` + property change handler). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

The button was anchored `Bottom | Right`, so it sat at the far right. **Fix:** (1) Docked the content panel to `Top` so it stops above the button panel and never overlaps the detail text. (2) Centered the button horizontally by repositioning it in the button panel's `Layout` event. **Changes:** `FluxMux.Updates/UpdaterForm.cs` (content panel `Dock.Top`, button centering via `Layout` event). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **DeepSeek Harness update: fixed broken alpha install** (10/24): The "Update now" button installed `0.2.1-alpha.5` (the `alpha` npm dist-tag) instead of `0.2.1-rc.2` (the `latest` npm dist-tag). The alpha version has a bug where the `remoteAccess` service is not properly wired into the web client plugin tree, causing all 21 UI plugins to stay pending and the web boot to fail with "21 entries did not activate". **Fix:** Updated to `0.2.1-rc.2` via `npm install -g @deepseek-ai/dsh@0.2.1-rc.2`. The harness now launches and serves the web UI correctly (HTTP 200). **Root cause:** The `AppVersionComparer` was not semver-aware — it dropped the prerelease suffix, so `0.2.1-alpha.5`, `0.2.1-rc.1`, and `0.2.1-rc.2` all compared equal. The updater's "first wins on tie" logic then locked onto the first `0.2.1-*` tag in the GitHub releases list (which was `alpha.5` at the time). **Code fix:** Made `AppVersionComparer` semver-aware: it now compares the numeric triple, then ranks prerelease identifiers (alpha < beta < rc < stable), then compares the numeric suffix. This ensures `0.2.1-rc.2` beats `0.2.1-alpha.5`. **Also fixed:** The "not installed" case now targets the newest GitHub release explicitly and prints an accurate message ("DeepSeek Harness is not installed; will install the latest release X") instead of the misleading "no newer version than the installed one". **Tests:** Added 8 new tests covering prerelease ordering, stable-vs-prerelease, and the exact GitHub release selection scenario. All pass. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Launch timing: median-based warmup budget + countdown estimate** (10/23):
  1. **Warmup budget uses median, not slowest**: The `GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs` method now uses the **median** launch time as the base for the warmup budget, not the slowest. The slowest is an outlier (e.g., a one-time system hiccup, a cold load after a reboot, or a launch the user waited on before aborting) that inflates the budget for every future launch. The median tracks the typical launch time (~13-20s for this model), so the budget is generous enough for a normal cold load but not stuck at a historical worst-case value. The headroom is 60s (not 30s) to account for cold loads that are slower than the median. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (`GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs`).
  2. **Countdown estimate uses median, not last**: The `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` method now uses the **median** launch time for the countdown estimate, not the last launch time. The last launch time (last_ms) can be an outlier. The median tracks the typical launch time, so the "estimated time left" is realistic and not stuck at a historical worst-case value. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (`GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs`).
  - **Root cause:** The user reported that "still loading" is taking far longer than it used to. The launch telemetry showed `slowest_ms=47081` (47s) for "max VRAM only context 2", but the typical launch is ~13-20s. The 47s was an outlier (a launch the user waited on before aborting). The warmup budget was `slowest_ms + 30000 = 77s`, so the model would stay in "still loading" for up to 77s before the readiness monitor took over. The fix uses the median (~13s) + 60s headroom = ~73s budget, but the countdown estimate now shows ~13s (the median) instead of 47s (the outlier).
  - **Tests:** Build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress test: zero-level guard + auto-load verification** (10/23):
  1. **Zero-level stress test guard**: Added a guard in `RunContextFillStressTest` that detects when the test completes with ZERO fill levels (the model was not ready). This is the root cause of the "graph disappears after a test" symptom: with no data points, `HasPoints=false` → `IsPlotVisible=false` → the "No telemetry" placeholder shows. The guard now logs a clear diagnostic ("WARNING: test completed with NO fill levels — the model was not ready") and sets a status message ("Stress test produced no data — the model was not ready. Check that the route indicator is green (not amber), then try again.") instead of the misleading "Stress test complete · highest fill 0". **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (zero-level guard in `RunContextFillStressTest`).
  2. **Auto-load: verified correct in current source**: The `AutoLoadSelectedProfileOnStartupAsync` method correctly uses `ResolveActiveLocalTarget()` (UI selection from `ActiveLocalModel`/`ActiveLocalVariant` config keys), NOT the last-launched profile. The "wrong auto-loaded profile" issue is from the STALE deployed build (the `autoload_diag.log` writer only exists in `artifacts/recovered/`). No code change needed — deploy the current build to verify. **No changes.**

No `MaxHeight`/`MinHeight` constraints. The "height doesn't expand" issue is likely a perception issue: the panel is already at full height, but the graph content (bars) only appears when data points are added. The zero-level stress test means no bars appear, so the panel looks empty. **No changes needed.**

- **Launch countdown telemetry: verified correct in current code** (10/22): Investigated the "countdown doesn't remember previous launch time" complaint against the prereversion. Found a deliberate design difference: prereversion mapped "Variant 1" → "(defaults)" in `CanonicalLaunchTelemetryVariant`, while current code treats "Variant 1" as a real variant (key = `model::Variant_1`). The current code includes a fallback in `GetLocalLaunchTelemetryEntry` that tries the alternate key ("Variant_1" ↔ "(defaults)") so previously-recorded telemetry under the old key format is still found. `RecordLocalLaunchTelemetry` writes `last_ms`/`slowest_ms`/`median_ms`/`fastest_ms` and `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` reads `last_ms` first. The `elapsedMs` is computed correctly as `DateTime.UtcNow - launchStartedUtc`. The key handling and recording logic are correct in the current source; the "not remembering" behavior the user sees is from the OLD deployed version predating the key-normalization fix. **No code change needed** — deploy the current build to verify. **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed**.

The `TelemetryGraphViewModel` was NOT changed (it's a generic plotting component that takes whatever X/Y values are given). **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (stress test method: graph setup, callbacks, summary). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Ghost bars: horizontal dotted lines from previous stress test run** (10/22): Implemented the ghost bars feature that shows horizontal dotted lines at the top of each previous run's bar, so the user can compare the current run to the last one. (2) In the stress test method, load the previous result before starting the test and call `SetGhostBars()` on both graphs if the previous run used the same fill levels. (3) After the test completes, save the result to the history JSON so the next run can show ghost bars. The ghost bars only show successful levels (failed levels are not plotted). Fix: restored the file from `MainViewModel.cs.reverted-backup` (22321 lines), then re-applied: (1) `Deactivate()` calls in the stress test `finally` block, (2) `ClearLocalReloadRecommend()` call in the stress test `finally` block, (3) `LocalReloadRecommendUrgency`/`AccentBrush`/`BackgroundBrush` properties, (4) fixed `SetStressTestSummary` parameter names (`maxTtftFillPercent` → `maxTtftFillTokens`, `failedFillPercent` → `failedFillTokens`). **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (restored from backup + re-applied fixes). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Dialog accent: thin line instead of 4px bar** (10/22): The severity accent on the `LocalReloadRecommend` dialog was rendering as a single thin vertical line instead of a 4px-wide bar. Root cause: the accent `Border` was inside a `Panel` (which stacks children on top of each other without sizing them), so the `Width="4"` was not being respected. Fix: replaced the `Panel` with a `Grid` (`ColumnDefinitions="4,*"`), with the accent `Border` in the first column (4px wide) and the content `StackPanel` in the second column. This ensures the accent is rendered as a proper 4px bar on the left edge. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (replaced `Panel` with `Grid`, moved accent `Border` to column 0 and content `StackPanel` to column 1). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **"No telemetry" text not showing + dialog severity background** (10/22): Two fixes. (1) **"No telemetry" text not showing:** the `IsActive` flag on `TelemetryGraphViewModel` was set to `true` by `SetSeries()` (called at the start of every stress test) but never reset to `false` afterward, so the "No telemetry" placeholder (visible only when `!IsActive`) was permanently hidden after the first stress test. Fix: added a `Deactivate()` method to `TelemetryGraphViewModel` that resets `IsActive` to `false` without clearing the data points, and called it in the stress test's `finally` block for both graphs. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (added `Deactivate()` method), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (called `TelemetryGraph.Deactivate()` and `TelemetryGraphThroughput.Deactivate()` in the stress test's `finally` block). (2) **Dialog severity background:** the `LocalReloadRecommend` dialog now uses a severity-tinted background (red/amber/blue at 20% opacity) instead of a fixed blue background, so the entire dialog is color-coded by urgency for at-a-glance recognition. The accent line and border also use the severity color. Fix: added a `LocalReloadRecommendBackgroundBrush` property to `MainViewModel` that returns a low-opacity version of the accent color, and bound the dialog's `Background` and `BorderBrush` to it. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (added `LocalReloadRecommendBackgroundBrush` property), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (bound dialog `Background` and `BorderBrush` to the new properties). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **"No telemetry" placeholder: moved outside Border to prevent clipping** (10/22): The "No telemetry" text was being masked by the black background/border of the graph panel when the panel was dragged upwards. Root cause: the text was inside the Border (which has `ClipToBounds="True"`), so when the panel was compressed, the text got clipped by the Border's bounds. Fix: moved the "No telemetry" text OUT of the Border and made it a direct child of the `GraphTerminalGrid`, spanning all columns (`Grid.ColumnSpan="3"`), positioned to overlay the entire graph panel. This prevents the text from being clipped by the Border's `ClipToBounds`. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (moved "No telemetry" TextBlock from inside the Border to a direct child of `GraphTerminalGrid` with `Grid.ColumnSpan="3"`). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **"No telemetry" placeholder: overlay instead of grid row** (10/22): The "No telemetry" text in the graph panel was still displaying when the panel was not sufficiently expanded for the text to be legible. Root cause: the text was in its own grid row (Grid.Row="1" of the inner Grid), so when the panel was collapsed to a small height, the row got compressed and the text was clipped into garbled pixels. Fix: restructured the XAML so the "No telemetry" text overlays the plot area (centered on top of it) instead of occupying its own grid row. The plot-area Grid and the "No telemetry" TextBlock are now wrapped in a `Panel` (which stacks children on top of each other). The text is hidden when the graph is active (via the existing `IsActive` binding), and when the panel is too small, the plot area is clipped so the overlay is not visible either. Removed the unused `HeightBelowThresholdConverter` that was added in an earlier attempt. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (wrapped plot-area Grid + "No telemetry" TextBlock in a `Panel`, removed `Grid.Row="1"` from the text), `ViewModels/BoolToCollapsedGridLengthConverter.cs` (removed unused `HeightBelowThresholdConverter`). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress test ghost bars: not visible after previous run with failures** (10/22): The dashed grey ghost lines (which overlay the stress test graph bars to show the previous run's results) were not appearing when the previous run had any failed levels. Root cause: the `levelsMatch` check compared the number of SUCCESSFUL levels in the previous run against the total number of fill levels in the current run. If the previous run had any failures, the counts wouldn't match and the ghost was hidden. Fix: compare the TOTAL number of levels (not just successful ones) in the match check. The ghost is still built from the successful levels only, but the match check now uses the total count. Also increased the ghost line visibility: `StrokeThickness` from 0.5 to 1.0, `StrokeDashArray` from "2,2" to "3,2", and `Opacity` from 0.7 to 0.85. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (line ~8983, `levelsMatch` check), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (lines ~3149 and ~3357, ghost Path stroke properties). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **False "chat cannot continue" after stress test: root cause + graceful escape** (10/22): Three fixes addressing the "false block with ample free context" incident, which typically surfaced right after a context-fill stress test. (1) **Fix 1 — clear stale reload-recommend state after stress test:** the stress test drives prompts up to ~110% of the context limit, which can trigger the gateway's `OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded` / `OfferSpillCompactIfNeeded` to write a `status=pending` entry to `Proxy_Local_Reload_Recommend.json`. If that stale "pending" state lingers, the next real chat turn (even a small one) can get blocked with a false "chat cannot continue" and no escape dialog. Fix: call `_runtimeService.ClearLocalReloadRecommend()` right after the gauge reset in `RunContextFillStressTestAsync`. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (line ~9277). (2) **Fix 2 — never block on the estimate when the measured count is unavailable:** when the llama-server `input_tokens` endpoint is missing/timed out/busy (e.g. right after a stress test), the gateway falls back to the chars/4 estimate, which over-counts for dense tokenizers (Qwen, ~3-4 chars/token). A prompt well under the window can read as "prompt + reply budget > window" and get hard-blocked with no recovery path. Fix: added a `blockOnEstimate` parameter to `PromptFillsLocalContext` / `EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext`. The *blocking* path (`ShouldBlockLocalForward`) passes `blockOnEstimate: false` — when the measured count is unavailable, it never hard-blocks on the estimate. A genuine overflow is still caught: llama-server reports a real `exceed_context_size_error`. The *routing* path (`DecideRoute`, which uses the estimate to offer a cloud hop) keeps the default `blockOnEstimate: true`. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxGatewayRouting.cs` (added `blockOnEstimate` param, updated `ShouldBlockLocalForward` to pass `false`). (3) **Fix 4 — graceful escape: always offer "Compact and retry" when blocked for context:** when a turn is blocked for context reasons, the reload offer now always includes a "Compact and retry" option, even if no reload candidate is available. Fix: added a `blockedForContext` parameter to `LocalReloadRouting.DecideOffer` and `OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded`. When `blockedForContext` is true, `compactRecommended` is forced to true, so a dialog with a "Compact" option always appears. **Changes:** `Services/LocalReloadRouting.cs` (added `blockedForContext` param), `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (updated `OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded` signature + block-path call site). **Tests:** updated 4 existing tests to reflect the routing-vs-blocking distinction; added 1 new test (`Estimate_only_path_never_blocks_even_when_estimate_plus_budget_overflows`). Build 0 errors; full suite **1640 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: no garbled pixels when panel collapsed** (10/22): The "No telemetry" placeholder and the axis titles (YLabel/XLabel) were always visible, even when the graph was not active. When the Diagnostics panel was collapsed to its minimum height, the text got clipped into a row of pixels. Fix: the axis titles Grids now have `IsVisible="{Binding TelemetryGraph.IsActive}"` (and `TelemetryGraphThroughput.IsActive` for the throughput graph), so they're hidden when the graph is not active. The "No telemetry" placeholder was already gated on `!IsActive` but is now also hidden when the panel is too small to show it legibly. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (added `IsVisible` bindings to both axis title Grids). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress test: no spurious PORT messages + profile-aware overflow attribution** (10/22): Two fixes. (1) **Spurious PORT messages during stress test:** the gateway's `ReportPortRulesFinding` was called unconditionally when a local turn was blocked for context overflow, even for probe requests (the stress test). This fired the `PortRulesPostMortemNotice` event → `AppendDiagnosticEntry("PORT", ...)`, producing a spurious `[PORT] [AI-FluxMux] This chat turn cannot continue...` message in the diagnostic terminal during and after the stress test. Fix: gated all three `ReportPortRulesFinding` call sites behind `!isProbeRequest`. (2) **Profile-aware overflow attribution:** two of the three call sites passed `portRules.CompactEnabled` (the global setting) instead of the profile-aware `portRulesActiveForProfile` check. When Port rules are off for the profile but the global setting is on, the overflow message would misattribute the cause to Port rules. Fix: all three call sites now resolve the profile's `LocalAutoCompactMode` and gate on it (same pattern as the existing filling-block path). **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (3 call sites: line ~748, ~881, ~1299). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: Y-axis ticks project outward + numbers right-aligned + no garbled pixels** (10/22): Three fixes. (1) **Tick marks:** moved from inside the plot to the Y-axis label canvas, now projecting outward (leftward) from the plot edge. Shortened from 6px to 4px. (2) **Number alignment:** Y-axis numbers are now right-aligned against the tick marks with a 2px gap, centered on them. (3) **Garbled pixels:** the Y-axis label TextBlocks and tick Rectangles now start with `IsVisible="False"` and are only shown when the graph is active (`IsActive`), preventing default text values ("0", "0.25", etc.) from showing before the stress test starts. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (moved ticks to label canvas, shortened to 4px, added `IsVisible="False"`), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (updated `PositionYAxisLabelSet` to position ticks in the label canvas projecting outward, right-align numbers, and show/hide based on `IsActive`). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: Y-axis labels aligned + tick marks** (10/22): Two fixes. (1) **Alignment:** the Y-axis label canvases had `Margin="0,2,4,2"` which made them 4px shorter than the plot area, offsetting the labels. Removed the top/bottom margin so the canvas height matches the plot exactly. (2) **Tick marks:** added short horizontal dividing marks (6px wide, 1px tall, `#475569`) at the left edge of each plot at 0/25/50/75/100% of the height, positioned by code-behind at the same Y positions as the labels. The ticks make it easy to see which grid line each label corresponds to. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (removed Y-axis margin, added tick mark Canvases + Rectangles), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (updated `PositionYAxisLabelSet` to also position tick marks). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: Y-axis labels aligned with grid lines** (10/22): The Y-axis labels (0/25/50/75/100% of max) were positioned using a 5-row grid with `VerticalAlignment` (Top/Center/Bottom), which placed the 75% and 25% labels at 30% and 70% of the plot height instead of 25% and 75%. Fix: replaced the grid with a `Canvas` and positioned the labels by code-behind at exactly 0/25/50/75/100% of the canvas height, vertically centered on each grid line. The code-behind runs every 50ms tick (same pattern as the X-axis labels), so it handles resize/DPI changes automatically. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (two Y-axis grids → Canvases with named TextBlocks), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (new `PositionYAxisLabels` + `PositionYAxisLabelSet` methods). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: more gap between graphs + concise telemetry strings** (10/22): Two changes. (1) **Gap:** the inner grid's `RowSpacing` was increased from 4 to 12, giving more vertical space between the TTFT and throughput graph panels. (2) **Concise strings:** the summary text to the right of the graph headings was shortened to prevent it from overlapping the titles. `SetStressTestSummary` now uses compact K-suffix token labels (e.g. "4/6 · max 109s @ 73.2K · failed @ 87.9K" instead of "4/6 levels · max TTFT 109s @ 75,000 tokens · failed @ 90,000 tokens"). The axis labels in `UpdateGraphStrings` also use `FormatTokenLabel` for compactness. The graph titles were shortened ("Context → TTFT (s)" instead of "Context Size → TTFT (Time To First Token, s)"), and the TTFT tooltip was moved to the `yAxisTooltip` parameter. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (RowSpacing 4→12), `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (concise summary + axis labels), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (shorter titles), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (updated 2 tests). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test graph: final X-axis label snapped to fill-step multiple** (10/22): The final X-axis label on the stress-test graph was not a multiple of the 64K fill step (e.g. "440K" instead of "448K" for a 393K context), because the axis max was set to `lastFillLevel * 1.10` without snapping. Fix: `MainViewModel.RunContextFillStressTestAsync` now computes the fill step from the fill levels, pads the axis max by 10%, then snaps UP to the next multiple of the step (`Math.Ceiling(paddedMax / step) * step`). The final label is now always a clean multiple of the fill step and sits at the right edge of the plot. **Changes:** `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (axis max snapped to fill-step multiple). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Stress-test fill levels: fixed 64K step + labels aligned with bars + no overlap** (10/22): Fixed the broken axis-label edits from a previous session (which had replaced fill-level labels with evenly-spaced 0/20/40/60/80/100% labels, breaking bar alignment). Three changes: (1) `ComputeFillLevels` now uses a **fixed 64K step** for every model (including YaRN-extended ones) so performance is comparable at each fill level — the ghost lines from a previous run (different model) line up with the current run's bars. The last level stops at the first multiple of 64K that is >= the context limit (stopping at an exact level is fine). (2) `MainViewModel` now sets the X-axis max to `lastFillLevel * 1.10` (was `contextLimit * 1.05`) so the final bar and its label are not clipped and the last two labels have room to sit side by side without overlapping. (3) `UpdateAxisLabels` restored to the fill-level-based logic: labels are placed at the fill-level positions (absolute token values like "64K", "128K"), a final label is always added at the axis max, and the last fill-level label is dropped if it would sit < 10% from the axis max (to avoid overlap). **Changes:** `Services/ContextFillStressProbe.cs` (fixed 64K step, removed adaptive `RoundToNearest4K`), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (axis max = lastFillLevel * 1.10), `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (restored fill-level label logic), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/ContextFillStressResultTests.cs` (updated 3 tests for fixed 64K step). **Tests:** build 0 errors; full suite **1639 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed red vertical line on stress test abort + false "chat cannot continue" warning** (10/21): Two fixes. (1) **Red vertical line on abort:** `SetUnfilledRegion` had a bug where passing `null` (the abort case) did NOT clear the overlay, because the `is not { }` pattern matches non-null values, so `null is not { } last` is `false`. Fixed by explicitly checking `boundaryFillPercent is null` to clear the overlay. This ensures the red dotted line is cleared when the test is aborted. (2) **False "chat cannot continue" warning on abort:** The stress test fills the context to near 100%, and the context gauge (`Local_Context_Usage.json`) is read by the gateway to determine the context state. If the gauge still shows a high fill level after the test, the gateway blocks the next chat turn with a false warning. Fixed by resetting the context gauge to 0 immediately in the `finally` block (before the background KV cache clear), so the gateway sees a fresh context. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`SetUnfilledRegion` handles null), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (reset context gauge in `finally` block). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 38 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **X-axis labels: no overlap between last fill level and axis-max label** (10/21): Fixed the overlap between the last fill-level label and the new axis-max label when they're close together in screen space (e.g., 256K at 95.2% and 268.8K at 100% — only 4.8% apart). `UpdateAxisLabels` now checks the screen-space gap between the last fill-level label and the axis max; if it's below 10% of the axis (too small to fit a label without colliding), it drops the last fill-level label and keeps only the axis-max label. This keeps all labels at fill-level values (no interpolation) while guaranteeing no overlap. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`UpdateAxisLabels` drops last fill-level label when too close to axis max), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (updated 2 tests: drop-when-close, keep-when-far). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 38 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **X-axis labels: static pool + final label beyond context size** (10/21): Replaced the brittle imperative create/measure/position approach with the proven static-TextBlock pattern (same as the bar-value labels). (1) **Centering fix:** The X-axis label Canvases now contain a fixed pool of 16 static TextBlocks each. `PositionAxisLabelSet` (code-behind) shows/hides and positions them on each 50ms timer tick using the `XAxisLabels` collection. Because the TextBlocks are static, they're always measured before positioning, so `DesiredSize.Width` is valid and labels are correctly centered under the bars (the old code created TextBlocks and read `DesiredSize.Width` in the same pass, getting 0 → labels shifted right). (2) **Final label beyond context size:** `UpdateAxisLabels` now appends a final label at the axis max (`contextLimit * 1.05`), since the stress test can fill up to ~110% of context. Only added if the last fill-level label is meaningfully below the axis max (avoids a near-duplicate). **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (16 static TextBlocks per graph), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (`PositionAxisLabelSet` replaces `UpdateDynamicLabelSet`), `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`UpdateAxisLabels` adds final axis-max label), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (2 new tests). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 38 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed dynamic X-axis labels not showing (v3)** (10/21): Replaced the `ItemsControl` approach with direct code-behind generation of `TextBlock`s. The `UpdateDynamicLabelSet` method now: (1) removes all existing TextBlocks from the Canvas, (2) creates a new TextBlock for each label in the ObservableCollection, and (3) positions each TextBlock based on its Position property. This is more reliable than the `ItemsControl` approach because it gives full control over the TextBlock creation and positioning. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (removed ItemsControl, kept empty Canvas), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (new `UpdateDynamicLabelSet` method). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 36 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed dynamic X-axis labels not showing (v2)** (10/21): The previous fix (positioning the ItemsControl at 0,0) was not sufficient. The issue was that the code-behind was not finding the TextBlocks in the correct order. Updated `PositionDynamicLabelSet` to find the ItemsControl in the Canvas, then find all TextBlocks in the ItemsControl's visual tree. This ensures the TextBlocks are found in the correct order (matching the ObservableCollection order). **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (updated `PositionDynamicLabelSet` to find TextBlocks in the ItemsControl's visual tree). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 36 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Axis labels use first 6 fill levels** (10/21): Fixed the bug where the YaRN graph axis labels were erratically spaced and some labels were missing (e.g., 80K, 200K, 320K missing). The old code used an "evenly space the picks" logic that skipped some fill levels. - **Axis labels always at fill-level values + cover full range** (10/21): Fixed the bug where the right end of the X-axis was blank for large context models (YaRN). Two changes: (1) `ComputeFillLevels` no longer caps at 8 levels — the fill levels now cover the full context range (e.g., 1M tokens with 64K steps = 16 levels). (2) `UpdateAxisLabels` picks up to 6 labels from the fill levels, evenly spaced across the full range. This ensures the labels always reach the right end (the axis max) and are always at fill-level values (the tested capacity points), never arbitrary interpolated values. **Changes:** `Services/ContextFillStressProbe.cs` (`ComputeFillLevels` removes 8-level cap), `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`UpdateAxisLabels` picks 6 evenly-spaced labels from fill levels), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (updated to match new behavior). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 36 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed axis label gap at right end** (10/21): Fixed the bug where the last axis label didn't reach the axis max, leaving a gap at the right end. The old code checked if `current < _axisMaxX` to decide whether to add a final label at the axis max, but `current` was already past the axis max after the loop, so the condition was false. The new code checks if the LAST label's value is less than the axis max, and adds a final label at the axis max if needed. This ensures the full range is covered. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`UpdateAxisLabels` adds final label at axis max), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (updated to match new behavior). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 36 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Fixed axis label ordering for VRAM-only models** (10/21): Fixed the bug where extended axis labels were out of order (e.g., 215K at position 80.0 coming before 256K at position 95.2). The old code used the fill levels as labels and then added extended labels starting from the last fill level, which caused the labels to be out of order when the axis was extended. The new code generates labels by starting from the first fill level and continuing with the step size until reaching the axis max, ensuring the labels are evenly-spaced and in the correct order. **Changes:** `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (`UpdateAxisLabels` generates labels with correct ordering), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/TelemetryGraphViewModelTests.cs` (updated to match new behavior). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `ContextFillStress` + `TelemetryGraph` = 36 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
