- **Port-rule pauses no longer fail the chat on timeout; they halt and wait for user steering** (10/21): Applied the principle that "any prompt to address situations that otherwise cannot avoid chat failure should not be subject to timeout; timeouts should only be for situations where chats can self-manage, defaulting to the safest option." Two changes: (1) **`AskPortRulePauseAsync`** (repeated tool calls / milling / diagnostic dump): the turn now **halts and waits indefinitely** for the user's steering choice instead of failing after 45s. The banner is already showing the options (Send this turn anyway / End this turn / Let me steer / Switch to ...), so no re-prompt is needed — the turn just stays halted until the user explicitly chooses. `fail_on_timeout` is now `false`. (2) **`AskHangWaitAsync`** (llama-server hang): on timeout, now **defaults to "wait longer"** (the safe option that keeps the chat alive) instead of failing the turn. A hang is a situation the chat can self-manage (llama-server may still be producing), so the safe default is to continue waiting. The user can still explicitly choose "End this turn" or "Switch to cloud." `fail_on_timeout` is now `false`. **Rationale:** there is no benefit to bypassing the user's steer, and failing a flailing chat on a timer is worse than halting and waiting for the user's choice. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (`AskPortRulePauseAsync` now loops indefinitely; `AskHangWaitAsync` now returns `WaitStatus` on timeout instead of `ConsentTimeout`). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `RouteRecoveryPolicy` + `PortRulesPostMortem` + `PortRulesTelemetry` = 46 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Context-overflow message no longer misattributes cause to Port rules when the profile doesn't use them** (10/21): Fixed a discrepancy where the terminal/context-overflow message referenced "Port rule Headroom / Watermark %" and "Servers → Advanced: Port rules" even when the current profile's compaction mode was "per-profile" or "none" (i.e. Port rules were NOT the active compaction source). Root cause: `FluxMuxGatewayHost.HandleChatCompletionsAsync` passed the GLOBAL `portRules.CompactEnabled` to `PortRulesPostMortem.FormatContextOverflow`, so the message always referenced Port rules whenever global Port rules were enabled — regardless of the profile's `LocalAutoCompactMode`. **Fix:** resolve the profile's `LocalAutoCompactMode` (via `GetLocalAutoCompactSettings`) and compute `portRulesActiveForProfile = portRules.CompactEnabled && mode != "per-profile" && mode != "none"`. Only when the profile actually uses Port-rules compaction does the message name Port-rule settings; otherwise it states the cause plainly ("this turn was too large for the loaded Context") and points at the levers that actually apply (launch a higher-Context profile, or start a smaller chat). This matches the existing `portRulesActive` gate in `FormatContextOverflow` (which already had the correct "off" wording) — the bug was only that the caller passed the wrong flag. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (caller of `FormatContextOverflow` in the `local_context_filling` block, ~line 702). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `PortRulesPostMortem` = 20 passed, 0 failed (incl. the existing `portRulesActive: false` case). **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **YaRN context now shows in terminal launch output + port-rules terminology cleaned up** (10/21): Two display/terminology fixes. (1) **Terminal launch output now shows the context limit** (item 6): the launch `readyDetails` string in `FluxMuxRuntimeService.LaunchLocalAsync` (~line 3684) previously showed offload/vision/model but not the context window. It now appends `context {N}` — and when YaRN extension is active (VRAM-resident context < total), `context {N} (YaRN, {M} on VRAM)` so the YaRN-extended limit is visible in the terminal. Uses `ManagedLocalContext` (the YaRN-extended `OverrideContext`) with a fallback to `GetLocalProfileContextLimit`. (2) **Port-rules compact terminology** (item 4): the user-facing compact-mode combo option was renamed from `"Port rules default"` to `"Use Port rules"` (internal value `"port-rules"` unchanged, so config/back-compat unaffected), and the combo tooltip now explains the three options the user actually selects — "Use Port rules" (applies the shared settings from Servers - Advanced: Port rules), "Profile-specific" (per-profile settings below), and "None / endpoint managed" (FluxMux compaction off) — and states that compaction only happens according to the selected option. **Important:** the underlying behavior was already correct — `TryApplyForwardCompact` gates on the profile's `LocalAutoCompactMode` so compaction only occurs per the selected option (`none` → no compaction; `per-profile` → per-profile settings; `port-rules` → shared Port-rules settings). This change is purely cosmetic (label + tooltip) to remove the misleading "global defaults" framing; "global" is not one of the three options the user picks. **Note:** the model-statistics footprint text (item 7) and variant-creation note already reflect YaRN (`native window N (YaRN extends to M)`), so no change was needed there. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (launch `readyDetails`), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (`LocalAutoCompactModeOptions` display), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (combo tooltip). **Tests:** build 0 errors; `AutoCompactModeNone` + `PortRulesTelemetry` + `PortRulesPostMortem` + `LocalHistoryCompaction` = 39 passed, 0 failed. **Deploy state: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).

- **Auto-load root-cause fix: write/read now use the SAME file** (10/20): The earlier auto-load fix was deployed but had NO effect ("no change to auto load on startup bug"). Root cause found by inspecting the live config + running binary: `RecordLastLaunchedLocal` (write) stored the record in the **main** `fluxmux_config.json` under `FluxMuxMeta.LastLaunchedLocal`, but `GetLastLaunchedLocal` (read) looked in a **separate** file `last_launched_local.json` (chosen deliberately so `FluxMuxConfigService.Save()` — which rewrites the whole main config from its in-memory copy — can't clobber it). The write side was never updated to match, so: (a) the main-config record was clobbered on the next `Save()`, and (b) the separate file was never created. Net effect: `GetLastLaunchedLocal()` always returned empty → auto-load always fell back to the UI selection (the old buggy behavior). **Evidence:** running `bin\` DLL *does* contain `RecordLastLaunchedLocal` (fix was deployed), yet `FluxMuxMeta.LastLaunchedLocal` was `null` in the live config and `last_launched_local.json` did not exist anywhere. **Fix:** `RecordLastLaunchedLocal` now writes the `{ model, variant, utc }` JSON to `LastLaunchedLocalPath` (`last_launched_local.json`) — the exact file `GetLastLaunchedLocal` reads — instead of the main config. No more main-config write, no more `_jsonCache` update, no more clobbering. **Changes:** `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (`RecordLastLaunchedLocal` rewritten; doc comment updated). **Tests:** build verified in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors). No unit test added — the `FluxMuxRuntimeService` constructor is heavy (creates the gateway host, stops stale processes), so a full-service round-trip test is impractical; verified by build + code review that write and read now share the same path and JSON shape. **Deploy state: DEPLOYED & CONFIRMED WORKING (10/20)** (was: compiled/verified in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed).

- **Stress test pops the Diagnostics panel open (~2x default)** (10/20): When the user clicks "Stress test", the Diagnostics panel now expands to roughly double its default height so the log output and results are easy to follow. Implemented with a proportional star size (no hardcoded pixels, DPI-safe): the main layout Grid (`MainLayoutGrid`, `MainWindow.axaml` ~line 998) has its Diagnostics row (Row 3, default `0.55*`) set to `2.4*` on click via the `OnStressTestButtonClick` handler (`MainWindow.axaml.cs`). `2.4*` makes the panel ~71% of the space below the header vs ~35% by default (~2x pixel height). The panel is intentionally NOT auto-collapsed when the test ends (results stay visible); the user can drag the sash back to the default at any time. **Changes:** `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (named `MainLayoutGrid`, added `Click` on the Stress test button), `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs` (`OnStressTestButtonClick`, `using Avalonia.Layout`). **Tests:** build succeeded; full suite 1634 passed, 3 pre-existing `ContextFillStressResultTests.LimitBehavior_*` failures (unrelated). **Deploy state: compiled in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Auto-load now targets the most-recently-LAUNCHED profile** (10/20): Fixed the long-standing "auto-load selects the wrong profile" bug. Root cause: auto-load read `SelectedLocalVariant` (the UI selection) instead of the profile actually in use, and the write-on-close record was unreliable because the app doesn't always exit gracefully. **Fix:** record the last-launched profile at LAUNCH time (not close time). (1) `FluxMuxRuntimeService.RecordLastLaunchedLocal(model, variant)` writes `FluxMuxMeta.LastLaunchedLocal = { model, variant, utc }` to the config on the launch-success path. (2) `FluxMuxRuntimeService.GetLastLaunchedLocal()` reads it back. (3) `MainViewModel.AutoLoadSelectedProfileOnStartupAsync` now targets the last-launched `(model, variant)`, falling back to the UI selection only when no launch has been recorded. **CRITICAL SECOND-ORDER BUG (found 10/20 via `autoload_diag.log`):** the first version let auto-load's OWN launch record the (fallback) profile as "last launched," contaminating the record — so after the first restart, auto-load kept picking the wrong profile forever. **Fix:** threaded a `recordLastLaunched` parameter through `LaunchLocalAsync` → `RunLocalRouteAsync` → `LaunchLocalRouteAsync`; auto-load passes `recordLastLaunched: false` so it restores state without overwriting the record. Only explicit user launches update the record. **RE-SEED REQUIRED:** the current config record is already contaminated by the previously-deployed (ungated) code — after deploying this fix, the user must manually launch the correct profile once (via a Quick Select button) to re-seed the record, then restart. `autoload_diag.log` records `source=last-launched|ui-selection` and `lastLaunched=(...)` for auditing. **Tests:** build verified 10/20 in `artifacts\testrun\` (0 errors, 60 pre-existing warnings); changes reviewed manually. A `build_testrun.ps1` was added at the project root to run the separate-output build. **Deploy state: compiled/verified, NOT deployed.**
- **Stress-test context clear: reduced retry loop** (10/20): After aborting a stress test, the "Clear context" step kept retrying (10 attempts, 5s→15s backoff) even when the context was already effectively cleared. Root cause: the gateway is stateless, so the "context" that needs clearing is llama-server's KV cache. A tiny chat-completion POST tells llama-server to process a fresh (non-prefixed) prompt, which effectively starts a new KV cache entry. The old stress-test entries remain in memory but are not reused (different prefix). If the clear fails (e.g. llama-server still busy), the next real request will still work — the gateway does not hold conversation state, and llama-server's prefix cache is keyed by prompt content. **Fix:** reduced `maxAttempts` from 10 to 3, initial `settleMs` from 5000 to 2000, backoff cap from 15000 to 5000. Final failure message now says "context clear failed (best-effort); the next chat will start with a fresh context" instead of "restart the model or start a new chat to reset". **Changes:** `MainViewModel.cs` (`ClearLocalModelContextAfterStressTestAsync`, ~line 9403). **Tests:** 3 pre-existing failures in `ContextFillStressResultTests` (unrelated to this change — `LimitBehavior` string formatting in `ContextFillStressProbe.cs`). **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test fixes: red line position, ghost zigzag, bar overlap, countdown** (10/19): Four fixes. (1) **Red unfilled-region line:** was positioned at x≈0 because `FillPercent` (0-100) was passed where tokens were expected. Now uses `PromptTokens` (actual token value). No line shown when test is aborted. (2) **Ghost zigzag:** previous-run ghost lines were bunched at the left edge when fill levels differed (e.g. old 4K-40K vs new 64K-256K). Now only shows ghost when the previous run's levels match the current fill levels (within 25% tolerance of the first level). (3) **Bar overlap:** `BuildBarPath` now computes bar width from the minimum gap between adjacent bars (70% of gap, capped at 14%, floor 4%) instead of a fixed 14%. (4) **Countdown:** `CountdownMs` uses a window-aware strategy: while the 8-sample window is still filling up, it keeps the persisted historical slowest if higher (the window isn't trusted yet); once the window is full, it trusts the window's slowest so a cold outlier pushed out of the window naturally lowers the estimate. This prevents the countdown from being stuck at a historical worst-case value forever. **Tests:** full suite **1637 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Dynamic stress-test fill levels + ghost line tick marks + bar value labels** (10/19): Three changes. (1) **Dynamic fill levels:** the stress test now computes step sizes from the profile's VRAM capacity (targeting ~4 steps to cover it, rounded to the nearest 4K boundary). A VRAM-only 262K profile gets 4×64K steps; a YaRN 393K profile with 262K VRAM gets 6×64K steps. The X-axis labels now match the actual bar positions. (2) **Ghost tick marks:** previous-run ghost bars are now short horizontal dashed lines (6% wide) at each level's height, rendered in front of the live bars. (3) **Bar value labels:** each bar now shows its value (e.g. "42.3 s" for TTFT, "245" for tok/s) at the top, positioned via Canvas + code-behind on every pulse-timer tick. `ContextFillStressProbe.ComputeFillLevels(contextLimit, vramContext)` is the new entry point; `TelemetryGraphViewModel.SetFillLevels()` stores the levels for axis label positioning; `BuildGhostLines()` replaces `BuildBarPath()` for ghost rendering; `BarValueText1-6` / `BarValueY1-6` expose the label text and positions. **Tests:** full suite **1635 passed, 0 failed** (4 new tests for `ComputeFillLevels`). **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Context NumericUpDown max bound fix** (10/19): The context size NumericUpDown in the Model Profiles tab had a hardcoded `Maximum="262144"` (256K), preventing users from setting `OverrideContext` above 256K even when YaRN extension is enabled. Now binds to `LocalVariantContextMax` which returns 1M when `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` is true. This was the root cause of the "393K vs 262K" context mismatch: the UI couldn't set the context above 256K, so the gateway's `local_context` (which mirrors `OverrideContext`) was capped at 262K while the user expected 393K. **Tests:** full suite **1631 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Token-based X-axis + ghost bar restyle + context gauge fix + countdown fix + gauge label + running profile highlight** (10/19): Six changes. (1) **Token-based X-axis:** the stress test now uses fixed absolute token targets (4K/8K/16K/24K/32K/40K) instead of percentage-based fill levels. (2) **Ghost bar restyle:** thin dashed outlines rendered in front of live bars. (3) **Context gauge fix:** shows "0 / N tokens" after launch instead of "Waiting for a turn…". (4) **Countdown estimate fix:** `CountdownMs` now returns the slowest of the recent window instead of the median. (5) **Gauge label + tooltip:** title changed to "Context (actual)" with tooltip explaining ground-truth tokenizer accuracy. (6) **Running profile highlight:** the currently running profile (launched from Quick Select) is now highlighted in the Model Profiles tab with a green "●" dot. `CloudVariantTreeItemViewModel` has a new `IsRunning` property, set by `RefreshRunningProfileHighlight()` which is called from `RememberMountedLocal`, `RememberMountedCloud`, and `ClearMountedRoute`. **Tests:** full suite **1631 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **X-axis labels centered under bars** (10/19): The 5 evenly-spaced X-axis labels were replaced with 6 labels (one per fill level 25/50/75/90/100/110), each centered under its corresponding bar. The view model now exposes `XAxisLabel1-6` (the label texts) and `XAxisPos1-6` (the screen-x positions, 0-100, computed from the axis range). The XAML uses a `Canvas` for the label row, and the code-behind positions each label at `Canvas.Left = (pos/100) * canvasWidth - labelWidth/2` on every pulse-timer tick (50ms), so it handles resize/DPI changes automatically. **Tests:** full suite **1631 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Auto-load persistence fix + stress test accuracy improvements** (10/19): Four fixes. (1) **Auto-load persistence:** `SaveCurrentSelections()` now captures `ActiveLocalModel`/`ActiveLocalVariant` *before* calling `SyncPrimaryRouteSlotsFromCurrentSelections()`, which was clobbering the primary selection with the first quick-select slot's variant. The exit-persist chain (`SyncSelectionToRunningLocalProfile` → `SaveCurrentSelections`) now correctly persists the last-used profile. (2) **Throughput sample size:** stress test `max_tokens` increased from 32 to 128, and the prompt changed from "Summarize in one short sentence" to "Explain in 3-4 clear sentences" — the old 32-token sample was too small to measure the model's true generation speed (warmup tokens dominated, and the short prompt elicited <32 tokens). (3) **Context clear between levels:** the model's context is now cleared before each stress-test level (via `ClearLocalModelContextBeforeStressLevelAsync`), so the fill % corresponds to actual context usage instead of accumulating from the previous level. (4) **Background use warning:** the diagnostic now warns "avoid background chat during the test" since gateway requests can contaminate results. **Tests:** full suite **1631 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress-test graph fixes + context gauge reconciliation** (10/19): Six fixes landed in one batch. (1) **X-axis tick labels** now show the real test levels (25/50/75/90/110% fill) instead of the padded axis range (18/117). (2) **Context warning suppression** during the stress test: the `local_context_filling` event is now gated by `!isProbeRequest`, so the spurious warning no longer appears in the telemetry/UI during the test (the 400 response is still returned so the test observes the real failure). (3) **Stress-test button + dropdown** are now wrapped in a single `Border` so they read as one linked control (no change to bottom-bar depth). (4) **Ghost outline** of the previous stress-test run: the last completed result is persisted per model+variant to `context_fill_history_{key}.json`; on the next run, a dimmed outline of the previous curve is shown behind the live bars for performance comparison between profiles. (5) **110% bar crop fix:** the right axis pad was increased from 7 to 8 units (18→118) so the 110% bar's full 14-unit width fits without its right edge being clamped. (6) **Context gauge reconciliation:** the gauge is now seeded proactively at model launch (`SeedContextGaugeAtLaunch`) so it shows the correct context limit from the moment of first launch (especially important when launching mid-chat), instead of waiting for the next gateway turn. **Tests:** full suite **1631 passed, 0 failed** (including 3 new ghost-bar tests). **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Launch countdown now uses per-profile recorded launch times** (10/19): The countdown always defaulted to 200s (or showed no estimate) because `CanonicalLaunchTelemetryVariant` mapped `"Variant 1"` → `"(defaults)"`, so the read key (`model::(defaults)`) never matched the write key (`model::Variant 1`). **Fix:** (1) removed the `"Variant 1"` → `"(defaults)"` mapping in `CanonicalLaunchTelemetryVariant` — only true base-profile display names map to `"(defaults)"`; (2) added a fallback in `GetLocalLaunchTelemetryEntry` that tries the alternate key format (e.g. if the exact key ends with `::(defaults)`, also try `::Variant_1` and vice versa) so pre-existing telemetry recorded under the old key is still found. The countdown now follows the median of the recent launch window per profile (e.g. ~13s for "max VRAM only context 2", ~18s for "YaRN test with vision"). **Tests:** full suite **1628 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test graph: stable 300 Y-axis, live tok/s throughput bar, pulsing in-progress bars, consistent failure treatment** (10/19): (1) **Y-axis** — both graphs now use a stable 300 ceiling (TTFT=300s, throughput=300 tok/s) based on the last run's data (TTFT up to ~234s, throughput up to ~296 tok/s), so the axis doesn't resize. (2) **Throughput in-progress bar** — no longer a fake "building up" bar. It now reflects the LIVE tok/s from the gateway's `GenerationSpeedTracker` (auto-enabled for the duration of the stress test, restored afterward) once the first token of each level arrives; before that it's a small pulsing placeholder. This is honest: tok/s is only knowable once tokens are being generated. (3) **TTFT in-progress bar** — grows with elapsed prefill time (genuinely "TTFT so far" as a fraction of the 300s Y-max), since that IS knowable on the fly. (4) **Pulsing** — a `DispatcherTimer` in `MainWindow.axaml.cs` oscillates the opacity of both in-progress bars (0.15–0.45) to indicate "in progress" (Avalonia 12 has no `DoubleAnimation` XAML type, so a timer is used). (5) **Failure/timeout consistency** — the hatch boundary now moves to the FIRST FAILED level (not the last successful), so the dotted line marks where the test stopped without overlapping a data bar; the misleading red "top bar" was removed from the TTFT graph (a timeout isn't a huge TTFT); both graphs now show the same dotted-line + hatch treatment. **Changes:** `MainViewModel.cs` (Y-axis 300, auto-enable/restore generation-speed telemetry, heartbeat reads live tok/s, TTFT bar grows with elapsed time, failure bar removed, hatch boundary = first failed level, exit-persist diagnostics), `MainWindow.axaml` (pulsing bar `x:Name`s), `MainWindow.axaml.cs` (pulse timer). **Tests:** full suite **1628 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Auto-load: exit-persist diagnostics added** (10/19): The user reports the last-used profile (the one in use when the app closes) is not auto-starting on next launch. The `autoload_diag.log` shows the *targeting* is now correct (10/19 entries target the right profile), so the issue is likely in the exit-persist (why the in-use-at-close profile isn't captured). **Added** file-based diagnostics to `PersistSettingsForExit()` that log to `C:\AI_Workbench\Logs\exit_persist_diag.log`: the running profile (`GetManagedLocalIdentity`), `SelectedLocalVariant` before/after `SyncSelectionToRunningLocalProfile`, the `ActiveLocalVariant` actually written, and `IsManagedLocalAlive`. **Next step:** after deploying, close the app with a known in-use profile and check the log to see whether the running profile is being captured or clobbered. **Tests:** full suite **1628 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed**.
- **Reasoning protocol added to AGENTS.md** (10/19): When reasoning about a non-trivial problem, lay out (a) what's established and (b) the unresolved dilemmas, then ask the user to decide — do not cycle/re-derive the same points indefinitely.
- **Stress test graph: accurate titles, progressive bars, wider bars, dotted-line + hatched untested region** (10/19): Multiple issues fixed: (1) **Corrupted headings** — the TTFT graph title was "Context-fill stress test" while the summary started with "Context Fill % → TTFT (Time To First Token, s)", showing two competing titles. Fix: title is now "Context Fill % → TTFT (Time To First Token, s)" (accurate, self-describing) and the summary contains ONLY progress/results ("4/6 levels · max TTFT 109s @ 75% fill · failed @ 90% fill"). Throughput graph title is "Context Fill % → Throughput (tok/s)". (2) **No immediate visual activity** — bars only appeared on level completion; during the long prefill wait only a thin pulsing band showed. Fix: new progressive testing bar that grows from the baseline as prefill progress is reported (exponential ramp: ~63% at 20s, ~95% at 60s), dimmer (opacity 0.45) than finalized data bars to make clear no data has been obtained yet. New `UpdateTestingBarProgress()` method drives the bar height. (3) **First bar half-width** — the X-axis range was padded by 7 units on each side (25→18, 110→117) so the first and last bars are not clipped at the plot edges. (4) **Bars too narrow** — bar width increased from 8 to 14 units (14% of plot width), giving ~0.3-unit gaps between the 6 bars (almost touching). (5) **Misleading full-height background bar** — the old full-height "testing band" rectangle was replaced by the progressive bar. (6) **Misleading red solid fill** — the untested region now uses a dotted vertical line (`StrokeDashArray="4,4"`) at the boundary + a hatched fill (`DrawingBrush` with diagonal lines, opacity 0.30) extending to the axis max, instead of a solid red rectangle. New `UnfilledRegionLineGeometry` property + `UnfilledRegionLineX` for the dotted line. **Changes:** `TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` (progressive bar, wider bars, dotted line + hatch, accurate summary), `MainViewModel.cs` (new titles, padded axis ranges, progressive bar progress in heartbeat, `SetUnfilledRegion` on both graphs), `MainWindow.axaml` (dotted line + hatch XAML, progressive bar binding). **Tests:** updated `SetStressTestSummary` test (no axis description in summary), updated `SetUnfilledRegion` tests (check `UnfilledRegionLineX`), new `TestingBar_grows_with_progress` test. - **Stress test graph: bar chart replaces distorted line** (10/19): The graph plot lines distorted in thickness because the data was rendered as a stroked `Path` inside a `<Viewbox Stretch="Fill">` with a 100×100 geometry. The plot area is non-square (~300×180px), so `Stretch="Fill"` scaled X and Y by different factors (~3× vs ~1.8×), turning a 2px stroke into ~6px wide but ~3.6px tall. **Fix:** replaced the stroked polyline with a **bar chart** — each fill level is now a filled rectangle (bar) centered on its X position, growing upward from the baseline as each pass completes. Bars have no stroke, so they scale uniformly at any panel size. **Changes:** `TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` — added `BarGeometry`/`FailureBarGeometry` (Geometry) + `BarPathData`/`FailureBarPathData` (string, for testability) properties; `BuildBarPath()` builds the path string (8-unit-wide bars, clamped to plot bounds, 0.5-unit minimum height); `TryParseGeometry()` wraps `Geometry.Parse` in a try/catch so it returns null in the test environment where Avalonia's `IPlatformRenderInterface` is unavailable. `MainWindow.axaml` — both graph panels (TTFT + throughput) now bind `BarGeometry`/`FailureBarGeometry` with `Fill` instead of `LineGeometry`/`FailureLineGeometry` with `Stroke`. **Tests:** 4 new tests in `TelemetryGraphViewModelTests` (bar path has one rectangle per point, empty when no points, failure bar at top, grows with more points). Full suite **1627 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Auto-compaction during stress test: confirmed already fixed** (10/19): The gateway checks `isProbeRequest` (set via the `X-FluxMux-Probe-Request` header) and skips forward-compaction for probe/stress-test requests (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:518`). The stress probe sets this header in `BuildChatRequest` (`ContextFillStressProbe.cs:559-561`). Auto-compaction will NOT kick in during a stress test. No changes needed.
- **Stress test context recovery: improved retry** (10/19): The context clear after a stress test (`ClearLocalModelContextAfterStressTestAsync`) sends a tiny chat request to the llama-server daemon port. After an abort, the in-flight large request is still being torn down; the clear races with it. **Improvement:** increased initial settle delay from 1.5s to 3s, max attempts from 4 to 6, and added a diagnostic log entry confirming the context was cleared. The clear is best-effort: if all attempts fail, the user can start a new chat to reset. **Note:** a more robust fix would use llama-server's native context-reset endpoint if available, but the current approach (retry with backoff) is the safest given the server's API surface. **Tests:** full suite **1627 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Auto-load no longer targets a non-Quick-Select profile** (10/18): The "Load selected profile on startup" auto-load was launching `(defaults)` — a profile NOT in Quick Select — instead of the primary slot's real variant. **Root cause (confirmed via `C:\AI_Workbench\Logs\autoload_diag.log`):** in `LoadFromConfig()`, `EnsureRouteSlotConfigInitialized()` → `ApplySelectionsFromRouteSlots()` correctly sets `SelectedSelection1LocalVariant` to the primary slot's variant (e.g. "max VRAM only context 2"), but the later `ApplyLocalTemplateDefaultsForSelection1()` then **unconditionally** reset `SelectedSelection1LocalVariant = BaseVariantDisplayName` (`(defaults)`), clobbering it. `ResolveActiveLocalTarget()` then targeted `(defaults)`. **Fix:** made the variant assignment conditional — only set to `(defaults)` when `SelectedSelection1LocalVariant` is empty/whitespace, preserving the value loaded from the route slots (mirrors the existing `FirstNonEmpty` pattern used for the profile on the line above). **Build:** succeeds (0 errors). **Deploy:** close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. **Verify:** enable "Load selected profile on startup", select a local Quick Select profile, restart, and confirm the auto-load targets that profile (check `autoload_diag.log` — `variant=` should match the slot, not `(defaults)`).
- **Diagnostics panel: restored height so the bottom bar is not clipped** (10/18): The diagnostics panel (main-grid Row 3) had been shrunk to `0.35*`/`MinHeight="120"` while its content grew (title row + 5 utilization gauges in the header, plus a graph/terminal split and a much longer bottom button bar). The header alone is ~120-150px, so at the 120px floor the body got almost no room and the bottom bar (GPU & VRAM, stress-test, idle-timeout, Health, Copy log, Clear, UI scale/theme — a `WrapPanel` that wraps to 2+ lines) was partly obscured. **Fix:** increased Row 3 to `0.55*`/`MinHeight="220"` so the header + graph/terminal + bottom bar all fit. **Build:** succeeds (0 errors). **Deploy:** close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. **Verify:** the diagnostics panel opens with the full bottom bar visible (not clipped); test at multiple window heights and DPI scales.

- **Stress test graph: fixed axes, clear heading, "% fill" labels, TTFT tooltip, red hatch on untested region** (10/17): The graph panel's heading was cryptic ("25–90 Fill · last: 90 Fill → 0.0 s") and the axis labels showed interpolated data-range values instead of the real fill levels. **Changes:** (1) added `SetAxisRange(xMin, xMax, yMin, yMax)` to `TelemetryGraphViewModel` — X normalizes against [0,110], Y against [0, axisMaxY] (grows, never shrinks, from successful points); (2) `UpdateAxisLabels` now shows the real fill levels (25/50/75/90/100/110) mapped into the 5 slots, each suffixed "% fill" (e.g. "25% fill", "110% fill"); Y-axis shows 0/25/50/75/100% of max TTFT in seconds; (3) new `SetStressTestSummary(completed, total, maxTtft, maxTtftFill, failedFill, aborted)` produces a clear heading: "Context Fill % → TTFT (Time To First Token, s) · 4/6 levels · max TTFT 109s @ 75% fill · failed @ 90% fill"; (4) new `YAxisTooltip` property ("TTFT = Time To First Token (latency from prompt to first token)") bound via `ToolTip.Tip` on the Y-axis label column; (5) new `SetUnfilledRegion(lastSuccessfulFill)` + `UnfilledRegionGeometry`/`HasUnfilledRegion` — a red-hatched `Path` overlay covering the X range from the last successful fill level to 110%, making it obvious the test stopped early; (6) `MainViewModel.RunContextFillStressTestAsync` calls `SetAxisRange(0,110,0,120)` after `SetSeries`, and after the probe returns calls `SetStressTestSummary` + `SetUnfilledRegion` from the result. **Rendering fix (10/17, 2nd pass):** the data `Path`s used `Stretch="Fill"` directly, which distorted the curve as the bounding box changed (axes appeared swapped, old points jumped/obliterated as new levels arrived). **Fix:** wrapped each data `Path` (and the hatch `Path`) in a `Viewbox Stretch="Fill"` with the `Path` sized 100×100 (matching the 0–100 normalized geometry space). The `Viewbox` scales the 100×100 geometry to fill the plot area, mapping X (0–100) to width and Y (0–100) to height, so Fill % stays on the X-axis and TTFT on the Y-axis, and the curve accumulates correctly. **Axis-title fix (10/17, 3rd pass):** the axis titles were back-to-front — "Fill %" (X-axis title) was on the left and "TTFT (s)" (Y-axis title) was on the right, both in a horizontal row below the plot. **Fix:** restructured the axis-title `Grid` to `ColumnDefinitions="Auto,*"` — Y-axis title ("TTFT (s)") now sits at the top-left (above the Y-axis tick labels), and X-axis title ("Fill %") is centered below the plot. **Data-accuracy fixes (10/17, 4th pass):** (a) failure points (timeouts, HTTP errors) were plotted on the TTFT curve at `TotalMs/1000`, which is misleading (an immediate HTTP 400 shows as ~0s TTFT, implying superior performance). **Fix:** failure points are now plotted at the TOP of the chart (Y=0 in screen coords = max TTFT) at the failure fill level, so it's clear the test stopped there without implying a fast TTFT. (b) the origin-prepending logic (which injected a fake (0,100) point when the bounding box was degenerate) was distorting the curve and causing the first phase to disappear when no longer being measured. **Fix:** removed the origin-prepending — the `Viewbox` in the XAML now handles the scaling correctly, so the curve accumulates all successful points without distortion. **Graph improvements (10/17, 5th pass):** (1) X-axis now starts at 25% (the first fill quantum) instead of 0%, avoiding wasted space on the empty 0–25% range. (2) Added a second graph for tok/s (throughput) alongside the TTFT graph, showing the tradeoff between speed and context fill. The tok/s graph uses a blue line (#60A5FA) and is displayed below the TTFT graph. **Context diagnostic (10/17, 6th pass):** Added diagnostic logs to track the `OverrideContext` value and the `local_context` value reported to Cline, to investigate the context-reporting discrepancy (Cline's context bar reads differently from FluxMux's context reporting). **Tests:** updated `SeedBaseline_creates_a_visible_curve` (now expects 2 points, not 3). Full suite: **1616 passed, 7 failed** (all 7 pre-existing `IPlatformRenderInterface` issue, down from 11 — the origin-prepending removal fixed 4 geometry tests). **Deploy state: compiled/tested in `artifacts\fixrun\`, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **"Load selected profile on startup" tickbox added to Quick Select tab** (10/17): Added a new tickbox adjacent to and inline with the "Route requests" tickbox. **Changes:** (1) added `LoadSelectedProfileOnStartup` property to `MainViewModel` (persisted to config as `LoadSelectedProfileOnStartup`); (2) added the tickbox to the Quick Select tab's `WrapPanel` (right after the "Route requests" `StackPanel`); (3) added `AutoLoadSelectedProfileOnStartupAsync()` method to `MainViewModel` that auto-loads the selected local profile on startup (no-op if the feature is off or no local profile is selected); (4) wired it up in `MainWindow.OnWindowOpened` so it runs after the window is shown. **Build:** succeeds (0 errors). **Deploy:** close the app → rebuild → restart. **Verify:** check the "Load selected profile on startup" tickbox appears next to "Route requests" in the Quick Select tab; enable it, select a local profile, save, restart the app, and confirm the profile auto-loads.
- **Graph/terminal split: 1/3 default via DPI-safe code-behind nudge** (10/17): The `1,Auto,2` star split brought back the huge right margin (confirmed by user). **Fix:** reverted to `*,Auto,*` (centered, confirmed working) and added a code-behind nudge in `MainWindow.OnWindowOpened` that sets Column 0 (graph) to 33% of the grid's actual width on first load (via a one-time `LayoutUpdated` handler). This is DPI-safe: it computes the sash position from the actual available width, so it works at any resolution or DPI scale. The user can still drag the sash to resize. **Build:** succeeds (0 errors). **Deploy:** close the app → rebuild → restart. **Verify:** the divider should sit at the 1/3 mark (graph smaller, terminal larger) with no huge right margin. Test at multiple DPI scales (1366×768 @ 100%, 1920×1080 @ 100%, 4K @ 150–200%).
- **Graph/terminal split: re-introduced 1/3 default with DPI-safe axis labels** (10/17): The user wants the divider NOT centered (graph 1/3, terminal 2/3). The `1,Auto,2` star split previously broke (huge right margin) — likely because the graph's axis label `TextBlock`s (default `TextWrapping="NoWrap"`) reported an oversized desired width that inflated the grid when the graph panel was narrow. **Fix (DPI-safe):** (1) added `TextWrapping="NoWrap" TextTrimming="CharacterEllipsis"` to all Y-axis label `TextBlock`s so they don't report an oversized desired width; (2) re-introduced `ColumnDefinitions="1,Auto,2"` (graph=1 part, terminal=2 parts → 1/3 : 2/3 default). This is proportional (star) sizing, so it scales with the actual available space and DPI — no hardcoded pixels. **Build:** succeeds (0 errors). **Deploy:** close the app → rebuild → restart. **Verify at multiple DPI scales:** 1366×768 @ 100%, 1920×1080 @ 100%, 4K @ 150–200%. If the huge right margin reappears, the cause is the `GridSplitter` interaction (not the axis labels) — in that case, fall back to `*,Auto,*` and nudge the sash via code-behind using `Bounds.Width * 0.33` on first load.

The `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` method is already using the recorded samples (if they exist). **Hypothesis:** the recorded samples might not be being saved, or the `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` method might not be finding them. **Next step:** check the debug output for `[LaunchTelemetry]` entries to see if `RecordLocalLaunchTelemetry` is being called and if `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` is finding the recorded samples.

The build cannot update `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` because the running app locks it (MSB3026 file-lock errors). **To deploy:** (1) close the running app, (2) run `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj -c Debug` (or `build-fluxmux.bat`), (3) restart from `bin\Debug\net10.0`. All latest fixes (origin-prepending graph, Grid-wrapped polylines, crash fix, context-limit fix, false-overflow fix) are confirmed in the source and in the `artifacts\fixrun` build. **NOTE:** the origin-prepending code is in `TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs` lines 277-295 (prepends a bottom-left origin point when the success points' bounding box is degenerate, so `Stretch="Fill"` doesn't collapse to zero size).
- **Stress test graph: origin-prepending fixes zero-bounds collapse** (10/17): The single-prompt stress test showed no curve. The diagnostic log confirmed points WERE being added, but `Stretch="Fill"` on the `Polyline` collapses to zero size when the geometry's bounds have a zero width OR zero height (Avalonia's `Shape.CalculateSizeAndTransform` sets the scale to 0 for a zero dimension). During prefill, all "waiting" points are at Y=0 (same Y → zero height), so the curve was invisible. **Fix:** in `UpdateGraphStrings`, when the success points' bounding box is degenerate (width < 0.5 OR height < 0.5), prepend a reference point at the bottom-left origin (0, 100 in screen coords). This guarantees the geometry spans the full plot and `Stretch="Fill"` renders it. The origin is a natural starting point for the curve. Also wrapped both `Polyline`s (success + failure markers) in a `Grid` (a `Border` only allows one child — the earlier AVLN3000 build error). **Tests:** updated `SeedBaseline_creates_a_visible_curve` (now expects 3 points: origin + 2 baseline); full suite **1615 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\fixrun\`), NOT deployed to `bin\Debug\net10.0`** (running app locks it). **OPEN:** the stress test button flickers rapidly before being clicked — likely a `StressTestRunning`/`IsVisible` binding re-render issue; needs investigation.
- **Build fixed: two Polyline elements in a Border (AVLN3000)** (10/17): The rebuild failed with `AVLN3000: Unable to find a setter that allows multiple assignments to the property Child of type Avalonia.Controls.Decorator` at `MainWindow.axaml` line 3036. **Root cause:** my graph fix put two `Polyline` elements (success + failure markers) directly inside the plot-area `Border`, but a `Border` is a `Decorator` that only allows **one** child. **Fix:** wrapped both `Polyline`s in a `Grid` (which allows multiple children, both filling the same space). Build now succeeds (0 errors). All fixes confirmed present in the freshly built `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll`: `IsBenignShutdownException`, `PromptAloneExceedsContext`, `ManagedLocalContext`, `SeedBaseline`, `ReplaceCollectionInPlace`. **NOTE:** the running app was launched from an OLD BACKUP (`backups\FluxMux.Avalonia_0.3.0_20261009_source`), not the current workspace — the user must close it and launch from `bin\Debug\net10.0` (via `start-ai-fluxmux.cmd` or `build-fluxmux.bat`) to get the fixes.
- **App no longer crashes on restart from benign shutdown/cancellation exceptions** (10/17): Many `FluxMux_Crash_*.txt` reports were accumulating on the Desktop (OneDrive). Two crash types: (1) `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` — a `SocketException (995)` "I/O aborted (thread exit / application request)" from a fire-and-forget `TcpClient.CompleteConnectAsync` cancelled during shutdown; (2) `Avalonia UI thread (Dispatcher.UnhandledException)` — an `OperationCanceledException` from `FluxMuxRuntimeService.LaunchLocalAsync` (line 3461, `launchToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested()` in the launch-poll loop) when a launch is cancelled or the app shuts down mid-launch. **Root cause:** the `Dispatcher.UIThread.UnhandledException` handler set `args.Handled = false` (let the crash continue), so the benign `OperationCanceledException` crashed the app on every restart. **Fix** (`App.axaml.cs`): added `IsBenignShutdownException(Exception?)` which walks the exception chain (incl. `AggregateException` inner exceptions) and returns true for `OperationCanceledException`/`TaskCanceledException`/`ObjectDisposedException`/`SocketException`/`HttpRequestException`/`IOException`. The UI-thread handler now sets `args.Handled = IsBenignShutdownException(ex)` — benign exceptions are still LOGGED (for diagnostics) but no longer crash the app; genuine faults still crash. **Tests:** full suite **1614 passed** (the 1 `CompiledXamlGuardTests` failure is a false positive from the separate-output `fixrun` build). **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed** (user must close the old-backup app → launch from `bin\Debug\net10.0`).
- **Stress test graph: fixed null-Points crash + curve rendering** (10/17): The app **crashed on restart** with `System.ArgumentNullException: Value cannot be null. (Parameter 'collection')` at `Avalonia.Points..ctor` → `PolylineGeometry..ctor` → `Polyline.CreateDefiningGeometry()` → `Shape.Render()`. **Root cause:** my earlier `Viewbox`+`Canvas` wrap re-created the `Polyline` visual, and during re-creation the `Points` binding had no DataContext yet → `Points = null` → `PolylineGeometry` threw on render → app crash. **Fix:** reverted to a direct `Polyline` (no Viewbox/Canvas) bound to `TelemetryGraph.PolylinePoints` (never null in the VM). The zero-bounds rendering issue (why the curve was invisible with `Stretch="Fill"`) is solved in the VM instead: `UpdateGraphStrings` adds a tiny 1-unit companion point when there's a single success point, so the geometry always has non-zero width and `Stretch="Fill"` scales it correctly. Also added a red failure-marker `Polyline` bound to `FailurePolylinePoints`. **Tests:** full suite **1615 passed** (the 1 `CompiledXamlGuardTests` failure is a false positive from the separate-output `fixrun` build — the DLL contains the XAML markers and the app builds/runs cleanly). **Deploy state: compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\fixrun\`), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart). **NOTE:** diagnostic logging still in `TelemetryGraphViewModel` (writes to `telemetry_graph_diag.log`) — remove before final release once confirmed working.
- **Stress test used wrong context limit (OverrideContext vs actual launched context)** (10/17): The stress test kept failing at 75% fill with `HTTP 400: "prompt already fills the window"` even after the false-overflow fix. **Root cause:** the stress test computed fill levels from `GetLocalProfileContextLimit` (which reads `OverrideContext` = 393,216), but the llama-server was actually **launched with a 262,144 context** (the gateway's `local_context` = 262,144, confirmed in the gateway log). So a "75%" prompt (294K tokens) was actually **112% of the real 262K window** — a genuine overflow. **Fix:** added `FluxMuxRuntimeService.ManagedLocalContext` (public property exposing the actual launched context the gateway is enforcing) and changed the stress test to use it (with fallback to `OverrideContext`). Now the fill levels match what the gateway will actually allow (confirmed in the latest run: `Context limit: 262144`). **Tests:** full suite **1615 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test graph panel now renders the live curve** (10/17): The graph panel showed no curve while the test ran. **Root cause:** `TelemetryGraphViewModel` exposed `PolylinePoints`/`FailurePolylinePoints` as `IList<Point>` and **replaced** the collection with a new `List<Point>` on every update (`PolylinePoints = successList;`). Avalonia's `PolylineGeometry` subscribes to `INotifyCollectionChanged` on the *bound* collection (via `OnPointsChanged` → `InvalidateGeometry`), so swapping the instance left the geometry pointing at the old (often empty) collection and the curve never re-rendered. **Fix:** changed both to stable `ObservableCollection<Point>` that are mutated **in place** (Clear + Add via `ReplaceCollectionInPlace`), never replaced — so the geometry's collection-changed subscription fires and the curve re-renders on every update. **Secondary fix:** the baseline was a single `AddPoint(0,0)`, which renders as a zero-size figure (invisible); added `SeedBaseline()` (two points: a flat baseline) and used it in `RunContextFillStressTestAsync` so the curve is visible from the moment the test starts. **Tests:** 8 new `TelemetryGraphViewModelTests` (stable-instance invariant across AddPoint/UpdateLastPoint/SetSeries/Clear, point routing, baseline visibility, summary text); full suite **1614 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\fixrun\`), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test false "context full" block at high fill + bogus "Failed at 100%" summary** (10/17): The stress test failed at 75% fill (294,912 tokens vs a 393,216 limit — ~98K free) with `HTTP 400: "this turn was too large for the loaded Context — the prompt already fills the window"`, and when zero levels completed the summary read a bogus "Failed at 100% fill ()". **Root cause 1 (false overflow):** the blocking path's prompt-alone check used `PromptExceedsContext`, which reserves a fixed 256-token headroom. On a 393K window that is 0.065% — so a prompt at 99.9%+ fill was hard-blocked even though the model had ~98K free tokens and would answer a 32-token summary fine. The earlier "20% headroom" fix only relaxed the prompt+reply-budget rule, not the prompt-alone check. **Fix:** added `FluxMuxGatewayRouting.PromptAloneExceedsContext` (strict `prompt > window`, no headroom) and used it in `EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext` for the blocking decision. Genuine overflows are still caught (the model reports them; `PromptExceedsContext` still reserves headroom for the non-blocking compaction / reload-offer paths). **Root cause 2 (bogus summary):** `LimitBehavior` used `Levels.FirstOrDefault(l => l.FillPercent == 100)`, which on an empty/partial list returns a default struct (`IsSuccess=false`, `Status=""`) → "Failed at 100% fill ()". **Fix:** guard `Levels.Count == 0` → "No fill level completed"; and check `FillPercent` before reading `IsSuccess`/`Status` so a missing 100%/110% level no longer reads as a failure. Also implemented the missing `FluxMuxRuntimeService.IsLocalReloadOfferStale` (static, `<70%` staleness rule) that the untracked `LocalReloadOfferStalenessTests.cs` referenced — it was blocking the whole test build. **Tests:** 3 new routing tests + 7 new `ContextFillStressResultTests` + 4 `LocalReloadOfferStale` tests; full suite **1606 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy state: compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\fixrun\`), NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test graph appears immediately** (10/16): The graph panel (title, axes, baseline point) now appears the moment the stress test starts, not after the blocking `GetLocalHardwareLaunchAdvice` call (which makes `nvidia-smi` / external process calls that can take 10-20s). Fix: moved `TelemetryGraph.SetSeries` and `TelemetryGraph.AddPoint(0, 0, ...)` to right after the model-running check, before the blocking hardware context computation. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Launch countdown compact single-line (no word-wrap)** (10/16): Fixed the countdown-monitor branch of `ConciseDiagnosticStatus` so the compact line is actually short. The previous fix stripped the `"llama-server is loading "` prefix but left `left` as `"{model}... cold-load wait 3m 20s"`, so the line was still long and word-wrapped. Fix: split `left` on `"..."` and keep only the first part (the model name). Now the terminal shows `loading {model} · est left · 2m 45s` — short, no word-wrap. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test abort reporting (re-throw OCE)** (10/16): Fixed an issue where cancelling the stress test still reported it as "complete." The `OperationCanceledException` was being caught by the inner `catch (Exception ex)` in `ProbeProgressiveAsync` / `ProbeSinglePromptAsync` before it propagated to the outer `catch (OperationCanceledException)` in `RunAsync`. The inner catch swallowed it and returned a failure result, so `RunAsync` never saw the cancellation. Fix: added a `catch (OperationCanceledException) when (cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)` that re-throws, so `RunAsync` sees the cancellation and sets `Aborted = true`. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Launch unresponsiveness fix (10-20s freeze)** (10/16): The launch path ran synchronously on the UI thread and made ~10+ blocking external calls (`nvidia-smi` ×3-4, `llama-server --help`, `where`, `netstat`, `Process.Kill` + `WaitForExit` on the previous llama-server) that together froze the app for 10-20s. Fix: wrapped the launch-prep phase (gateway check, stale cleanup, endpoint checks, process stop) in `Task.Run` in `FluxMuxRuntimeService.LaunchLocalAsync`, moving it to the thread pool. The lambda returns an early-exit `RuntimeActionResult` (already-running / port-in-use / switch-blocked) or `null` to continue; `stoppedPrevious` and `reclaimableVramGiB` are hoisted out of the lambda so the rest of the launch still sees them. The UI stays responsive and the countdown monitor keeps ticking. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded` (0 errors). **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Launch telemetry compact single-line (no word-wrap)** (10/16): Rewrote the countdown-monitor branch of `ConciseDiagnosticStatus` to produce a SHORT single line that won't word-wrap in the terminal: `loading {model} · {phase} · {timer}` (e.g. `loading qwen3-27b · est left · 20s`). Added `ExtractTimerFigure` (pulls the trailing duration out of the countdown fragment) and `ShortenPhaseLabel` (shortens verbose phase labels like "AI-FluxMux is testing the public endpoint" → "testing endpoint"). The full text stays in the status bar; only the terminal log uses the compact form. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test button re-enabled immediately after cancel/complete** (10/16): The `finally` block's context-clear HTTP call (10s timeout) ran inline, so the stress test button stayed greyed out for that whole window. Fix: reset `_stressTestCts = null` and `StressTestRunning = false` FIRST, then run the context clear in the background via `Task.Run(() => ClearLocalModelContextAfterStressTestAsync(...))`. The button is now re-enabled immediately. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test context clear targets daemon port directly** (10/16): The previous context clear sent a chat prompt to the gateway's `/v1/chat/completions`, which routed it to whichever model was currently active — not necessarily the one just stress-tested — so it frequently reported "context clear failed." Fix: `ClearLocalModelContextAfterStressTestAsync` now sends the tiny "Clear" prompt directly to the managed llama-server's daemon port (`OrchestratorPort + 1`), bypassing the gateway. Best-effort: any failure is logged but swallowed. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test graph baseline point** (10/16): The graph stayed empty during the first level's prefill wait because no point existed yet. Fix: seed a baseline point at 0% fill / 0s TTFT right after `SetSeries`, so the curve is visible from the start. Build verified: main project `Build succeeded`. **Deploy state: compiled, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test abort reporting** (10/16): Fixed an issue where cancelling the stress test reported it as if it completed. Now the `RunAsync` method catches the `OperationCanceledException` and returns a result with `Aborted = true` instead of letting it propagate. The partial results collected before the cancellation are still written to the log file and graphed, but the description clearly states "Test ABORTED by user at X% fill" and the status message says "Stress test ABORTED at X% fill". Build verified: 0 errors; full test suite: 1596 passing. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test context clear on termination** (10/16): Fixed an issue where cancelling or completing the stress test left the model's KV cache filled with the test's large prompts, causing the next chat turn to fail with "context too small." Fix: added a "context clear" step in the `finally` block that sends a small "Clear" prompt to the model after the test completes or is cancelled, resetting the model's internal state. This is a best-effort operation — if it fails, the user can still start a new chat in the client app. Build verified: 0 errors; full test suite: 1596 passing. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Stress test JSON parent error fix** (10/16): Fixed "The node already has a parent" error in `ContextFillStressProbe`. Both `MeasurePromptTokensAsync` and `BuildChatRequest` were adding `JsonObject` nodes to a new `JsonArray` without cloning them first. The nodes already had a parent (the original `messages` list), so adding them to a new array threw. Fix: use `DeepClone()` when adding nodes to the new `JsonArray`, matching `LocalPromptTokenCounter.CountAsync`. Build verified: 0 errors; full test suite: 1596 passing. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Graph/terminal overlap fix** (10/16): Removed `MinHeight="120"` from the graph/terminal grid so it can shrink when the diagnostics top bar is dragged down. Previously the `MinHeight` forced the graph/terminal area to stay at least 120px tall, causing it to overlap the bottom bar when the available space shrank. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Concurrent launch race fix (second launch wins, immediate)** (10/16): Fixed a race condition where initiating a second local launch while the first was still in progress (between `RememberParkedLocalLaunch` and `process.Start()`) caused two llama-server processes to run concurrently, competing for the port/VRAM. Root cause: `_localServerProcess` is only set after `process.Start()`, so the second launch's stop-previous logic couldn't see the first launch's in-progress work. Fix: added `SupersedeInProgressLocalLaunch` which immediately cancels any in-progress launch when a new one starts, using a linked `CancellationTokenSource` so the superseded launch aborts at its next cancellation check. The second launch (user's revised intent) cleanly takes over. Build verified: 0 errors; full test suite: 1596 passing. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Concise launch telemetry** (10/16): Added `ConciseDiagnosticStatus` formatter that shortens verbose launch-phase `StatusMessage` lines into compact single-line forms for the diagnostic terminal (e.g. "launch: starting local model", "loading: {model} · {elapsed} · est {remaining}"). The status bar keeps the full text; only the terminal log uses the compact form. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Narrower vertical divider** (10/16): Removed `Margin="3,0"` from the vertical `GridSplitter` between the graph and terminal so it matches the horizontal sash width. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Real-chat stress test (progressive + single-prompt modes)** (10/16): Rewrote `ContextFillStressProbe` to drive a **progressive, diverse, measured** conversation through the gateway (the same path a real chat uses), so the logged behavior reflects the model's *real* behavior as context fills — including the YaRN-extended region. Each level builds a realistic multi-turn conversation (diverse, non-compressible content: prose, code, numbers, tool-style output), measuring the model's **actual prompt-token count** after each turn via llama-server's `input_tokens` endpoint (the same ground-truth mechanism the gateway uses) and re-measuring until the target is hit. A **fast single-prompt mode** is available via a UI toggle (ComboBox next to the "Stress test" button). Context is cleared between levels (new conversation per level), and the model is handed a clear context back at the end. **Fix (10/16):** the first version relied on `usage.prompt_tokens` in the SSE stream, which the gateway does not populate — so the measured count read 0 and the test "completed instantly." Now uses `LocalPromptTokenCounter.CountAsync` (llama-server's `input_tokens` endpoint) for the ground-truth count. Build verified: 0 errors; full test suite: 1596 passing. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed** (user must close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart).
- **Live graph during stress test** (10/16): Added `TelemetryGraphViewModel.UpdateLastPoint` so the graph builds in real time during long prefill waits (the heartbeat now updates the last point in place instead of leaving the graph empty until the level completes). Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Stretchy divider between graph and terminal** (10/16): Replaced the fixed `*,2*` graph/terminal split with `*,Auto,*` + a vertical `GridSplitter` (new `DiagnosticsVerticalSashTheme` mirroring the existing horizontal sash). The user can now drag to resize the graph vs. the terminal. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Concise stress-test telemetry** (10/16): Made all stress-test log lines short single-line format (e.g. `fill 90% · 324K · TTFT 41.2s · OK 8.3 tok/s`) to stop word-wrap clutter. The heartbeat now logs only on change (5s cadence). Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test prompts to launch model first** (10/16): The context-fill stress test now checks if a local model is running before starting. If no model is running, it shows a message asking the user to launch a model first. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Stress test graph panel height matches terminal panel** (10/16): Restructured the diagnostics panel layout so the graph panel (left) and terminal panel (right) are in the same row, with the button row spanning both columns below them. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.
- **Telemetry graph fills available height** (10/15): The reply-speed telemetry graph in the diagnostics panel now expands to fill the available height dynamically, matching the adjacent terminal panel. Build verified: 0 errors. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed**.

Fix: moved `Background="#7C3AED"` / `Foreground="White"` / `BorderBrush="#5B21B6"` **inline** on the button (matching the working "Stop model" button pattern). The `ToolTip.Tip` was already inline and is preserved. Full suite **1670 passed, 0 failed** (XAML-only). **Deploy: compiled, NOT deployed** (user: close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart). |
| 10g | **Graph Y-axis: top/bottom numbers misaligned with tick marks** | ✅ DONE 10/24 — the Y-axis "0" and max numbers were vertically centered on their tick positions, but the code-behind clamped them to the canvas bounds (`top<0 -> 0`, bottom overflow -> clamp up), which pushed the top number DOWN and the bottom number UP, off-centre from their tick marks. Fix (DPI/font-aware, no static pixel constants): reserve a top+bottom **buffer zone** (otherwise empty) around the plot. The buffer = half the tallest Y-label's rendered height (`DesiredSize.Height`, which already reflects DPI scaling + active font), applied as a top/bottom `Margin` on the plot border so its drawing area insets and the buffer space sits within the shared grid row. The code-behind `PositionYAxisLabelSet` now offsets the tick/label Y positions by the buffer so the numbers sit **centred on their ticks** within the buffer, with no clamping. The margin is only rewritten when the buffer actually changes (e.g. after a DPI/font-scale change), so no layout pass on every 50 ms tick. Falls back to the current margin if labels aren't measured yet (first frame). Full suite **1670 passed, 0 failed** (code-behind-only; no XAML change needed). **Deploy: compiled, NOT deployed** (user: close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart). |

Root cause: the button's `IsVisible` is bound to `HailMaryButtonVisible`, but its command's `CanExecute` (`CanHailMaryShrinkTurn => HailMaryButtonVisible`) was **never re-queried** when that property changed. CommunityToolkit.Mvvm's `[RelayCommand]` does not automatically call `NotifyCanExecuteChanged` when an observable property the `CanExecute` depends on changes. So when `HailMaryButtonVisible` flipped false→true, the button became visible but stayed disabled (grey + no tooltip). Fix: added `partial void OnHailMaryButtonVisibleChanged(bool value)` in `MainViewModel.cs` that calls `HailMaryShrinkTurnCommand.NotifyCanExecuteChanged()`, so the command re-evaluates `CanExecute` (now true) and the button becomes enabled (purple + tooltip shows). Full suite **1670 passed, 0 failed**. **Deploy: compiled, NOT deployed** (user: close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart). |
