## Visual theme & maintenance roadmap (10/15, planning — NOT yet started)

**Context:** Avalonia is at **12.1.1**; latest stable is **12.1.4** (patch-level only: TextBlock ellipsis/wrap fixes, theme-switch crash fix, Windows pointer-capture + DPI robustness, `:empty`/`Content`/`TableViewColumn.IsVisible` additions). Upgrading is low-risk but **not required** to fix the Polyline crash (that was a code bug, version-independent). No breaking changes in 12.1.2→12.1.4.

**Current theming state (audited):**
- Base theme: `FluentTheme` (Avalonia 12.1.1), `RequestedThemeVariant="Light"` (locked to Light).
- `App.axaml` has a full **Light + Dark** `ThemeDictionaries` with ~45 named `Flux*` brushes (card/pane/muted backgrounds, borders, foregrounds, link, tints, quick-select states, row stripes). Good, maintainable foundation.
- Coverage in `MainWindow.axaml` (3,291 lines): **178 theme-brush refs vs 83 hardcoded hex colors** (~1/3 hardcoded). The hardcoded ones are mostly accent/status colors (`#2563EB`/`#1D4ED8`/`#175CD3`/`#2260A5`/`#53B1FD` blues, `#34D399`/`#4ADE80`/`#16A34A` greens, `#344054` border, `#101828` fill, etc.) — used in the telemetry graph, status pills, inline highlights. These are **wrong in dark mode** and have already drifted apart (5+ near-identical blues doing similar jobs).

**Logical route to a more attractive theme (incremental, build-verifiable, no layout logic touched):**
1. **Migrate the 83 hardcoded colors → `Flux*` theme brushes** (add missing semantic brushes: `FluxAccent`(+Hover), `FluxSuccess`/`FluxWarning`/`FluxDanger`/`FluxInfo`, `FluxChartLine`/`FluxChartFill`). Fixes dark mode everywhere; makes any future theme a one-file edit.
2. **Consolidate into a small semantic accent/status palette** (the scattered hex values become named intents → cohesiveness).
3. **Tune `FluentTheme`** for the "attractive" feel: card `CornerRadius` (4→6–8), `BoxShadow` elevation on cards, Inter font-size/weight hierarchy, consistent spacing scale, subtle gradients on chrome/pane backgrounds.
4. **(Optional) Add a Light/Dark/"Follow system" toggle** in settings, persisted to config — the Dark dictionary already exists.
5. **(Not recommended) Swap the base theme** — high effort; `FluentTheme` + `Flux*` overlays is already clean. Payoff is in steps 1–4.

**Does it reduce code bloat / ease maintenance?**
- **Yes, meaningfully:** 83 scattered hex values → ~10–15 named brushes; one source of truth per theme (two `ResourceDictionary` blocks in `App.axaml`); removes the entire "looks broken in dark mode" defect class; smaller cognitive surface.
- **Honest limits:** `MainWindow.axaml` line count roughly stays the same (brush refs are often longer than hex literals) — the win is in **distinct values + duplication + defect surface**, not raw line count. `App.axaml` grows ~10–15 lines/theme (pays for itself).
- **Does NOT fix the real bloat:** the structural monoliths — `MainWindow.axaml` (3,291 lines) and `MainViewModel.cs` (6,000+ lines).

**Recommended sequence if maintenance is the driver (do theming + de-bloating together):**
1. **Extract the big panels into `UserControl`s** (telemetry graph, quick-select, diagnostics terminal, etc.) — the real bloat reducer.
2. **Migrate colors to theme brushes as each panel is extracted** — each new component built clean; migrate one panel at a time instead of a 3,000-line big-bang; build-verify each step.

**Status:** planning only. No code changes made for theming. Next step if pursued: read-only map of natural `UserControl` boundaries in `MainWindow.axaml` (line ranges) to size the extraction plan.

## Crash fix: telemetry-graph Polyline ArgumentNullException (10/15)

**Symptom:** Current dev build crashed to desktop on launch (window came up, then exited). `FluxMux_Startup_Error.log` (2026-10-15 21:29:33):
`System.ArgumentNullException (Parameter 'collection')` in `Avalonia.Media.PolylineGeometry..ctor` ← `Polyline.CreateDefiningGeometry` ← `Shape.Render`.

**Root cause:** The new telemetry-graph panel's `Polyline` (`MainWindow.axaml`) binds `Points` to `TelemetryGraph.PointsString`, which is `string.Empty` until the context-fill stress test runs. An empty point collection makes Avalonia's `Polyline` throw during render. The panel is always present on the main window, so every launch crashed.

**Fix (keeps axes/labels visible once a series is initiated, only gates the line):**
- `TelemetryGraphViewModel`: new `[ObservableProperty] bool HasPoints` — `false` in `SetSeries`/`Clear`, set in `UpdateGraphStrings` to `successPoints.Length>0 || failurePoints.Length>0`.
- `MainWindow.axaml`: `Polyline` now has `IsVisible="{Binding TelemetryGraph.HasPoints}"`. The axes `Canvas` (gated on `IsActive`) and the `XLabel`/`YLabel` row stay visible the moment `SetSeries` runs, so the frame/axes/labels display before data is collected; the line draws in as points arrive.

**CRITICAL CORRECTION (10/15, "no improvement" round):** The `IsVisible` guard ALONE was NOT enough. In **Avalonia 12.1.1**, `Polyline` builds its `PolylineGeometry` eagerly and throws `ArgumentNullException` on an empty `Points` string **even when `IsVisible="False"`** — so the crash persisted (log entries 21:30, 21:40, 21:41). The real fix: the bound `PointsString`/`FailurePointsString` must **never be empty**. Added `SafePointsString(value)` → returns `"0,0"` (a valid single degenerate point) when the value is null/whitespace; applied to the field initializers, `SetSeries`, `Clear`, the no-data branch, and the final assignment in `UpdateGraphStrings`. Now the `Polyline` always has a non-empty point collection, so `PolylineGeometry` cannot throw.

**DEPLOY TRAP (why the user saw no improvement):** The running app was launched from a **stale** build folder `artifacts\diagbuild\Debug\net10.0` (DLL dated 12/10/2026, NO fix at all). There are now MANY build output folders (`diagbuild`, `fixbuild`, `fixtest`, `testrun`, `verify`, `publish`). The fix only lands in `bin\Debug\net10.0` when you run `start-ai-fluxmux.cmd` (or `dotnet build`). **Do NOT launch from any `artifacts\*` folder.** To deploy: close the running app → run `start-ai-fluxmux.cmd` in the project root (it kills the old process, builds fresh Debug into `bin\Debug`, and launches that).

**TRUE ROOT CAUSE (10/15, 3rd round — the string binding was the bug):** Both the `IsVisible` fix AND the `SafePointsString` (never-empty string) fix FAILED — the 21:47:33 crash came from `bin\Debug` rebuilt at 21:47:28 which HAD `SafePointsString`, yet still threw the identical `Polyline` `ArgumentNullException`. Inspecting the actual **Avalonia 12.1.1 source** (`src/Avalonia.Controls/Shapes/Polyline.cs`): `Polyline.Points` is typed **`IList<Point>`** (a collection), NOT a string. The XAML bound it to a `string` (`PointsString`). Avalonia 12 has no string→`IList<Point>` binding conversion that produces a valid collection, so the property ended up **null**, and `PolylineGeometry..ctor(IEnumerable<Point>)` → `Points..ctor` → `List..ctor(collection)` threw `ArgumentNullException` on the null collection. No amount of string-side guarding (non-empty, `IsVisible`) could fix a type mismatch.
**Final fix:** `TelemetryGraphViewModel` now exposes `IList<Point> PolylinePoints` and `IList<Point> FailurePolylinePoints` (always non-null, start as `new List<Point>()`, replaced on each data change). `MainWindow.axaml` binds `Polyline.Points="{Binding TelemetryGraph.PolylinePoints}"` (a real collection, matching the property type) and keeps `IsVisible="{Binding TelemetryGraph.HasPoints}"`. The `PointsString`/`FailurePointsString` string props remain (harmless) but are no longer what the `Polyline` binds to. Build: 0 errors.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors, 33 pre-existing warnings) in `artifacts\testrun\`. **Deploy state: compiled/tested, NOT deployed.** To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Live telemetry graph + dynamic-timeout stress test (10/15)

**Goal:** A live, inline graph that plots the context-fill stress test's prefill curve (fill% vs TTFT) as data arrives. Designed as a *generic* panel for future consumers (launch progress, model-switch progress, chat port-rule triggers, etc.).

**Changes:**
- **New file `ViewModels/TelemetryGraphViewModel.cs`** (~120 lines): `TelemetryPoint` record struct + `TelemetryGraphViewModel` with `SetSeries()`, `AddPoint()`, `Clear()`. Normalizes X→[0,100]% and Y→[0,100]% (inverted for screen coords), with optional log-scale Y (for the steep prefill curve). Exposes `PointsString` (for `Polyline`) and `FailurePointsString` (for red markers).
- **Dynamic timeout + stop-at-first-failure** (`ContextFillStressProbe.cs`): replaced the fixed 90s timeout with a generous 600s (10 min) default so slow hardware can actually measure the prefill curve. The test now **stops at the first failed level** (prefill cost is monotonic, so higher levels can't succeed under the same timeout). Added `StoppedEarly` flag to `ContextFillStressResult`; updated `LimitBehavior`, `FormatForDiagnostics`, and `WriteToLogFile` to report the early stop.
- **Graph XAML panel** (`MainWindow.axaml`): restructured the diagnostics Row 1 into a horizontal split — graph (1/3 width, `Grid.Column="0"`) + terminal (2/3 width, `Grid.Column="1"`). The graph is a `Border` with a title bar, a `Canvas` plot area (axes + `Polyline` for successful points), a "No telemetry" placeholder, and axis labels. Height matches the terminal (`MinHeight="120"`).
- **Wiring** (`MainViewModel.RunContextFillStressTestAsync`): calls `TelemetryGraph.SetSeries("Context-fill stress test", "Fill %", "TTFT (s)", isLogScaleY: true)` at start, and `TelemetryGraph.AddPoint(fillPercent, ttftSeconds, label, isFailure)` on each `onLevelComplete`.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). Full suite: **1596 passed, 0 failed**.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`. **NOT deployed.** To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

**Future consumers (Phase 2):** launch progress, model-switch progress, chat port-rule triggers, generation speed. Each just pushes points into the same `TelemetryGraphViewModel` — no new UI code.

## Warm-launch telemetry + honest first-reply message (10/15)

**Issues:**
1. The countdown estimate was not being updated for warm launches (when the model is already loaded). The `alreadyRunningThisProfile` path in `LaunchLocalAsync` returned early without calling `RecordLocalLaunchTelemetry`, so the config's samples array only reflected cold launches.
2. The "Local route ready" first-reply message referenced the invisible first-reply probe ("last first-reply probe took Ns"), which the user has no context for — they didn't issue the request, didn't see the response, and had no sense of when it happened.

**Fixes:**
- **Warm-launch telemetry** (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.LaunchLocalAsync`): added a `RecordLocalLaunchTelemetry` call in the `alreadyRunningThisProfile` path, using `reuseProbe.LatencyMs` (the time to verify the already-running model is responsive). This ensures the countdown estimate reflects both cold and warm launches.
- **Honest first-reply message** (`MainViewModel.ApplyLocalPublicEndpointSuccess`): removed the probe-referencing branch. The message is now a single, honest, hardware-agnostic note: "your model is now launched, but depending on your settings, and hardware, and the complexity of your first message, you should expect a noticeable delay before it can respond. Please be patient…". No specific time is quoted because it depends on the user's message size, settings, and hardware.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). Full suite: **1596 passed, 0 failed**.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`. **NOT deployed.** To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Data-driven "Local route ready" first-reply message (10/15)

**Issue:** The "Local route ready" message used a static `isLongLoad` guess ("few minutes" vs "a moment") that mislabeled VRAM profiles (context > 131072 → "few minutes" even when GPU-only and fast). The user wanted an honest, hardware-agnostic message, ideally driven by recorded first-reply telemetry.

**Fix** (`MainViewModel.ApplyLocalPublicEndpointSuccess`): replaced the `isLongLoad` branch with a data-driven message:
- **When first-reply telemetry exists** (`GetLocalLaunchFirstReplyMs > 0`): "· last first-reply probe took Ns (a larger first message will take longer — please be patient)". The recorded probe is a ~10K-token request, so it's framed as a floor.
- **When no data yet** (first launch of the profile): "· your model is now launched, but depending on your settings, and hardware, and the complexity of your first message, you should expect a noticeable delay before it can respond. Please be patient…".

This covers GPU+CPU VRAM profiles, YaRN cold KV-cache allocation, and other impediments without over-promising, and self-corrects as more launches are recorded.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). Full suite: **1596 passed, 0 failed**. No tests reference the old strings.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`. **NOT deployed.**

## Spill-compact suppression for probe requests (10/15)

**Issue:** The "context too small" / "switch to VRAM profile" offer (from `OfferSpillCompactIfNeeded`) was firing during the stress test at 50%+ fill (when the prompt exceeds the VRAM window of 163840). This blocked the test's fill progression and confused the user. The probe-header suppression for the *document* dialog was deployed, but the spill-compact offer was not suppressed.

**Fix** (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.OfferSpillCompactIfNeeded`): added the same `isProbeRequest` early-return as `OfferDocumentContextDialogIfNeeded`. Probe requests (stress test) now skip both steering offers.

**Expected stress-test behavior after deploy:**
- 25-50%: succeed (no dialog, no block)
- 75%: either complete (if prefill is fast enough) or time out (with the heartbeat showing the wait)
- 100%: hit the `ShouldBlockLocalForward` check → 400 error "context too small" (this is the *expected* limit behavior for a "test to failure")
- 110%: same

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). Full suite: **1596 passed, 0 failed**.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`. **NOT deployed.** To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Stress test feedback + steering-dialog suppression + context gauge (10/15)

**Issues (user report):**
1. Clicking "Stress test" gave no visible response for minutes; the long inactivity between launch and a responsive model was not monitored/reported.
2. The "large document" steering dialog appeared *during* the stress test and its unanswered state likely caused the 75%+ timeouts (the test's single dominant user message >50K tokens triggers `DetectDominantDocument`, which sets `documentContextMode=pending`).
3. Countdown numbers were wrong: the YaRN profile's `slowest_ms` was stuck at a 99s cold-start outlier while real launches were ~18–20s.
4. The "retain fidelity" NumericUpDown in the document-context dialog was too compressed (Width=60, number not visible).
5. No live context gauge/meter.

**Root cause of the 75% "failure":** the stress probe sends one dominant user message; `DetectDominantDocument` flags any user message >50K tokens as a "document" and offers the steering dialog. The probe's 90s timeout expired while the dialog sat unanswered. The test was being *steered*, not measuring raw prefill.

**Fixes:**
- **Steering-dialog suppression for probes** (root fix): added `CloudEndpointValidationProbe.ProbeRequestHeader` (`X-FluxMux-Probe-Request`). The stress probe sets it; the gateway reads it and `OfferDocumentContextDialogIfNeeded` early-returns when `isProbeRequest`. The test now measures raw prefill/decode unsteered.
- **Immediate + live terminal feedback** (`MainViewModel.RunContextFillStressTestAsync` + `ContextFillStressProbe`): added `onLevelStart` ("Starting fill 75%… waiting for first token"), a 5s `onLevelHeartbeat` ("Still waiting… 20s elapsed"), a **Cancel stress test** button (`CancelContextFillStressTestCommand` + `StressTestRunning` flag + `InvertBoolToVisibilityConverter`), and a configurable per-level timeout (default `max(90s, promptTokens/5)` so slow hardware can reach the limit region).
- **Countdown accuracy** (`LocalLaunchCountdownEstimate.CountdownMs`): changed from "window slowest" to "window **median**" so a single cold-start outlier (the 99s) no longer dominates. Updated `LocalLaunchCountdownEstimateTests` (5 tests, incl. a new outlier-robustness case).
- **Launch→first-response telemetry** (`FluxMuxRuntimeService`): added `progressReporter` calls around the first-reply probe ("Model ready; measuring first reply…" / "First reply in N ms (launch→first-response total M ms)") + a new "Measuring first reply" phase in `NormalizeLaunchPhase`.
- **Document-context dialog UX** (`MainWindow.axaml`): widened the NumericUpDown (60→84) and added a one-line plain-English explainer of each option.
- **Live context gauge** (new): 5th "Context" gauge in the diagnostics panel (reuses `LiveUtilizationGaugeViewModel` + `SegmentedUtilizationGauge`). Gateway writes measured prompt tokens + `hotContext` to `Local_Context_Usage.json` each turn (`ReportContextUsage` callback → `WriteLocalContextUsage`); the UI's 1s telemetry loop reads it via `ReadLocalContextUsage` → `UpdateContextGauge`.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). Full suite: **1596 passed, 0 failed** (incl. the 5 updated countdown tests).

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\testrun\`). **NOT deployed.** To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

**Note on the 75% timeout:** with the dialog now suppressed, re-run the stress test to see whether 75%+ actually completes (the model may genuinely be too slow to prefill 294K tokens on GPU-only offload, in which case the heartbeat will show the wait and the higher timeout will let it finish or time out cleanly). The log `C:\AI_Workbench\Logs\context_fill_perf.log` will record the real behavior.

## YaRN "context too small" false positive (10/14)

**Issue (user report):** "Context is too small" errors firing on the YaRN profile despite "very large context space left." User confirmed the current chat is on the YaRN profile and context grew 190K→220K (both above the 163840 "nominal" limit).

**Initial (wrong) diagnosis:** Thought the YaRN profile was clamped at `n_ctx=163840` due to missing `--cache-ram`. Added `--cache-ram -1` to launch args.

**Corrected diagnosis (via gateway log):** The current session's log shows `ctx=393216` and `local prompt tokens (measured): 218134` with `status=200` — the YaRN profile **IS actually running at 393216** and serving 218K tokens fine. The earlier `n_ctx=163840` errors were from a **stale `local_context` value** in the gateway state at the moment of the overflow check. The overflow check (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:1708`) reads `hotContext` from `state["local_context"]`, which can be stale (a race between the gateway reading the state and the sync writing the correct value — documented in the comment at `FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:7976-7981`).

**Fix (defensive):** Added `--cache-ram -1` to launch args when `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` is true (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:5337-5348`). This is harmless and provides a safety net (ensures the full context is allocated even if there's a VRAM constraint), but it's **not the root cause** of the false "context too small" errors.

**Root cause (unfixed):** The stale-state race in the overflow check. The gateway reads `local_context` from the state file, which can be stale. Fix requires either (a) a public getter for `_managedLocalContext` + gateway using it, or (b) more frequent state syncs. Documented as a known issue.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). 19 launch/fit tests pass.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in separate output dirs. NOT deployed. Backup: `C:\AI_Workbench\Backups\FluxMux.Avalonia-2026-10-14_0144`.

## Context-fill stress test (on-demand, 10/14)

**Goal:** See how context fill affects performance (TTFT, tok/s) and what happens at the limit (graceful slowdown vs. crash/freeze/OOM). Useful for users with different hardware (VRAM/RAM) — the shape of the curve and the limit behavior are generalizable.

**Implementation:**
- `ContextFillStressProbe` service (`Services/ContextFillStressProbe.cs`): sends requests at increasing context fill levels (25/50/75/90/100/110% of the profile's context limit), each asking for a short 32-token answer. Measures TTFT + tok/s at each level. The 110% level deliberately exceeds the limit to observe the failure behavior.
- "Stress test" button in the diagnostics panel (`MainWindow.axaml:3207`).
- `RunContextFillStressTestCommand` in `MainViewModel.cs`: runs the probe on-demand, shows live telemetry in the diagnostics panel (via `AppendDiagnosticEntry`), and writes results to `context_fill_perf.log` (survives crash/freeze).
- Hardware context (GPU VRAM, RAM, offload mode) is recorded with each run so results are self-describing and generalizable.
- Clear "test to failure" warning shown before the test runs.

**What it reports:**
- Per fill level: fill %, prompt tokens, TTFT, total time, tok/s, status.
- Highest successful fill %.
- Limit behavior (what happened at 100% and 110%).
- Hardware context.

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). 15 launch tests pass.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in separate output dirs. NOT deployed. Backup: `C:\AI_Workbench\Backups\FluxMux.Avalonia-2026-10-14_0144`.

## YaRN "context too small" false positive — SOURCE FIX (10/14)

**Issue (user report):** "Context is too small" dialogs firing on the YaRN profile despite the model actually serving 220K+ prompts fine. User confirmed the current chat is on the YaRN profile (393K context) and the dialogs can be ignored — nothing bad happens.

**Root cause (found at source):** In `LaunchLocalAsync`, `_managedLocalContext` was set at line 3380 (from the profile's `OverrideContext`), but `WriteProxyRuntimeStateForLocal` was called at lines 3285 and 3357 — **BEFORE** line 3380. So the early state writes carried `local_context = 0` (or a stale value from a previous launch). The gateway's overflow decision reads `local_context` from the state file; when it read the stale/zero value, it misclassified prompts as "context too small" even though the actual llama-server was running at 393216.

**Fix (at source):** Moved the profile resolution + `_managedLocalContext`/`_managedLocalExtendContextIntoRam`/`_managedLocalVramContext` assignments to the **start** of `LaunchLocalAsync` (right after `root` is loaded), before any `WriteProxyRuntimeStateForLocal` call. This ensures every state write during the launch carries the correct `local_context` (393216 for the YaRN profile), so the gateway's overflow decision always uses the right value. No bandaid, no suppression — the data is correct at the source.

**Verification:** `ApplyRecommendedAutoLaunchSettings` (called later) does NOT modify `OverrideContext`, so the early and late `_managedLocalContext` values are identical. Build succeeded (0 errors). 15 launch tests pass.

**Note:** The `--cache-ram -1` fix (added earlier this session) is still in place and is a separate, correct improvement (ensures the full KV cache is allocated). This source fix addresses the *false "context too small" dialogs*, which were caused by the stale `local_context`, not by the KV cache allocation.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in separate output dirs. NOT deployed. Backup: `C:\AI_Workbench\Backups\FluxMux.Avalonia-2026-10-14_0144`.

## Launch telemetry: continuous countdown + hard-data estimate (this session, 10/14)

**Issue (user report):** The terminal "estimated time left" countdown (a) restarted at each launch stage instead of spanning the whole launch, and (b) showed a high "time left" (e.g. 187s) even after the model was already ready. The estimate was also padded (`slowest_ms + 30s`, 60s floor) rather than being hard data from previous launches of the same model+variant.

**Root causes:**
1. `StartLocalLaunchCountdownMonitor` (`MainViewModel.cs`) restarted `phaseStopwatch` on every `_localLaunchPhaseVersion` bump, so `elapsedMs` reset to 0 at each stage (pre-warmup → cold load → testing endpoint → …).
2. `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` returned `GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs` = `max(60s, slowest_ms + 30s)` — a padded *timeout budget*, not the recorded hard data. The +30s guess and 60s floor made the countdown disagree with the actual launch.
3. No distinct label for the 4th stage (model loaded, port open, but first reply not yet returned).

**Fix (7 changes):**
1. **Continuous timer.** Added `_localLaunchCountdownStartUtc` (set once at monitor start, never reset on phase change). `elapsedMs` is now `DateTime.UtcNow - _localLaunchCountdownStartUtc`, so the timer spans the entire launch. The main launch call site passes `launchStartedUtc` so the baseline matches the recorded `readyMs` baseline.
2. **Hard-data estimate.** `GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs` now returns `slowest_ms` of the same `model::variant` (fallback `median_ms` → `fastest_ms` → 0). No +30s, no 60s floor. `GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs` (the launch-loop *timeout*) is unchanged and keeps its padding — it's a safety deadline, not a user-facing estimate.
3. **4th stage label.** `NormalizeLaunchPhase` maps "waiting for first reply / waiting for health check / waiting for the public endpoint / still loading" → "Awaiting endpoint response".
4. The "taking longer than expected" flag now only fires when real elapsed > previous `slowest_ms` (hard data), not a padded number.
5. **Descriptive ready message.** `ApplyLocalPublicEndpointSuccess` now shows `"Local route ready in Xs — first reply may take a moment"` instead of the terse `"Local route ready"`. This addresses the user's report of staring at silence for 60-120s after the launch completed: the previous message gave no launch time and no "what happens next" hint, while the health monitor polled quietly.
6. **Countdown stop guard + ordering.** Added `_localLaunchCountdownStopped` (volatile bool). The guard is checked **inside the `SafeUiInvoke` lambda on the UI thread** (atomically with the write), not on the background thread — checking it cross-thread raced with the stop and let a stale "estimated time left 1s" frame overwrite the ready notice. Additionally, `ApplyLocalPublicEndpointSuccess` now calls `StopLocalLaunchCountdownMonitor()` **first** (before writing the ready status), so the flag is set and the countdown text cleared on the UI thread ahead of the ready write; any countdown frame already queued on the UI thread is then suppressed. This fixes the user's report of the "Local route ready" notice appearing *before* the countdown's final frame.
7. **First-real-reply: honest "extent" message (prediction removed).** The launch "ready" boundary (a 1-word "ready" probe) fires when llama-server can do a *trivial* completion — NOT when it can serve a real Cline request. The first large request triggers the full cold KV-cache allocation for the (extended/YaRN) context, which adds a large fixed cost a small probe can't capture. Prefill is **not linear** during cold allocation, so a scaled prediction from a 10K-token probe (e.g. "~6s") is misleading — the user's real 120K-token request took ~3 minutes. **Fix:** removed the unreliable `PredictFirstReplyMsForPrompt`-based "~Xs" figure. The ready message now shows an honest *extent*: for long-load (YaRN/extended-context) profiles, `"first real request may take a few minutes while the full context cache is allocated (normal, not a freeze)"`; for ordinary profiles, `"first reply may take a moment"`. The post-ready probe (`first_reply_ms`, now ~2K-token prompt) is still recorded for telemetry/diagnostics but no longer drives a user-facing prediction. The countdown itself is correct: it measures launch-to-ready time and stops when the launch completes (which can be well before the previous slowest, hence "stopped at 64s" — not a bug).

**Tests:** Build succeeded (0 errors). 28 launch-related tests pass (`LocalLaunch*`, `LocalFitLaunch*`, `LocalValidateLaunch*`, `LocalMultiGpuLaunch*`). No existing test asserted the old padded estimate. (Full `FluxMuxRuntimeService` is not unit-constructable — its ctor starts the gateway host + idle loops — so the estimate change is verified by build + the launch test suite, not a direct unit test.)

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in separate output dirs (`artifacts\fixbuild\`, `artifacts\fixtest\`). NOT deployed to the running app. Backup taken first: `C:\AI_Workbench\Backups\FluxMux.Avalonia-2026-10-14_0144` (full source tree + `_companion_config\` with live `fluxmux_config.json`, secrets, port rules, launch log). To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Three fixes: KV auto-pick, false context overflow, launch slowdown (this session, 10/13)

### Fix 1: Auto KV cache not picking q4_0 for hybrid models (FIXED)

**Issue (user report):** 'Auto' is not setting KV cache to q4_0 with the user's hardware (32 GB GPU, 17.8 GB Qwen3.8 NVFP4 model, 393K context).

**Root cause:** Two separate code paths were both wrong:
1. `PickKvTypeForYarnFit` used raw `KvBytesPerTokenForType` values without the hybrid-KV (0.38) and Flash Attention (0.75) adjustments.
2. The wizard's "Auto" resolution (`UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`) used `fitting[0]` (the first type in `["q8_0", "q6_k", "q5_1", "q4_1"]` that passed speed/fit checks), which picked q6_k instead of q4_0. The options list didn't include q4_0.

**Fix:**
1. `PickKvTypeForYarnFit` now computes an `effectiveScale` from the `effectiveKvBytesPerToken` parameter and applies it to all KV type byte estimates.
2. The wizard's "Auto" resolution now uses `PickKvTypeForYarnFit` instead of `fitting[0]`, and the options list now includes `q4_0`.

For the user's setup (393K context, 32 GB GPU, 17.8 GB model):
- Raw q8_0: 256 KB/token → 96 GB at 393K → doesn't fit (12.8 GB available)
- Effective q8_0 (0.38 × 0.75 = 0.285): 73 KB/token → 27.9 GB → still doesn't fit
- Effective q4_0: 27.4 KB/token → 10.4 GB → fits → **q4_0 picked**

**Tests:** 3 new tests in `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculatorTests` (hybrid model picks q4_0, effective KV fits picks q8_0, raw fallback works). All 1595 tests pass.

### Fix 2: False "context full" error when there is free context

**Issue (user report):** VRAM model gets error "This chat turn cannot continue: this turn was too large for the loaded Context — the prompt already fills the window" when there is a lot of free context.

**Root cause:** `EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext` blocked when `promptStd + maxTokens > hotContext`. The `max_tokens` reply budget (32768) is a *request*, not a guarantee — the model stops generating when it finishes its answer, which is usually far shorter than the budget. When the prompt is large but the model would naturally stop, the reply budget pushed the sum over the window, causing a false block.

**Fix:** The blocking check now only considers `prompt + maxTokens` when the prompt is already close to the window (less than 20% headroom). When the prompt fits with meaningful headroom (≥ 20%), the reply budget cannot realistically push the turn over the edge, so it is not a blocking condition. The prompt-alone check (`PromptExceedsContext`) still catches genuine overflows.

**Tests:** All 57 `FluxMuxGatewayRoutingTests` pass. All 1595 tests pass.

### Issue 3: Ordinary VRAM model profiles take massively longer to launch (FIXED)

**Issue (user report):** Ordinary VRAM model profiles now take massively longer to launch.

**Root cause:** A recent commit changed `--cache-ram -1` (unlimited) to a capped value: `max(16, totalRam * 0.60)`. For a 64 GB RAM machine, the cap is 38 GB. But YaRN profiles need ~85 GB of RAM for the KV overflow (393K context × 256 KB/token at q8_0). The capped 38 GB forces llama-server to allocate a smaller RAM KV cache, which changes launch behavior and makes it slower.

**Fix:** Reverted `--cache-ram` to `-1` (unlimited) for "Unlimited" profiles. The memory guard (`ConsiderMemoryGuardStopAsync`) already protects against OOM by stopping the server when free RAM drops below 4 GB, so an unbounded cache is safe: the guard fires before the machine runs out of memory.

**Tests:** All 1595 tests pass.

**Deploy state:** All 3 fixes compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## YaRN KV cache auto-quantization for VRAM fit (this session, 10/13)

**Issue (user report):** 3-5 tokens/sec for a 27B NVFP4 model on a 5090 with YaRN 393K context. The KV cache at q8_0 is ~96 GB, but only ~12.7 GB fits in VRAM (32 GB GPU - 17.8 GB model - overhead). The remaining ~85 GB spills to system RAM, causing a 10-20x slowdown.

**Root cause:** The YaRN wizard's "Auto" mode always used `q8_0` as the KV cache type (the `MinimumKvCacheType` floor). When the context target exceeds what fits in VRAM with q8_0, the KV cache spills to RAM, causing unacceptable generation speeds.

**Fix:** Added `PickKvTypeForYarnFit` to `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator`. When YaRN is enabled and the context target exceeds what fits in VRAM with q8_0, the wizard now automatically picks a lower KV quantization:
- q8_0 (128 KB/token) if the KV cache fits in VRAM
- q5_0 (72 KB/token) if q8_0 doesn't fit but q5_0 does
- q4_0 (48 KB/token) if q5_0 doesn't fit

For the user's setup (393K context, 32 GB GPU, 17.8 GB model):
- q8_0: 96 GB KV → doesn't fit (12.7 GB available)
- q5_0: 55 GB KV → doesn't fit
- q4_0: 36 GB KV → doesn't fit (but minimizes the RAM spill)

The user can still manually override the KV type in the wizard UI. The auto-pick only applies when the user hasn't explicitly chosen a KV type.

**Tests:** All 1592 tests pass.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## YaRN model hang: diagnostic logging added (this session, 10/13)

**Issue (user report):** Cline never responds to a YaRN-launched model. Changing model triggers "Max think first-byte hit 112s quiet (the limit is 180)" hang abort. The user confirmed the 600s `ThinkingFirstByteSeconds` extension was never actually waiting — the hang fires at 180s (the `MaxThinkFirstByteSeconds` clamp), not 600s.

**Investigation:** Added three log statements to trace the actual values. The logs reveal:

1. **`long-load detect:`** — `ctx=262144 offload=GPU only isLongLoad=True local_phase=ready` — the long-load detection IS working correctly.
2. **`hang clock:`** — `long-load profile, FirstByteDeadline=600s (ThinkingFirstByteSeconds=600, baseDeadline=25s)` — the 600s extension IS in effect.
3. **`upstream request:`** — `status=200 route=local loadingRetries=0 timeoutSec=900` — the request succeeds immediately (no 503 retries needed).

**Root cause:** The model IS responding (first byte at 30s), but then goes quiet for 82 more seconds before the hang fires at 112s total. The `thinking_budget=0` (no thinking budget) means the model isn't "thinking" in the Qwen sense — it's just slow to produce tokens after the initial SSE connection. The 30s to first byte is already very slow for a 27B model, and then it goes quiet for another 82s. This is a **model performance issue**, not a gateway timeout issue. The 600s extension is working correctly — the model just takes too long.

**User options:**
1. Use a smaller/faster model
2. Reduce the context size (less KV cache to allocate)
3. Accept the slowness and wait longer (raise `Max think first-byte` above 180s in Servers → Advanced: Port rules)

**Tests:** All 57 `FluxMuxGatewayRoutingTests` pass.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Context-overflow message referenced Port rules when Port rules were off (this session, 10/13)

**Issue (user report):** The context-overflow error message included "Port rule Headroom (currently 350 tokens) is just the small buffer reserved for the reply" and "Those settings are in Servers → Advanced: Port rules" even when Port rules were disabled. The user noted: "included reference to port rules when port rules not active."

**Root cause:** `FormatContextOverflow` in `PortRulesPostMortem.cs` has a `portRulesActive` parameter (default `true`) that gates the Port-rule-flavored wording. Two call sites in `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` passed `portRules.Enabled` as this argument, but `portRules` is always the result of `ForForwarding()` (set in the `PortRules` property setter at line 120). When Port rules are disabled, `ForForwarding()` returns a copy with all sub-flags (`CompactEnabled`, `OmitEnabled`, etc.) set to `false` but **`Enabled` remains `true`** (it's not in the `with` expression). So `portRules.Enabled` was always `true`, and the Port-rule wording was always shown. Two other call sites (lines 1207, 1224) used the 2-arg overload, defaulting `portRulesActive` to `true` unconditionally.

**Fix:** Changed all four call sites in `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` to pass `portRules.CompactEnabled` instead of `portRules.Enabled` (or the 2-arg default). When Port rules are off, `CompactEnabled` is `false` (set by `ForForwarding()`), which correctly triggers the plain-language message that omits Port-rule references. When Port rules are on, `CompactEnabled` is `true`, preserving the Port-rule wording.

**Tests:** All 20 `PortRulesPostMortemTests` + 57 `FluxMuxGatewayRoutingTests` pass.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Duplicate llama-server on profile switch (this session, 10/13)

**Issue (user report):** "I must stop both llama server versions and relaunch every time that happens." Switching from one profile (e.g. YaRN 393K) to another (e.g. VRAM-only 262K) left two live `llama-server.exe` instances fighting over the daemon port, causing 503s and 400s until the user manually killed both.

**Root cause:** In `LaunchLocalAsync`, the old code:
1. Checked if the endpoint was ready and the tracked process was null → **failed the launch** with "Local port already in use" (no kill attempt)
2. If the tracked process was alive → killed it, then waited up to 15s for the port to close
3. Only **after** the port wait → swept the port owner by PID (belt-and-braces)

The problem: when the tracked `_localServerProcess` was stale (null or exited) but a **different-profile** llama-server still held the port, step 1 failed the launch without killing anything. When the tracked process was alive but a **second** server also held the port (rapid re-launch), the 15s port wait could expire before the second server released the port, and the sweep at step 3 was the only chance to kill it — but by then the new launch had already started, producing two live instances.

**Fix:** Reordered the launch path to **kill first, verify dead, then wait for the port**:
1. Kill the tracked `_localServerProcess` (if alive) and wait up to 5s for it to exit (`WaitForProcessExitOrKillAsync`)
2. Sweep any remaining port owner by PID (`TryStopLocalEndpointOwnerByPort`) — catches stale references and rapid re-launch orphans
3. Wait up to 15s for the port to actually release (owners are already dead, so this is just TCP teardown)
4. If the port is still open → fail the launch with "Local switch blocked" (no second instance started)

The old "Local port already in use" early-fail path is removed — the launch now always attempts to reclaim the port before deciding it's blocked.

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## YaRN context window misreported as 262144 instead of 360K (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** When a YaRN profile with 360K context was active, the Cline sync status message said "Context Window was set to 262144" and Cline never showed more than 260K. The YaRN extension to 360K was being silently clamped to 262144.

**Root cause:** `DeepSeekHarnessSetup.MaxLocalContextWindow` is a hardcoded const of 262144. This value was used as the cap in:
- `LocalVramFootprintEstimate.SnapContextTokens` — clamped `ContextSafeDefault` to 262144
- `LocalVramFootprintEstimate.AdviseModelMaxContext` — capped the model's max context
- `ClineContextSyncOptions.Create` — clamped the context written to Cline's models.json
- `DeepSeekHarnessSetupOptions.Create` — clamped the context written to Harness settings.yaml
- `MainViewModel` validation check — rejected context > 262144
- `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator.KeepHandTunedContext` — treated ≥ 262144 as "already at max"

Even though `LocalVariantContextMax` allowed up to 1M when YaRN was enabled, the backend clamped everything to 262144.

**Fix:** Added `MaxLocalContextWindowWithYarn = 1048576` constant. All clamping sites now accept an optional `maxContextWindow` parameter that defaults to the old 262144 cap. When YaRN extension is enabled (`LocalExtendContextIntoRam` / `LocalVariantExtendContextIntoRam`), the YaRN-aware cap (1M) is passed through:
- `FluxMuxRuntimeService.BuildLocalHardwareLaunchAdvice` / `GetLocalHardwareLaunchAdvice` — new `maxContextWindow` param
- `FluxMuxRuntimeService.ApplyRecommendedAutoLaunchSettings` — reads `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` from profile
- `FluxMuxRuntimeService.BuildLocalServerArgs` — reads `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` from profile
- `ClineContextSyncOptions.Create` / `DeepSeekHarnessSetupOptions.Create` — new `maxContextWindow` param
- `EndpointSettingsSnapshot` — new `MaxContextWindow` field
- `ClineEndpointAdapter` / `HarnessEndpointAdapter` — pass `snapshot.MaxContextWindow`
- `MainViewModel.BuildEndpointSettingsSnapshot` / `BuildHarnessSetupOptions` — pass YaRN-aware max
- `MainViewModel` validation check — uses YaRN-aware max
- `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator.KeepHandTunedContext` — new `maxContextWindow` param

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Launch timing: self-correcting estimates + 200s YaRN default (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** The terminal reports wrong cold load wait times for local VRAM models, and FluxMux does not seem to record faster previous launches to better estimate the next time. Only YaRN-based models should start with 200s launch times by default, and all model profiles should be self-correcting as to launch times.

**Root cause:**
- `GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs` capped the budget at 180s (3 min) — too low for YaRN models that can take 5+ minutes
- `StartLocalLaunchCountdownMonitor` used 180s default for long-load profiles — should be 200s
- `CountdownMs` only kept the old slowest when the sample window wasn't full — faster launches didn't update the estimate quickly enough

**Fix:**
1. **`GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs`** — default is now 200s (was 90s). No hard cap: the budget follows the recorded slowest launch + 30s margin, with a 60s floor. If the model genuinely takes 5 minutes, the budget reflects that.
2. **`StartLocalLaunchCountdownMonitor`** — default for long-load profiles is now 200s (was 180s).
3. **`CountdownMs`** — now always follows the most recent window's slowest, not the persisted value. If the model is launching faster now, the estimate drops; if slower, it rises. The persisted value is only used when there are no samples.
4. **`GetLocalLaunchEstimateMs`** — now returns `GetLocalLaunchWarmupBudgetMs` instead of the raw persisted slowest. This ensures the countdown UI shows the same "cold-load wait" value that the launch loop actually uses (recordedMs + 30s margin), so the second launch of a profile shows the updated estimate instead of the raw first-launch time.

**Tests:** Updated `Countdown_keeps_a_colder_mark_until_the_sample_window_fills_with_faster_launches` → `Countdown_follows_the_most_recent_window_slowest`. Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

## Cline context sync uses stale YaRN flag (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** After launching a YaRN profile (ctx 393216), the terminal reported "Cline 4.x Context Window was set to 262144" instead of 393216. Cline also never responds to the YaRN model if it is the first launched model.

**Root cause:** `BuildEndpointSettingsSnapshot` and `BuildHarnessSetupOptions` read `LocalVariantExtendContextIntoRam` (a UI property) to decide whether to pass the YaRN-aware max context (1M) to the Cline/Harness sync. However, when `RememberMountedLocal` fires right after a launch, the UI property has NOT yet been loaded from the profile settings (it's only loaded when the user navigates to the profile editor). So the sync passes `0` as `MaxContextWindow`, and `ClineContextSyncOptions.Create` clamps the context to the default 262144 cap.

**Fix:** Added `ResolveRunningProfileExtendContextIntoRam()` which reads the `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` flag directly from the running profile's settings on disk (via `GetLiveLocalProfileSettings`), falling back to the UI property when no profile is loaded. Both `BuildEndpointSettingsSnapshot` and `BuildHarnessSetupOptions` now use this method instead of the raw UI property.

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Two llama-server processes running at once (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** Two llama-server versions were running at the same time; user had to close both before relaunching.

**Root cause:** When switching to a *different variant* of the same model, `LaunchLocalAsync` kills the tracked `_localServerProcess` and waits for the port to close. But if a second managed llama-server was already holding the daemon port (rapid re-launch spawned a new process before the previous fully exited, or the tracked reference was stale), only the tracked one was killed — the port owner was left alive, so a third server could start while two were still up.

**Fix:** After killing the tracked process and waiting for the port, `LaunchLocalAsync` now also sweeps the daemon-port owner by PID (`TryStopLocalEndpointOwnerByPort`) if the port is still open, then waits again. This guarantees no second managed llama-server lingers before a new one starts.

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## False "Context is too small" block when the bar shows half full (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** After launching a YaRN profile, the terminal immediately reported "the loaded model profile's Context is too small" even though the Context bar showed only ~half full. The chat history was existing (resumed session).

**Root cause:** `PromptExceedsContext` always used the pessimistic `chars/4` estimate (`EstimatePromptTokens`), even when FluxMux had already measured the **real** prompt token count from llama-server's `input_tokens` endpoint (`LocalPromptTokenCounter.CountAsync`). For dense tokenizers (Qwen, ~3-4 chars/token), the estimate over-counts, so a prompt that's genuinely ~half the window (per the real count) still tripped the estimate and got hard-blocked. The first term of the block check (`promptStd + maxTokens > hotContext`) correctly used the measured count, but the second term (`PromptExceedsContext`) did not.

**Fix:** `PromptExceedsContext` now accepts an optional `measuredPromptTokens` parameter and prefers it over the estimate when available. All call sites that have the measured count (from `LocalPromptTokenCounter.CountAsync`) now pass it through:
- `FluxMuxGatewayRouting.EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext` (both image and non-image paths)
- `FluxMuxGatewayRouting.PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact` (both image and non-image paths)
- `FluxMuxGatewayHost` line 786 (`exceed_context_size_error` path)
- `FluxMuxGatewayHost` line 2055 (`TryApplyForwardCompact`)
- `FluxMuxGatewayHost.OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded` (new `measuredPromptTokens` param; call sites at lines 675 and 723 pass it)

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Hang error no longer blames Port rules when Port rules are off (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** When a model switch caused a first-byte timeout, the error said "Port rule Max think first-byte hit 72s quiet" even though Port rules were not enabled. The hang detection is a safety check that runs regardless of Port rules, but the error message incorrectly blamed "Port rule" settings.

**Root cause:** `PortRulesPostMortem.FormatHang` always used `StoppedWhenRuleHit` which prefixes "Port rule" to the setting name. Unlike `FormatContextOverflow` (which already had a `portRulesActive` flag), `FormatHang` had no way to distinguish between "Port rule tripped" and "safety check tripped."

**Fix:** Added `portRulesActive` parameter to `FormatHang` (default `true`). When `false`, the message says "This is a safety check that runs even when Port rules off" and does not prefix "Port rule" to the setting name. `FluxMuxGatewayHost` now passes `portRules.Enabled` into `FormatHang`.

**Tests:** Full suite: **1592 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## Context-overflow message no longer blames Port rules + "barely used" no longer blocks (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** Two related problems. (1) The context-overflow error ("This chat turn cannot continue: this turn was too large for the loaded Context…") named **Port rule Headroom / Watermark %** and "Servers → Advanced: Port rules" even though the block that fired is a *safety check* (`ShouldBlockLocalForward` → `PromptFillsLocalContext`) that runs regardless of Port rules — and the user had explicitly set compaction to "None / endpoint managed". (2) The gateway declared an error state when context was barely used (~20k of 262k).

**Root cause:**
- The overflow post-mortem (`PortRulesPostMortem.FormatContextOverflow`) was written when context-overflow *was* a Port-rules compaction failure, so it unconditionally emitted Port-rule-flavored text. The 10/11 wording fix only softened the Headroom sentence; it did not remove the Port-rule framing.
- The blocking decision (`FluxMuxGatewayRouting.EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext`) uses the 4-chars/token estimate + reply budget. For dense tokenizers (Qwen, ~3–4 chars/token) `chars/4` over-counts, so a prompt well under the window can read as "prompt + reply budget > window" and get hard-blocked. "None" mode disables *compaction* but not this *blocking* gate.

**Fix:**
1. **`PortRulesPostMortem.FormatContextOverflow`** now takes a `portRulesActive` flag (default `true`). When Port rules are off / compaction mode is "None", it emits a clean message that just states the cause and the levers — "this turn was too large for the loaded Context — the prompt already fills the window." + launch-higher-Context + smaller-chat advice — and never mentions Port rules (no "Port rule Headroom/Watermark %", no "Servers → Advanced: Port rules"). When Port rules are on, the original Port-rule wording is preserved.
2. **`FluxMuxGatewayHost`** passes `portRules.Enabled` into both `FormatContextOverflow` call sites (the pre-forward `local_context_filling` block at ~:599 and the `exceed_context_size_error` overflow at ~:790).
3. **`FluxMuxGatewayRouting.EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext`** adds a "barely used" guard: never block when the standard 4-char estimate is ≤ 70% of the window (`promptStd * 10 <= hotContext * 7`). Genuine overflows (estimate near/over the window) still block; if the prompt truly doesn't fit, llama-server reports a real `exceed_context_size_error`.

**Tests:** 4 new tests — `Context_overflow_with_port_rules_off_does_not_name_a_Port_rule`, `Context_overflow_with_port_rules_on_still_names_the_Port_rule`, `Prompt_barely_using_the_window_is_never_blocked_even_with_a_large_reply_budget`, `Prompt_near_the_window_still_blocks_despite_the_barely_used_guard`. Full suite: **1587 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

**Note (deeper root cause, NOT addressed here):** the blocking/compaction thresholds are still computed from the chars/4 *estimate*, not the model's real token count. The "barely used" guard stops the false blocks, but the proper long-term fix is to use llama-server's actual `usage.prompt_tokens` / `/v1/tokenize` for pre-flight sizing and adaptive keep-size (so per-profile compaction converges instead of oscillating). Tracked as a follow-up.

## Gateway now reads the model's real prompt token count (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** "can you not just read the token count?" — the blocking/compaction decisions were still using the chars/4 *estimate*, which over-counts for dense tokenizers (Qwen) and causes false "context full" blocks + oscillating compaction.

**Fix:** The gateway now reads the model's **real** prompt token count from llama-server's `POST /v1/chat/completions/input_tokens` endpoint (accepts the exact chat-completion body, returns `{"input_tokens": N}` after the model's chat template is applied). This is the ground-truth count for the exact prompt we forward.
- **New `Services/LocalPromptTokenCounter.cs`** — `CountAsync(localPort, payload, model)` returns the real count or `null` on any failure (endpoint missing, server busy, no messages). Callers treat `null` as "unknown" and fall back to the estimate. 10s timeout; never fails the turn.
- **`FluxMuxGatewayRouting`** — `PromptFillsLocalContext` / `EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext` / `PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact` / `ShouldBlockLocalForward` accept an optional `measuredPromptTokens` and use it instead of the chars/4 estimate when available. The "barely used" guard now only applies to the estimate path (the real count is accurate, so no guard needed).
- **`FluxMuxGatewayHost`** — in the local route, after the payload is finalized (`PrepareLocalChatPayloadAsync` + overlay + compaction), it measures the real count once and passes it to the compact trigger (`TryApplyForwardCompact`) and the blocking check. The local "context filling" block was **moved** from before payload-finalization to after it, so it measures the *actual* forwarded prompt. Logs "local prompt tokens (measured): N (estimate M)".

**Behavior:** Dense-tokenizer prompts are no longer blocked/compacted on an over-count; the real count is authoritative. When the endpoint is unavailable (older llama-server, server busy), it falls back to the estimate + barely-used guard (the prior behavior). Per-profile compaction now triggers on the real count, so it converges instead of oscillating.

**Not hard-wired:** the count is read live from the *currently-loaded* model's own tokenizer — `local_port` (the launched model's port) and `local_preferred_model` (the active model/variant) both change per profile, so switching profiles automatically yields the correct count. No token number is baked in; the only constants are the 10s HTTP timeout and the chars/4 fallback estimate (used only when the endpoint is unavailable).

**Tests:** 2 new routing tests — `Measured_token_count_overrides_the_estimate_for_blocking`, `Measured_token_count_near_window_still_blocks`. Updated `AutoCompactModeNoneTests` reflection helper for the new `TryApplyForwardCompact` signature. Full suite: **1589 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

**Caveat:** the `input_tokens` endpoint requires a recent llama-server (added for OpenAI Responses API parity). On older builds the probe returns `null` and the gateway falls back to the estimate — so this is a strict improvement, never a regression.

## build-fluxmux.bat: reliable app termination + stats on every exit path (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** `build-fluxmux.bat` "fails to terminate the running FluxMux app (PID 36544) and exits without writing build stats." Two separate bugs.

**Root cause:**
- **Kill:** the app is launched by the VS Code "Start AI-FluxMux (Debug)" task (`run_orchestrator.cmd` → `start-ai-fluxmux.cmd`) or the taskbar shortcut. The script killed it **once**, then only *observed* it for 10s. The app's background-cleanup can linger past that, and if the build was run right after the task launched the app the launching task may still be settling — so the verify loop aborted with "still running" even though `taskkill` *could* have killed it.
- **Stats:** the result log (`build-fluxmux-result.txt`) was only written on the **success** path (`:done`). On a **failed** build (main app or source projects) the script did `pause` + `exit /b 1` **without** writing the log. Worse, `%BUILDLOG%` was **never set**, so the post-build MSB3027/MSB3021 copy-error scan silently read an empty path and missed copy failures.

**Fix (in `c:\AI_Workbench\Workspace\build-fluxmux.bat`):**
1. **Terminate everything that locks the build output** (the user's ask: "the rebuilt bat should terminate everything"). A new comprehensive PowerShell block runs *before* the build and kills **all** lock-holders, then **prints what it killed**:
   - The running app (`FluxMux.Avalonia.exe`) — the primary holder. It's launched by the VS Code "Start AI-FluxMux (Debug)" task / taskbar shortcut, whose terminal panel stays open, so the app is still alive when the build runs.
   - `VBCSCompiler.exe` (the C# compiler server, respawns on every build).
   - `MSBuild.exe` + the MSBuild node-reuse worker (`dotnet.exe` hosting `MSBuild.dll /nodemode:1`).
   - **Any other process that has a FluxMux module loaded** (a stray host, a 2nd instance, a debugger attach) — found by scanning every process's loaded modules. The editor (Code/Cursor) is explicitly skipped.
   - The old early `taskkill VBCSCompiler/MSBuild` lines were removed (now covered by this block); the Python kill is kept (source-file locks, different purpose).
   - Verified the detection logic in isolation (dry-run, no kill): it correctly found the live app (PID 38156) + VBCSCompiler (PID 33816). Output is deduped so the report is clean.
2. **Robust app-kill verify loop:** after the comprehensive kill, the verify loop **re-kills** `FluxMux.Avalonia.exe` on *every* poll and waits up to **30s** (was 10s), so a lingering process is actively stopped, not just watched. The abort message names the real relauncher (the VS Code task / taskbar shortcut) and tells the user to press the red STOP button on that task panel.
3. **Stats on every exit path:**
   - `BUILDLOG` is now set (`%ROOT%\build-fluxmux-main.log`) and the main-app `dotnet build` output is redirected to it, so the MSB3027/MSB3021 copy-error scan actually reads the build output.
   - The main-app **failure** path now prints the last 25 lines of the build log and writes a `BUILD FAILED` result log (with the log tail) before `pause`/`exit`.
   - The two **source-project** failure paths call a new `:WriteFailLog` subroutine that writes a `BUILD FAILED` result log.
   - The **app-still-alive** abort path already wrote a log; now consistent with the others.

**Why stale DLLs "never happened a few days ago":** the launch flow (`start-ai-fluxmux.cmd`, `run_orchestrator.cmd`) is unchanged since 9/13. The difference is the app is now launched via the VS Code task whose panel stays open, so the app (the sole DLL lock holder — confirmed: VBCSCompiler has *zero* FluxMux modules loaded) is still alive when the build runs. The comprehensive kill now handles this.

**Verified:** batch structure checked — all labels defined, all `goto` targets resolve, paren balance correct (the only "imbalances" are in `rem` comment lines, which are not parsed). The comprehensive-kill PowerShell is a **single-line** command with exactly 2 double-quotes (all embedded quotes are single-quotes, which cmd.exe treats as literal), so it does not break the batch parser. NOT run end-to-end because the running app must not be killed (it serves this chat).

**Fix (batch parser crash):** the first version of the comprehensive kill used a **multi-line** `powershell -NoProfile -Command "..."` block. cmd.exe cannot parse a multi-line double-quoted string with embedded `$` / `(` / `)` / `{` / `}` / `|` / `&` characters — the script died instantly. Fixed by collapsing the kill logic to a **single physical line** (all embedded quotes are single-quotes, which are safe inside cmd's outer double-quoted string). Verified: the line has exactly 2 double-quotes and the PowerShell syntax is valid.

**Full list of batch-parser bugs fixed (the script now runs end-to-end and always pauses):**
1. Multi-line `powershell -Command` block → single line.
2. `%` not escaped in `ForEach-Object` → doubled to `%%`.
3. Single-line `if (...) else (...)` → split onto separate lines.
4. `\"` embedded quotes in the MSBuild-node kill → module-scan approach (no embedded double-quotes).
5. PowerShell with `|`/`&` inside an `if` block → extracted to a `:KillCompiler` subroutine.
6. `for /f` + backtick + complex PowerShell for SHA256 → `certutil -hashfile` writing to a temp file, read with `for /f "usebackq skip=1"`.
7. **Unescaped parentheses in `echo` lines inside `if (...)` blocks** — the final, most insidious bug. cmd's block parser treats `(` / `)` in echo text as block delimiters, aborting with ". was unexpected at this time." Fixed by replacing `(obj)` / `(the app was still running)` / `(stale DLLs possible)` / `(copy may have been blocked)` / `(source ... bytes, target ... bytes)` with `[...]` brackets in 5 echo lines (the freshness-check if-block, the `:done` result if-blocks, and `:CheckStale`).

**Verified end-to-end:** the script now runs to completion — kills lock-holders, verifies the app is dead, builds source + main app, kills compiler servers, verifies DLLs (copy step OK), runs the certutil freshness check (FRESH), writes `build-fluxmux-result.txt`, and **pauses for a keypress**. Every exit path (success, each failure, clean, and the end-of-file safety net) has a `pause`, so the window never closes without the user seeing the result.

**Deploy state:** the gateway real-token-count fix is still compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed. To deploy: stop the VS Code "Start AI-FluxMux (Debug)" task + close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. After deploy, confirm the log line `local prompt tokens (measured): N (estimate M)` appears (proves llama-server supports the endpoint).

## App now reliably terminates on window close (exit path moved off the UI dispatcher) (this session, 10/12)

**Issue (user report):** "fluxmux is supposed to clean up on exit by itself" — the process lingers **minutes** after the window is closed, still holding the build-output DLLs, so the build script has to brute-force kill it.

**Root cause:** the exit path was `OnWindowClosing` → `_ = FinishExitCleanupAsync(vm)` (a fire-and-forget `async Task`). That method's final `Environment.Exit(0)` lived in the `await` continuation, which is posted back to the **UI dispatcher**. When the window closes, Avalonia's desktop lifetime is shutting down and the UI dispatcher stops pumping new work items — so the continuation (and with it `Environment.Exit(0)`) can be **dropped or delayed indefinitely**. The process is then kept alive by its background threads (gateway, llama-server monitor, timers) for minutes, holding the DLLs.

**Fix (in `Views/MainWindow.axaml.cs`):** `OnWindowClosing` now calls `StartExitCleanupOnDedicatedThread(vm)`, which starts a **dedicated, non-UI thread** that:
1. Runs `vm.ShutdownManagedRuntimesForExit()` off the UI thread, capped at 8s (`Task.WhenAny(cleanup, Task.Delay(8000)).Wait()`).
2. Stops the help file watchers.
3. Calls `Environment.Exit(0)` — the hard termination.

Because this runs on a dedicated thread (not the UI dispatcher), the cleanup and the exit **always run** even while the UI dispatcher is shutting down. The 8s cap means a hung llama-server/gateway shutdown can't keep the app alive; we exit anyway (a lingering llama-server is better than a lingering app that locks the DLLs — the next launch reclaims the port). The old `FinishExitCleanupAsync` method was removed.

**Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors** (33 pre-existing warnings, unrelated). NOT deployed to the running app.

**Deploy state:** compiled/tested in `artifacts\testrun\`, NOT deployed. To deploy: stop the VS Code "Start AI-FluxMux (Debug)" task + close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. After deploy, closing the window should terminate the process within ~8s (no more minutes-long lingering), so the build script's kill becomes a rarely-needed safety net rather than the primary mechanism.

This reuses the same green/amber/red color language the user already sees on the "Validate to endpoint" status, so the feedback is consistent with the existing UI.

## Port-rules compaction now respects per-profile autocompact mode (this session, 10/12)

**Issue:** Even when the port-rules tickbox was enabled in the Servers tab, port-rules compaction would fire even when a model profile had its autocompact mode set to "Profile-specific" (`per-profile`) or "None / endpoint managed" (`none`). The user expected that when a profile uses profile-specific compaction settings, the port-rules fallback should not also fire.

**Root cause:** In `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (`TryApplyForwardCompact`), the wizard-controlled auto-compaction path (lines 1895-1960) and the port-rules compaction path (lines 1962+) were independent. When the per-profile mode was `"per-profile"`, the wizard path used the profile-specific settings, but if the wizard path didn't compact (e.g., because the prompt was below the trigger threshold), execution would fall through to the port-rules path, which could still compact if `rules.CompactEnabled` was true.

**Fix:** Hoisted the `perProfile` and `perProfileMode` variables to the function scope (lines 1870-1878). Added a check at line 1966: `if (!rules.CompactEnabled || fullFidelityActive || perProfileMode == "per-profile") return false;`. This ensures that when the per-profile autocompact mode is `"per-profile"`, the port-rules compaction path is skipped. The `"none"` mode was already handled correctly (line 1890: `if (!perProfile.HasValue || perProfileMode == "none") return false;`).

**Behavior:**
- Mode = `"none"`: No compaction at all (wizard or port-rules). ✓ (Already correct)
- Mode = `"per-profile"`: Only the wizard path compacts (using profile-specific settings). Port-rules path is skipped. ✓ (Fixed)
- Mode = `"port-rules"`: Both wizard and port-rules paths can compact. ✓ (Unchanged)

**Tests:** Full test suite → **1583 passing, 0 failing**.

**Deploy state:** Compiled/tested in separate output dir (`artifacts\testrun\`). NOT deployed to the running app. To deploy: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

## GitHub release v0.3.0-beta (published 10/10)

**Published:** `https://github.com/ArchPrime/AI-FluxMux/releases/tag/v0.3.0-beta` — now listed as **Latest**.
- **Asset:** `AI-FluxMux-0.3.0-beta.zip` (self-contained win-x64, from `artifacts\publish\`).
- **Tag:** `v0.3.0-beta` → `main` @ `1f42c78` (0.3.0 beta).
- **Notes:** version-bump release; install steps + port `http://127.0.0.1:5001` + "do not point Client app at llama-server" + "do not unzip dev config/secrets".
- **Note:** the `publish-release.bat` zip is named `AI-FluxMux-<version>-win-x64-portable.zip`; the release asset here is `AI-FluxMux-0.3.0-beta.zip` (renamed for the release). Both are the same self-contained build.

### Release-notes correction (10/10)

The first draft of the release body listed **0.2-era features** (local GGUF launch, model profile editor, auto-detect llama-server, local model readiness, one Port for local and cloud, route requests, compact and omit, context budget awareness, in-app Help) under "What's new since 0.2.2" — but those were already shipped in 0.2.2, so they were not new in 0.3.

**Fixed:** the release body (`C:\Users\paulk\AppData\Local\Temp\fluxmux_release_body.md`) now splits into two sections:
- **"What's new since 0.2.2"** — only items that actually landed after the 0.2.2 tag: **Port rules (turn supervision)**, **Diagnostics footer**, and **version single-sourcing** (`FluxMuxAppInfo`).
- **"What's in 0.2 (already shipped)"** — the 0.2-era features, listed for reference but explicitly marked as unchanged in 0.3.

**Trap to remember:** when writing release notes for a version bump, only list features that landed *after the previous tag*. The 0.3.0 bump commit (`1f42c78`) was a version-bump + Diagnostics-footer change; the local-model / routing / compact features predate it. Check `git log v0.2.2..main` (or the tag range) to confirm what is genuinely new before drafting.

## publish-release.bat (added 10/10)

New `publish-release.bat` at the project root — one-click portable release build. **Compiled/validated (syntax + guard logic verified), NOT YET RUN** (the app is running, so step 1 correctly refuses; the user will close the app and run it).

- **What it does (4 steps):**
  1. Refuses to run if `FluxMux.Avalonia.exe` is running (prevents the locked-DLL failure; does NOT taskkill).
  2. Builds `FluxMux.Updates` (Release) → `FluxMux.Updates.exe` (the standalone updater).
  3. Publishes the main app (`-c Release -r win-x64 --self-contained`) → `artifacts\publish\`.
  4. Zips `artifacts\publish\` → `AI-FluxMux-<version>-win-x64-portable.zip` (version read from `FluxMuxAppInfo.Version`).
- **Output:** `c:\AI_Workbench\Workspace\FluxMux.Avalonia\AI-FluxMux-0.3.0-beta-win-x64-portable.zip` (self-contained, no .NET install needed).
- **Safety:** writes `artifacts\publish\.publishing.lock` before publishing and removes it on every exit path (success + all 3 failure paths). The `artifacts\publish\` dir is git-ignored (regenerable).
- **To run:** close the app → double-click `publish-release.bat` (or run from a terminal). The user confirms the app is closed before running.
- **Verified:** parens balanced (19/19), all 4 steps present, lock cleanup on all 4 paths, step-1 guard correctly detects the running app.
