## Refactor plan: split the god files into logical modules (updated 10/10)

**Goal:** break the oversized files into logical modules for maintainability, with **zero behavioral change** — every step is a pure move/reorganization verified by the test suite.

### Target files (current sizes, re-measured 10/10)

| File | Lines | Key fact that shapes the approach |
|------|-------|-----------------------------------|
| `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` | 18,670 | **already a `partial class`**, properties already `partial` (MVVM source-gen). Constructed in **one place** (`App.axaml.cs:93`). → split is a pure member-move, zero API change. |
| `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` | 8,320 | `sealed class` (NOT partial) + 4 top-level records. Must add `partial` first. |
| `Views/MainWindow.axaml` | 3,174 | 6 clean `TabItem` boundaries (cut map below). |
| `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` | 3,139 | `sealed class : IDisposable` (NOT partial). Must add `partial` first. |

**Safety net:** 90 test files / **753 test methods**. All build+test uses the **separate-output** workflow (per AGENTS.md) so the running app's locked `bin\` is never touched:
```
dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests\FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests.csproj -c Debug -p:BaseOutputPath=artifacts\testrun\ -p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath=artifacts\testrun\obj\
dotnet test  FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests\FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests.csproj -c Debug --no-build -p:BaseOutputPath=artifacts\testrun\ -p:BaseIntermediateOutputPath=artifacts\testrun\obj\
```

### Ordering (lowest-risk first; one commit each; tests green after each)

**Phase 1 — `MainWindow.axaml` → per-tab UserControls** (lowest risk, no logic change).
Cut map (current line ranges): Quick Select 1020–1264 · Servers 1266–1903 · Model Profiles 1905–2252 · Live-Switch Testing 2254–2330 · Environment 2332–2621 · Help 2623–2673. Each tab's content becomes `Views/Tabs/<Name>Tab.axaml` (+ `.axaml.cs`), still bound to `MainViewModel`. `MainWindow.axaml` keeps the `TabStrip`+`TabControl` shell and hosts the 6 UserControls. **Proof of concept: Model Profiles tab first.**

**Phase 2 — `MainViewModel.cs` → partial files by feature area** (biggest win).
Because it's already `partial`, this is a pure member move into `ViewModels/MainViewModel.<Area>.cs`:
- `.QuickSelect.cs` — quick-select slots, selection editors, route types
- `.LocalProfiles.cs` — local profiles, variants, YaRN, KV-cache, auto-compact
- `.CloudProfiles.cs` — cloud providers/profiles/variants
- `.Servers.cs` — local server discovery/install, ports, runtime state
- `.Gateway.cs` — gateway/routing/port-rules state
- `.Updates.cs` — update checks, version, diagnostics
- `.Core.cs` — constructor, lifecycle (NoteUserInterfaceActivity, ShutdownManagedRuntimesForExit), shared fields

Move members verbatim, **no signature changes**. Keep it a single `partial class` (bindings rely on one DataContext type).

**Phase 3 — `FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` / `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` → partial files.**
Add `partial` to the class, then split by concern (runtime: launch/stop, idle-timeout, memory-guard, telemetry; gateway: host lifecycle, routing, request handling). Keep the 4 top-level records in a `FluxMuxRuntimeService.Types.cs`. Lower priority than Phases 1–2.

### Invariants / do-NOTs
- **No behavioral change** in any phase — if a test's expectation changes, stop and investigate; it's a bug, not a refactor.
- **Never close the running app to build/test** — use the separate-output workflow above.
- **One file/area per commit**, tests green before the next. Keep `MainViewModel` a single `partial class` (don't fragment into many classes — bindings rely on one DataContext type).
- Deploy to the running app is a **user action** (close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart); record deploy state here.

**Status:** Plan updated 10/10. Not started. Awaiting go-ahead to begin **Phase 1 (Model Profiles tab)** as proof of concept.

**⚠️ UI regression reported (this session, 9/10):** User reports the UI has "significantly regressed" and is "reversing about a day's worth of updates." The running app (PID 15808) started 9/10 7:00 PM — it's running a **stale build** from yesterday. The current source has many uncommitted changes (see `git status`). **Before starting the file-split refactor, the user should deploy the current source** (close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart) to confirm the regression is just a stale-build issue and not a source-code problem. If the regression persists after a fresh deploy, it's a source-code issue that needs investigation before any refactoring.

**Root cause of recurring regressions (this session, 9/10):** The user suspects "an ongoing confusion between various net10 repositories." Investigation confirmed:
- The main app's `bin\Debug\net10.0\` has DLLs from **9/10** (stale).
- The current source has commits from **9/10** (YaRN, document context, port rules, etc.).
- The build script (`build-fluxmux.bat`) is supposed to copy the latest DLLs to the main app's bin, but it's not working reliably due to file locks and the net10 confusion.
- There's also a **backup directory** (`FluxMux.Avalonia_backup_20261009_155219`) with even older DLLs (5/10), which could accidentally be used if the build/deploy process is misconfigured.

**Fix:**
1. **Commit the current changes** (done: commit `916534f`).
2. **Close the app** (manually).
3. **Run a clean build**: `build-fluxmux.bat clean`.
4. **Manually copy the latest DLLs** to the main app's bin (if the build script's auto-repair fails):
   ```
   copy /Y FluxMux.Updates.Core\bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll bin\Debug\net10.0\
   copy /Y FluxMux.Updates\bin\Debug\net10.0-windows\FluxMux.Updates.dll bin\Debug\net10.0\
   ```
5. **Verify the DLLs in the main app's bin are from the latest build** (check the timestamps — they should be from 9/10, not 7:09 PM).
6. **Restart the app**.

If the regression persists after a fresh deploy, it's a source-code issue that needs investigation.

**⚠️ CONFIRMED: source-code regression from partial rollback (this session, 9/10):** After a fresh deploy (app restarted 7:19 PM, DLLs manually copied), the regressions **persisted** — proving this is a **source-code problem**, not a stale-build problem. Interrogating the 3:52 PM backup (`FluxMux.Avalonia_backup_20261009_155253\MainWindow.axaml`, 3216 lines) against the current `Views\MainWindow.axaml` (3199 lines) revealed the current source is in a **mixed/inconsistent state** — some features added *after* 3:52 PM are present, but features that *were* there at 3:52 PM have been lost:

| Feature | 3:52 PM backup | Current (before fix) |
|---------|:---:|:---:|
| "How far:" label before YaRN dropdown | ✗ | ✓ (newer) |
| YaRN dropdown MinWidth/MaxWidth | ✗ | ✓ (newer) |
| **CUDA minor picker** (`LlamaCudaMinorPickerVisible`) | ✓ | ✗ **LOST** |
| "Pin user:" `IsVisible` gate (per-profile only) | ✓ | ✗ **LOST** (always visible) |

**Fixed this session:**
- **CUDA minor picker restored** (commit `fb70fce`): Re-inserted the `LlamaCudaMinorPickerVisible` StackPanel (CUDA minor label + ComboBox) into `Views\MainWindow.axaml` after the `LlamaServerUpdateStatusText` TextBlock. All three ViewModel properties (`LlamaCudaMinor`, `LlamaCudaMinorPickerVisible`, `LlamaCudaMinorChoices`) already exist (lines 4901-4908), so the binding works. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Model Profiles: IsVisible gates restored** (commit `30c6206`): The "Keep turns:", "Tool results:", and "Pin user:" controls in the Context management section lost their `IsVisible="{Binding LocalAutoCompactMode, Converter={x:Static vm:StringEqualsConverter.Instance}, ConverterParameter=per-profile}"` gates. These gates ensure the controls only appear when the auto-compact mode is set to "per-profile". Without them, the controls were always visible (a regression). Restored all three gates. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Model Profiles: Auto-compact mode selector restored** (commit `d58dd97`): The `ComboBox` for selecting the auto-compact mode (`LocalAutoCompactModeOptions` / `SelectedLocalAutoCompactModeOption`) was completely missing from the current source. Without it, the user could not change the auto-compact mode, and the `IsVisible` gates for "Keep turns:", "Tool results:", and "Pin user:" would keep those controls hidden. Restored the ComboBox between the "Auto-compact" checkbox and the "Trigger:" control. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **KV cache dropdown binding fixed** (commit `f0c8851`): The `SelectedItem` was bound to `LocalKvCacheWizardChoice` (a string) instead of `SelectedKvCacheWizardOption` (a `KvCacheWizardOption?` object). This type mismatch broke the dropdown, preventing the "Auto → q8" display from showing correctly. Fixed the binding to `SelectedKvCacheWizardOption` and added `MinWidth="130"` / `MaxWidth="420"` to match the YaRN context dropdown width. The ViewModel already appends the resolved value to the "Auto" option (`Display = $"Auto → {autoResolved}"`), so the dropdown now correctly shows e.g. "Auto → q8". Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **KV cache statistics fixed** (commit `ce8f9c7`): The model statistics (`LocalVariantFootprintText`) were showing the stale profile default (`LocalVariantKvCacheTypeK`) when "Auto" was selected in the KV cache dropdown, instead of the type that "Auto" actually resolves to. Added a new property `LocalKvCacheAutoResolvedType` that stores the resolved type (set in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`), and updated the footprint text logic to use it when the choice is "Auto". Now the statistics show e.g. "KV cache: q8_0" (the resolved type) instead of the stale profile default. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Auto-compact tickbox bidirectional sync** (commit `6cb0f6a`): The wizard "Auto-compact" checkbox (`LocalAutoCompactEnabled`) and the detailed Port rules "Compact" checkbox (`PortRules.CompactEnabled`) were independent — checking one did not update the other. Added bidirectional synchronization with a re-entrancy guard (`_isSyncingAutoCompact`): `OnLocalAutoCompactEnabledChanged` updates `PortRules.CompactEnabled`, and `OnPortRulesPropertyChanged` (subscribed in the constructor) updates `LocalAutoCompactEnabled` when `CompactEnabled` changes. Now the two tickboxes stay in sync. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Auto-compact sync gated on mode** (commit `aeda4f9`): The bidirectional sync between wizard and PortRules compact settings is now gated on `LocalAutoCompactMode`. When mode = "port-rules", the wizard settings sync bidirectionally with `PortRules.*` (global). When mode = "per-profile" or "none", the wizard settings are profile-specific and do NOT sync to or from `PortRules.*` — they override the global PortRules for that profile. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Auto-compact sync direction fixed** (commit `f97814c`): Corrected the sync to be one-way only: PortRules → Wizard (adopt), NOT Wizard → PortRules. Port rules cannot be set from a local profile. When mode = "port-rules", the wizard adopts the PortRules values (PortRules changes update the wizard). When mode = "per-profile" or "none", the wizard settings are profile-specific and do not sync with PortRules at all. The `OnLocalAutoCompactEnabledChanged` handler no longer writes to `PortRules.CompactEnabled`. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Model Profiles: "How far:" label removed (this session, compiled clean — NOT DEPLOYED):** The `TextBlock Text="How far:"` before the YaRN "Extend context into RAM" dropdown was reinstated by the partial rollback. It pushed the dropdown out of its host column. Removed the label (the ComboBox already has `MinWidth="130"` / `MaxWidth="420"`). Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Model Profiles: YaRN "Auto" display uses `→` not `=` (this session, compiled clean — NOT DEPLOYED):** The "How far" dropdown's Auto option read `Auto=384K (...)` (equals sign) instead of `Auto → 384K (...)` (right arrow), inconsistent with the KV cache dropdown which correctly shows `Auto → q8_0`. Changed `BuildYarnMaxContextWizardOptions` line 5218 from `Auto=` to `Auto → `. Also updated the `YarnContextOption.Display` doc comment. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.
- **Model Profiles: Trigger row on its own line + per-profile gate (this session, compiled clean — NOT DEPLOYED):** The "Trigger:" row (NumericUpDown + %) was crammed into the same horizontal StackPanel as the auto-compact mode selector ComboBox, so it appeared in-line with the mode dropdown instead of on its own line like the three rows below (Keep turns / Tool results / Pin user). Split the mode selector into its own row and moved the Trigger row onto its own line, adding the `IsVisible="{Binding LocalAutoCompactMode, ...ConverterParameter=per-profile}"` gate so it only shows when Profile-specific is chosen (matching the other three rows). Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**. XML well-formedness confirmed.
- **Note:** The KV cache dropdown binding change (from `SelectedKvCacheWizardOption` to `LocalKvCacheWizardChoice`) is **intentional** (a later improvement), not a regression. Both properties exist in the ViewModel; the current single-string binding is correct.

**Root cause hypothesis:** The source got partially rolled back at some point today, scrambling the feature state. The 3:52 PM backup is a useful reference for what the Model Profiles / Servers UI looked like at that time. **Do NOT assume the current source is the latest state** — cross-check against the backup when a feature seems to have "disappeared."

## Build fix (this session)

- **KV cache statistics show wrong type when YaRN enabled — FIXED (this session, 9/10, compiled clean — NOT deployed):** The model-statistics text at the bottom of the Model Profiles wizard (`LocalVariantFootprintText`) showed the profile's saved KV cache type (`LocalVariantKvCacheTypeK`, defaults to "q8_0") instead of the wizard's current selection (`LocalKvCacheWizardChoice`). When YaRN is enabled and the user picks q6 in the wizard, the statistics still showed q8. **Fix:** when YaRN is on, the statistics now read `LocalKvCacheWizardChoice` first, falling back to `LocalVariantKvCacheTypeK` only if the wizard choice is empty or "Auto". Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.

- **Model Profiles tab: layout corruption — detailed settings rendered in wizard column — FIXED (this session, 9/10, compiled clean — NOT deployed):** In `Views/MainWindow.axaml` the "Context management" nested panel (line 400, indent 73) was closed by a `</StackPanel>` at indent 77 (line 453) instead of indent 73. This caused the outer wizard column (`Grid.Column="0"`, line 276, indent 64) to close too early (line 454, indent 68), so the footprint/telemetry text and VRAM/RAM warnings (lines 455-476) fell into the "Detailed settings" column (`Grid.Column="1"`) instead of staying in the wizard column. **Fix:** changed line 453's indent from 77 to 73 so it correctly closes the nested panel. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**.

- **Updater dialog: "Restart AI-FluxMux" button crowded/clipped the text above it — FIXED (this session, 9/10, compiled clean — NOT deployed):** In `FluxMux.Updates/UpdaterForm.cs` the content `FlowLayoutPanel` had a static `Padding(16,16,16,4)` (4px bottom) and the button `Panel` had `Padding(16,8,16,12)` (8px top), so the gap between the status/detail text and the action button was only ~12px — too tight, and the button's larger 11pt-bold font made it visually crowd/clip the text on high-DPI / large-font displays. **Fix:** replaced the static paddings with DPI-aware values derived from `SystemFonts.MessageBoxFont.Height` (gap = 0.75×line-height, side/top = 0.6×, bottom = 0.5×, each with a minimum floor). The content panel's bottom padding now carries the gap; the button panel's top padding is 0. All spacing scales with system DPI/font. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` of `FluxMux.Updates` → **0 errors**.

- **`build-fluxmux.bat` false-positive "STALE FluxMux.Updates.dll" — FIXED (this session, 9/10):** The script's `:CheckDll`/`:RepairDll` compared the main app's `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.dll` against `FluxMux.Updates\bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.dll` (22016 bytes, a **stale 5/10 leftover**). But `FluxMux.Updates` targets **`net10.0-windows`** (csproj line 4), so its real current output is `bin\Debug\net10.0-windows\FluxMux.Updates.dll` = **31744 bytes**, which is exactly what the main app's bin already holds. The "STALE" warning was a **false positive** (wrong source path), and the "auto-repair" would have *downgraded* the main app's DLL to the stale 22016 build. **Fix:** changed all three `FluxMux.Updates` source paths in the script (post-build echo, `:CheckDll`, `:RepairDll`) from `net10.0` → `net10.0-windows`. Verified: main app `FluxMux.Updates.dll` = 31744 = `net10.0-windows` source. **Do NOT** treat a "STALE FluxMux.Updates.dll" warning as a real problem — check the `net10.0-windows` source, not `net10.0`.

- **Environment tab update-message audit + consistency fixes** (this session, 9/10, compiled clean — NOT deployed): Audited every user-facing message across all stages (check-for-updates → update-now → standalone updater → recovery). Found and fixed: (1) **Deleted dead in-process `UpdateLlamaServerNowAsync`** (was not wired to any button — the live "Update now" button uses `UpdateLlamaServerNowViaUpdaterAsync`/standalone updater). Its confirm dialog described an in-app download/swap that the user never actually gets, so it was a misleading trap. Also removed its `CanUpdateLlamaServerNow` helper. (2) **Fixed circular "Click Update now" status** on the update path: `UpdateLlamaServerNowViaUpdaterAsync` re-runs the check and was showing the check result's trailing "Click Update now to back up and replace..." instruction *after* the user already clicked it. Added `TrimUpdateNowInstruction()` to strip that tail and append "Closing AI-FluxMux to run the updater...". (3) The two "folder not set" / "folder does not exist" messages were confirmed to map to two distinct conditions and are already consistent — no change needed. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.

- **Stale-DLL saga — CORRECTED root cause (this session, 9/10):** Earlier I wrongly concluded the "stale DLL" was a phantom (a stale leftover in the Core project's bin). That was **wrong** — I had been reading a bogus artifact from a separate-output-dir build that produced no DLL. A **true clean rebuild** of `FluxMux.Updates.Core` from current source produces **86016 bytes**, which is the correct current build. The main app's `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` is **84480 bytes (5/10) — genuinely stale**. The build script's stale detection was **correct all along**. The real blocker: **the running app (FluxMux.Avalonia.exe) holds a lock on the DLL**, so no copy can overwrite it while the app is alive — and the agent must NOT kill the app (it serves the chat). The build script's auto-repair "succeeds" in its log but the `copy` silently fails ("0 file(s) copied / used by another process"). **The only reliable fix is the user's manual deploy: close AI-FluxMux → `build-fluxmux.bat` → restart.** The script's pre-build `del` of the main app's DLLs (added this session) is still useful: it guarantees the post-build check reports a true "MISSING/STALE" if the build's copy is blocked, instead of a false "OK". Do NOT trust separate-output-dir builds for size verification of this project — they can produce no DLL or a stale one.

- **CRASH: `MissingMethodException` on `LlamaServerUpdateCheck.CheckAsync` (5-param)** (this session, 9/10): The "Update now" button (`UpdateLlamaServerNowViaUpdaterAsync`, line 6007) called `CheckAsync` **directly**, unlike the "Check for updates" path which already used reflection. With a stale Core DLL (4-param `CheckAsync`), the JIT threw `MissingMethodException` before any try-catch could catch it. **Fix:** the "Update now" path now uses the **same reflection probe** as the check path (5-param → fall back to 4-param → friendly error if neither). Both `CheckAsync` call sites are now reflection-guarded. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.

- **Outdated llama-server update advisory** (this session, 9/10): `GpuUpdateAdvice()` told the user to manually extract zips and check `nvidia-smi` for the CUDA minor, which is now handled automatically by the updater. **Fix:** simplified to "the updater will use the CUDA/Vulkan zip" per detected GPU. Compiled clean.

- **XAML tag mismatch in `MainWindow.axaml` fixed** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): The `StackPanel` opened at line 276 (left column of the local profile editor's `Grid`) was never closed before the `</Grid>` at line 682. Added the missing `</StackPanel>` between lines 681 and 682. Verified: XML parser → valid; separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.

- **CRASH: `MissingMethodException` on `UpdatePlan.LlamaArgs(string, string)`** (this session, 10/09): The running app's `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` was stale (Oct 5, 84480 bytes, 2-param `LlamaArgs`), while the new `FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` called the 3-param version. The `CheckAsync` call already used reflection (safe), but the two `LlamaArgs` calls at lines 6036/6065 were direct calls → crash. **Fix:** added `BuildLlamaArgsSafe()` helper that probes for the 3-param `LlamaArgs` via reflection and falls back to the 2-param version (or manual arg building) when the Core DLL is stale. Both call sites now use `BuildLlamaArgsSafe`. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.

- **Text regression in Environment tab** (this session): The update status text showed "minor version not in the folder name" because the stale Core DLL was loaded. This is the same root cause as the crash above — the old DLL's `LlamaServerFamilyFingerprint` produced a different summary. Will resolve once the new Core DLL is deployed.

- **KV cache "Auto" now shows its resolved type in the dropdown** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): The KV cache for extended context dropdown in the profile wizard showed "Auto" without revealing what it resolves to, unlike the YaRN context dropdown which shows `Auto=384K total (32K VRAM+352K RAM)`. **Fix:** (a) Created `KvCacheWizardOption` class (Value + Display, like `YarnContextOption`). (b) Changed `LocalKvCacheWizardOptions` from `ObservableCollection<string>` to `ObservableCollection<KvCacheWizardOption>`. (c) Added `SelectedKvCacheWizardOption` property bound to the ComboBox. (d) The "Auto" option's Display now shows `Auto → q8_0` (the resolved type). (e) Removed the redundant `LocalKvCacheWizardAutoResolvedText` TextBlock that was beside the dropdown. (f) ComboBox now stretches to fill the width (matching the YaRN context dropdown). Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.

- **CRASH: `MissingMethodException` on `LlamaServerUpdateResult.get_CudaMinorAmbiguous()`** (this session, 9/10, recurring): The app crashed when the user clicked "Check for updates" on the llama-server section. **Root cause (two-part):** (1) The running app's `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` was stale (84480 bytes, predates the CUDA-minor picker changes), while `FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` was rebuilt with the new code that calls `get_CudaMinorAmbiguous()`. (2) **The build couldn't copy the new Core DLL because VBCSCompiler (the C# compiler server, PID 32796) was holding a file lock on it** — even after all FluxMux processes were closed. VBCSCompiler keeps running in the background after a build and locks DLLs, preventing the next build from copying updated project-reference DLLs. **Fixes applied this session:** (a) `build-fluxmux.bat` now kills VBCSCompiler + MSBuild before building (`taskkill /F /IM VBCSCompiler.exe /T`), and adds a post-build verification that warns if the Core DLL size doesn't match (stale detection). (b) `MainViewModel.CheckForLlamaServerUpdatesAsync` now wraps the `CudaMinorAmbiguous` / `CudaMinors` / `preferredCudaMinor` accesses in try-catch for `MissingMethodException`, so a stale Core DLL degrades gracefully (no picker shown) instead of crashing. **Deploy:** close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. The VBCSCompiler kill in the build script should now allow the Core DLL to be copied correctly.

- **Removed dead `scripts/Update-LlamaServer.ps1`** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): The script was bundled via the csproj Content include but never called (the update is done entirely in C# via `LlamaServerUpdateService`). Removed the Content include from `FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` and deleted the script file + empty `scripts/` directory. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.
- **Added CUDA-minor picker for ambiguous llama-server updates** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): When a llama.cpp release ships multiple CUDA-minor zips (e.g. 12.4, 12.6, 13.0) and the installed family's minor is ambiguous, the "Update now" button was hidden. Now: (a) `LlamaServerUpdateResult` gained `CudaMinors` (list of available minors) + `CudaMinorAmbiguous` flag; (b) `LlamaCppReleaseMatcher.FindNewerMatching` gained a `preferredCudaMinor` param that resolves the ambiguity when a remembered choice is available; (c) `LlamaServerUpdateCheck.CheckAsync` passes the preferred minor through; (d) `MainViewModel` gained `LlamaCudaMinor` (persisted to config as `LlamaCudaMinor`), `LlamaCudaMinorPickerVisible`, and `LlamaCudaMinorChoices`; (e) `MainWindow.axaml` shows a CUDA-minor ComboBox when the picker is visible. The user picks a minor once; it's remembered for future checks. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.

- **Fixed the "Validate to endpoint" 502 retry loop for YaRN profiles** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): Validating a YaRN profile (context extended into RAM, so `local_vram_context < local_context`) looped forever with `public endpoint is not free yet ... Retrying. HTTP 502 Bad Gateway`. **Root cause (confirmed in `C:\AI_Workbench\Logs\Proxy_Cloud_Handshake.log`):** `gateway handler failed: The node already has a parent.` — a `System.Text.Json.Nodes.JsonException`. `LocalRequestOverlayRouting.DetectDominantDocument` (called on **every** local chat request via `OfferDocumentContextDialogIfNeeded`, `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:2165`) built a throwaway payload with `new JsonArray { msg }` where `msg` was the **live** node still parented to the original `messages` array. `JsonArray.Add` throws "The node already has a parent." The gateway's catch-all (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:327-332`) turned that into a 502 `proxy_error`, so the validation probe (`WaitUntilLocalPublicEndpointReadyAsync`) never succeeded and retried every 4 s for the full 5-minute CTS. **Fix:** `msg.DeepClone()` before adding to the throwaway array (`LocalRequestOverlayRouting.cs:284`). **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.
- **"Compile errors" were actually file-lock errors, not code errors** (this session, no source change): the user's `build-fluxmux.bat` reported "2 error(s)" — `MSB3027`/`MSB3021` "Could not copy `FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` to `bin\Debug\net10.0\`... locked by AI-FluxMux (33432)". The C# source had **0 `error CS####`** (only 25 pre-existing warnings: CS8604/CS8602 nullability, CS0219 unused var, CA1416 platform). The failure was purely the copy step: the running app (`FluxMux.Avalonia.exe`, PID 33432, confirmed running from `bin\Debug\net10.0\`) holds `FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` open. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` (`-p:BaseOutputPath=artifacts\testrun\`) → **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s)**. **Resolution (user action, agent must NOT close the app):** close the running app → `c:\AI_Workbench\Workspace\build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. No code fix needed.

- **Fixed the misleading "Port rule Headroom" post-mortem message** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): The post-mortem message in `MainViewModel.cs` (`SendLocalMessageCoreAsync`) said "Raising Port rule Headroom or Port rule Watermark % will not fix this turn" — which is **false**. Raising the headroom/watermark DOES prevent the overflow by triggering compaction earlier. The message also only appeared in the diagnostic terminal, not in the chat UI. **Fix:** rewrote the message to be accurate ("Raising Port rule Headroom or Port rule Watermark % may prevent this overflow by triggering compaction earlier") and added a user-facing `Log` line that also surfaces in the chat. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.
- **Fixed the red "■ Stop model" button not stopping an in-flight "Validate to endpoint"** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): `KillLocalModelNowAsync` (MainViewModel.cs:7370) called `_runtimeService.StopLocalAsync()` but did NOT cancel `_localValidateCts`, so the validation polling loop kept running until its 5-minute timeout even after the user clicked Stop. The Quick Select Stop button (`StopLocalRouteAsync`) already had the cancel (added in a prior session), but the diagnostic header bar's red Stop button (`KillLocalModelNowCommand` → `KillLocalModelNowAsync`) was missing it. **Fix:** added `_localValidateCts?.Cancel()` at the top of `KillLocalModelNowAsync`, matching `StopLocalRouteAsync`. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**.
- **Fixed a XAML build error (AVLN3000) in `Views/MainWindow.axaml` line 411.** The "Trigger:" `<StackPanel>` opening tag was missing its closing `>` (the `IsVisible="{Binding LocalAutoCompactMode, ...}"` attribute on the next line was orphaned), so the parser failed with "Unable to find suitable setter or adder for property Children". Closed the tag so `IsVisible` is a valid attribute of the same element. Verified: separate-output `dotnet build` → **0 errors**, and `<StackPanel>` tag balance is consistent (135 open = 127 close + 7 self-closing).
- **Silenced the CA1416 warning at `MainViewModel.cs:3934`** (`WindowsGpuPreference.PreferenceForLabel`) by adding an `if (!OperatingSystem.IsWindows()) return;` guard at the top of `ApplyDesktopGpuAsync`. `WindowsGpuPreference` is `[SupportedOSPlatform("windows")]` but `MainViewModel` targets all platforms. Note: a handful of *other* pre-existing CA1416 call sites remain (field initializers at 3793/3801, `RefreshDesktopGpuPreferenceFromRegistry`, `ApplyGpuAdapterResult`) — these are warnings only, not build errors; left untouched to keep the change minimal.
- **Added a crash logger that writes the full stack trace to the Desktop on any unhandled exception** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): new `CrashLogger.cs` (`internal static class CrashLogger.LogUnhandled(Exception, string)`) writes one timestamped `FluxMux_Crash_<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>.txt` to the user's Desktop (falls back to `%TEMP%` if the Desktop path is unavailable) containing the exception, full stack trace, and up to 10 inner exceptions. Wired into `App.axaml.cs` `OnFrameworkInitializationCompleted` for three paths: `Dispatcher.UIThread.UnhandledException` (UI thread), `AppDomain.CurrentDomain.UnhandledException`, and `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` (marked observed). **API note (verified against the installed `avalonia\12.1.1` XML docs, not GitHub):** the event is `Avalonia.Threading.Dispatcher.UnhandledException` — `Application` has **no** `DispatcherUnhandledException` member, so the correct wiring is `Dispatcher.UIThread.UnhandledException`. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` (`artifacts\testrun\`) → **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s)**.
- **Deploy:** NOT deployed — compiled/tested only. To get it live: close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.
- **Fixed the "not a benign crash" — unobserved `SocketException 995` from `IsTcpPortOpen`** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): `FluxMuxRuntimeService.IsTcpPortOpen` did `Task connectTask = tcpClient.ConnectAsync(host, port); return connectTask.Wait(250ms);`. When `Wait` timed out the connect task was left **unobserved**; disposing the `TcpClient` then aborted the in-flight connect, faulting the task with `SocketException 995` (operation aborted). The GC finalizer rethrew it as an unobserved `AggregateException` (`TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException`), crashing the app. **Fix:** replaced `connectTask.Wait(250ms)` with `Task.WaitAny(new[] { connectTask }, 250ms) == 0` — `WaitAny` observes the task, so a timeout/abort can no longer surface as an unobserved exception. Method stays synchronous `bool`, so all 16 call sites (incl. `if`/`&&` conditions) are unchanged. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` (`artifacts\testrun\`) → **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s)**.
- **Fixed the "None / endpoint managed" auto-compact mode being silently ignored when `local_context` is 0** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): In `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (`TryCompactLocalPayload`), the per-profile auto-compact settings lookup and the `modeNone` check were *inside* the `if (autoContext > 0)` gate. When `local_context` was 0 (e.g. YaRN not yet applied, or profile not yet loaded), the "None / endpoint managed" mode was never read, so the Port-rules fallback compaction could still fire — defeating the user's explicit opt-out. **Fix:** moved the `GetLocalAutoCompactSettings` lookup and the `modeNone` early-return *outside* the `autoContext > 0` gate, so "None" is honoured regardless of whether `local_context` is set. The per-profile trigger/keep settings still only apply when `autoContext > 0` (they need a context size to compute the threshold). **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` (`artifacts\testrun\`) → **0 errors**, full test suite **1575 passing**.
- **Fixed "None / endpoint managed" still applying Port-rules compaction when the profile can't be resolved** (this session, compiled clean, TESTED 4/4 new + 68/68 compaction/routing — NOT deployed): In `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (`TryApplyForwardCompact`), the `mode == "none"` early-return only fired when `GetLocalAutoCompactSettings` returned a **non-null** value. That callback returns `null` whenever it can't resolve the profile (no config, no `LocalProfiles`, or the `model::variant` key is missing). When that happened, execution fell through to the **Port-rules fallback** (gated only by `rules.CompactEnabled`, default `true`), so Port-rules compaction fired even though the user chose "None / endpoint managed". **Fix:** changed the gate to `if (perProfile is null || perProfile.Value.Mode == "none") return false;` — an unresolvable profile is now treated as "none" (hard opt-out) instead of falling back to Port-rules. **Tests:** new `AutoCompactModeNoneTests.cs` (4 tests via reflection on `TryApplyForwardCompact`): none-mode skips, unresolvable-profile skips, port-rules still compacts, per-profile compacts at trigger. All pass. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` + `dotnet test` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors; 68/68 pass**.
- **Recurring build lock: DEFINITIVE root cause = the running app itself (this session, script hardened + verified + freshness check added):** Confirmed via handle inspection that the **only** process locking `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` and `FluxMux.Updates.Core.dll` is the running app (it maps both DLLs in memory). NOT an editor, NOT VBCSCompiler, NOT MSBuild (those were all absent when the lock persisted). **Why "close → build" never stuck:** a .NET process holds its loaded DLLs for its entire lifetime, so if *any* `FluxMux.Avalonia.exe` is alive when the build's copy step runs, the copy fails (MSB3027/MSB3021) and leaves a stale DLL. The app can linger briefly after the window closes (background idle-timeout loop + `desktop.Exit` cleanup). **Fixes to `build-fluxmux.bat`:** (1) **verify-app-dead loop** after the `taskkill` — polls up to 10s and **aborts with a clear message** if the app is still alive; (2) **removed `taskkill /F /IM Code.exe`** — it was killing the VS Code editor hosting the Cline chat and the editor does not lock the output DLLs; (3) post-build VBCSCompiler + MSBuild node-reuse kills (PowerShell `Get-CimInstance`, since `wmic` is unavailable); (4) **NEW: freshness check** at the end of a successful build — `:CheckFresh` compares the SHA-256 hash of the just-compiled DLL (`obj\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll`) against the deployed DLL (`bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll`). MATCH = "FRESH: ... matches the just-built binary"; MISMATCH = a clear WARNING that the copy was blocked and the deployed binary is STALE. Uses .NET `SHA256` via PowerShell (works in both `powershell.exe` 5.1 and `pwsh` 7; `Get-FileHash` is NOT available in Windows PowerShell 5.1). Verified the subroutine logic via test `.bat` files (identical files → FRESH_OK=1; the multi-line `if (...)` block form was a batch-parsing bug, replaced with single-line `if`/`goto`). **User workflow:** close the app for real (window + tray), do NOT use the taskbar "Start AI-FluxMux" shortcut or VS Code "connect" task immediately before building (they restart the app), then run `build-fluxmux.bat` — it now confirms the app is dead before building AND confirms the fresh binary landed after building.
- **Fixed the stack-overflow crash in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo` sets `LocalKvCacheWizardChoice`, which fires `OnLocalKvCacheWizardChoiceChanged` → `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo` — infinite recursion → `StackOverflowException`. **Fix:** added a re-entrancy guard (`_isUpdatingLocalKvCacheWizard` bool field) at the top of the method; if already in progress, return immediately. Wrapped the body in `try/finally` to reset the flag. **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s)**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.
- **Added a visible "Auto → q4_1" badge to the KV cache wizard** (this session, compiled clean — NOT deployed): when the user selects "Auto" in the KV cache type dropdown, the resolved type was only shown inline in the info text ("Auto picks q4_1. ..."). **Fix:** added two new observable properties (`LocalKvCacheWizardAutoResolvedText`, `ShowLocalKvCacheWizardAutoResolved`) and a `TextBlock` in `MainWindow.axaml` that displays "Auto → q4_1" (or the resolved type) next to the dropdown. The badge is only visible when the choice is "Auto" (or empty, which resolves to Auto). **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet build` → **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s)**; full test suite → **1575 passing, 0 failing**.

## Known issues (reported 9/10)

User-reported defects. #1–#6 RESOLVED, #8 MITIGATED (memory guard) — all fixed 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED.
#7 (post-halt "retry" delay) is still open. All fixes require deploy: close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

1. **The "same 3 pre-existing context-compaction failures" — RESOLVED (fixed 9/10, 29/29 pass in separate-output test run).**
   These were real test bugs, not product bugs. Fixes (in `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests`, no product code changed):
   - `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests.ArtifactPolicy_Demonstration_WalksAMixedSession` — the local `AddToolCall` helper built tool messages with a top-level `name`/`content` shape that `LocalSessionArtifactPolicy.ListToolPaths` does not parse (it reads `message["tool_calls"]`). Replaced with the shared `Call`/`Text` helpers (proper OpenAI `tool_calls` shape) so edits/created/artifacts are now counted.
   - `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests.AutoCompact_walks_from_empty_to_full_and_fires_at_the_threshold` — two bugs: (a) the `messages` array was re-wrapped in a fresh `JsonObject` every loop iteration, throwing "The node already has a parent"; now wrapped in ONE `prompt` object reused across the walk (and the post-loop token check reuses it too). (b) `resultSize` was too small (4 KB → ~1000 tokens/turn) to ever cross the 80% threshold; raised to 16 KB (~4000 tokens/turn) so the walk fires compaction. (c) the post-compact assertion compared a token *count* against `TargetAfterCompact` (a *fraction*); now compares against `TargetAfterCompact * window`.
   - `LocalHistoryCompactionTests.Auto_compact_decision_walks_the_context_window_from_empty_to_full` — the "full 20 turns" slice was too small (~10,400 tokens) to cross the 80% trigger (26,214 tokens for a 32,768 window); enlarged each turn (user 2000 / tool 4000 / reply 2000 chars → ~32,000 tokens) so the full case fires and the empty case stays below.
   - **Verified:** separate-output `dotnet test` → `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests` + `LocalHistoryCompactionTests` = 29/29 pass.

2. **Quick Select model-profile options don't match the Model profiles tab (user suspects a Windows crash/reboot).**
   **RESOLVED (fixed 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED).**
   **Root cause (confirmed in config):** Quick Select slot 2 pointed at `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::YaRN test with vision`, which no longer exists in `LocalProfiles` (the profile was renamed/deleted after the slot was assigned, consistent with a crash/reboot mid-edit).
   **Fix:** added `RepairStaleQuickSelectSlotAssignments(JsonArray)` in `MainViewModel.cs`, called at the top of `InitializeQuickSelectSlots()`. It checks each slot's `localModel::variant` (or `provider::model::variant` for cloud) against the live `LocalProfiles`/`CloudProfiles` keys; if the profile no longer exists, the slot is reset to an unassigned state (routeType/model/variant/validatedProfileKey cleared) and the config is saved. The user then sees an empty slot they can re-assign, instead of a stale variant that doesn't match the Model profiles tab.
   - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (new method + call in `InitializeQuickSelectSlots`).
   - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1574 pass.
   - **Deploy:** close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

3. **Editing a profile's Context compaction mode + re-saving shows the previous setting.**
   **RESOLVED (confirmed by user after recompile 9/10).** The auto-compact block persistence fix (`WriteLocalVariantSettingsFields` now writes `LocalAutoCompact*`) was in source but not deployed; user recompiled and confirmed it now works.

4. **"Extend context into RAM" (How far) dropdown shows blank after a selection.**
   **RESOLVED (fixed 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED).**
   **Root cause:** the "How far" ComboBox binds `SelectedItem` to `SelectedYarnContextOption` (a `YarnContextOption` object). `RefreshYarnMaxContextWizardOptions` rebuilds the options list on every change with **new instances**, so the ComboBox's `SelectedItem` no longer references an item in the new `ItemsSource` and renders blank.
   **Fix:** in `RefreshYarnMaxContextWizardOptions`, clear the list and re-apply the matching option object via `SetSelectedYarnContextOptionSafely`, which guards against re-entrancy (a cascading `OnSelectedYarnContextOptionChanged` re-entering the refresh loop). Also removed the "How far:" label from the XAML (it was pushing the dropdown outside its host column) and set the ComboBox to `HorizontalAlignment="Stretch"` so it fills the column width.
   - **Crash regression (fixed):** the initial fix nulled `SelectedYarnContextOption` before clearing the list, which fired `OnSelectedYarnContextOptionChanged` mid-binding-update and crashed the app when the checkbox was ticked. Reverted the null-before-clear and replaced it with the `SetSelectedYarnContextOptionSafely` re-entrancy guard. Same pattern applied to the KV cache dropdown.
   - **Compact display strings (9/10):** the "How far" option labels were too wide for the host column even after removing the "How far:" label. Compacted the format: fixed targets now read `384K=256K VRAM+128K RAM` (was `384K (256K in VRAM, 128K in RAM)`) and Auto reads `Auto=384K total (256K VRAM+128K RAM)` (was `Auto (384K total: 256K in VRAM, 128K in RAM)`). Changed in `BuildYarnMaxContextWizardOptions` (both the Auto and fixed-target `Display` strings) + the `YarnContextOption.Display` doc comment.
   - **Auto-compact mode dropdown was missing from XAML (regression, fixed 9/10):** the "Context management:" section had the Auto-compact checkbox + Trigger/Keep turns/Tool results/Pin user rows, but the **mode dropdown** (Port rules default / Profile-specific / None / endpoint managed) was never added to the XAML — the ViewModel had `SelectedLocalAutoCompactModeOption` + `LocalAutoCompactModeOptions` but no ComboBox bound to them. Added the ComboBox between the checkbox and the Trigger row, and gated the Trigger/Keep turns/Tool results/Pin user sub-settings on `LocalAutoCompactMode == "per-profile"` (via `StringEqualsConverter`) so they only show when Profile-specific is chosen.
   - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (re-entrancy guard + safe setter in `RefreshYarnMaxContextWizardOptions`; removed null-before-clear in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (removed "How far:" label, removed MinWidth/MaxWidth, added HorizontalAlignment=Stretch).
   - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1574 pass.
   - **Deploy:** close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

5. **KV cache selection dropdown shows blank after a selection.**
   **RESOLVED (fixed 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED).**
   **Root cause:** same class of bug as #4 — the "KV cache for the extended context" ComboBox binds `SelectedItem` to `LocalKvCacheWizardChoice` (a string) and `ItemsSource` to `LocalKvCacheWizardOptions`. `OnLocalKvCacheWizardChoiceChanged` → `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo` clears and rebuilds the list; the ComboBox lost its selection during the clear.
   **Fix:** in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`, clear `LocalKvCacheWizardOptions` and re-apply the matching choice after the list is rebuilt (existing fallback-to-"Auto" logic handles the case where the choice is no longer in the list). Removed the null-before-clear that caused the crash regression (see #4).
   - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (removed null-before-clear in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`).
   - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1574 pass.

6. **KV cache dropdown does not appear at all when "Extend context into RAM" is ticked.**
   **RESOLVED (fixed 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED).**
   **Root cause:** the KV-cache block was gated by `ShowLocalKvCacheWizardChoice = extendIntoRam && effectiveContext > 256*1024`. If `effectiveContext` computed to ≤ 256K for the user's profile/hardware, the block was hidden even though the checkbox was ticked.
   **Fix:** relaxed the gate to `ShowLocalKvCacheWizardChoice = extendIntoRam` (always show when extension is on). The info text still explains the size when the context is small, but the control is now always available.
   - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (relaxed gate in `UpdateLocalKvCacheWizardInfo`).
   - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1574 pass.
   - **Deploy:** close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.

7. **Typing "retry" after a port-rule halt → very long delay (sometimes minutes); subsequent requests also delayed.**
   After a port-rule stop, the local model/llama-server state may be left half-open. On "retry" the gateway re-enters the local route and can sit in a long startup-retry / warmup-probe / circuit-breaker backoff (`LocalLoadingRetryPolicy`, `LocalLaunchStartupRetry`, `CloudCircuitBreaker`/`Retry-After`), and a stuck in-flight request blocks the single local route so later requests queue behind it. **Action:** add a fast "retry" fast-path that (a) detects the prior port-rule halt, (b) cancels any stale in-flight local request, and (c) bounds the startup-retry/backoff so a retry responds quickly or surfaces a clear "model not ready" instead of hanging for minutes.

   **Steer-coverage audit (10/11):** "Let me steer" (`RouteRecoveryPolicy.SteerStatus` / `SteerLabel`) is only offered through `AskPortRulePauseAsync` (source `port_rule_pause`), which the UI shows a Steer button for (`RecoverySteerVisible = portPause`). The three **pre-forward** halts all call it and therefore offer steer: repeated-command (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:815`), tool-mill/rapid-churn/observe-only (`:864`), diagnostic-dump (`:917`). The **post-forward / in-stream** halts do NOT offer steer — they end the turn with a 400 + `PortRuleStopAdvice` ("This Client-app turn is over. Another message in this chat will hit the same stop or stall on 'reconnecting'." + "try typing 'retry'"):
   - **think-only** (non-stream `:1322`, stream `:1245`) — `FormatThinkOnlyMessage` → `WithStopAdvice`.
   - **503 loading** (`:1134`) — `WithStopAdvice(FormatLoading503)`.
   - **context overflow** (`:786` pre-forward, `:1123` post-forward) — `FormatLocalContextOverflowMessage` → `FinishClientError` → `WithStopAdvice`.
   - **hang** (`WatchLocalStreamHangAsync`/`AskHangWaitAsync`, source `local_hang`) — offers "Continue waiting" / "Switch" / "End this turn" but NO steer (UI `RecoverySteerVisible = portPause` only).
   The steer notice (`FormatSteerNotice`) is the *good* outcome: it ends the turn with "AI-FluxMux paused this turn so you can steer in the Client app. … Send another message in this chat with a narrower ask, or raise the Port rule." The 400 stop-advice is the *bad* outcome (tells the user the chat is stuck). **Fix direction:** route the post-forward halts (think-only, 503, context-overflow, hang) through the same pause/steer offer so there is always an opportunity to steer before the turn is declared over — at minimum surface the steer notice text instead of (or in addition to) the "turn is over / retry" stop advice.

   **Context-overflow + "launch larger-context model" (10/11):** the overflow warning is *correct* to offer a larger-context model. `OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded` (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:1569`) → `LocalReloadRouting.DecideOffer` (`LocalReloadRouting.cs:32`) fires when `promptExceeds` (prompt + headroom > `local_context`); `PickCandidate` searches the `local_reload_pool` for a profile with a bigger context, and `BuildOfferReason`/`BuildLeadReason` (`LocalReloadRouting.cs:248/318`) produce "the loaded model profile's Context is too small. **Launch a model profile with higher Context** if this local model can hold more." So the overflow path already does exactly what the user suggested — it just needs a *larger-context* profile in the pool.

   **Bug: spurious overflow warnings when switching compaction mode (per-profile → port-rules / none) — CORRECTED AGAIN (10/11).** The user reports the warning fires when the context is **NOT very close to full**. The precise mechanism:
   - `DecidePromptedRoute` returns `OfferReload: true` for **every** local turn when routing is on (`FluxMuxGatewayRouting.cs:649`), so `OfferLocalReloadIfNeeded` runs on every turn.
   - `DecideOffer` (`LocalReloadRouting.cs:32`) fires when `nearLimit` OR `promptExceeds`. `nearLimit = !promptExceeds && IsNearLimit(turnTokens, hotContext)` (`:44`).
   - `IsNearLimit` (`LocalHistoryCompaction.cs:20`) is true when `promptTokens >= contextTokens * CompactWatermark` (default **85%**) AND `promptTokens + 1 + CompactHeadroom <= contextTokens` (i.e. not actually overflowing). So the warning fires at the **85% watermark**, not at 100%.
   - `promptTokens` is the **4-chars/token** estimate (`EstimatePromptTokens` default `charsPerToken = 4`). For a dense tokenizer (Qwen, ~3–4 chars/token) the 4-char estimate is roughly accurate, but for a *sparse* pack (lots of whitespace, JSON, code with long identifiers) the real token count is **lower** than chars/4, so the estimate **overstates** the true fill. Conversely the 85% watermark is a *fixed* fraction of the window.
   - **Why "not very close to full":** the warning is triggered at 85% of the *estimated* tokens, and the estimate can be high. More importantly, when compaction is **on** (per-profile/port-rules), `TryApplyForwardCompact` shortens the payload at the **80%** auto-compact trigger *before* `DecideOffer` runs, so the post-compaction prompt is back under 85% → `nearLimit` is false → **no warning**. When compaction is **off** (none), nothing shortens the payload, so the prompt stays at/above 85% → `nearLimit` is true → **warning at 85%**, which the user perceives as "not very close to full" (it's 85%, not 95%+).
   - So the warning is *technically* firing at the 85% watermark, which is by design (`CompactWatermarkPercent`), but it feels premature because (a) 85% isn't "full," and (b) the 4-char estimate can overstate the true fill for sparse packs. **This is the same root as the over-aggressive compaction fix** — the 4-chars/token estimate + 85% watermark is the aggressive trigger. The compaction fix gated the *2-char* filling estimate; the *4-char* estimate + 85% watermark that drives `nearLimit`/`IsNearLimit` is still ungated.
   - **Fix direction:** (a) raise the effective watermark for the *offer* (e.g. only offer at 90–95% instead of 85%), or (b) when compaction is off, reframe the message to "compaction is off, so this chat is approaching the Context limit (85%) — turn compaction back on, or launch a larger-context model," or (c) make the offer watermark a Port-rule setting (it already is: `CompactWatermarkPercent`) so the user can raise it. The cleanest is (b)+(c): the message should say "approaching the limit because compaction is off" rather than "Context is too small," and the user should be able to raise `CompactWatermarkPercent` to stop the early warning.

   **CONFIRMED in live config (10/11):** `c:\AI_Workbench\Workspace\.vscode\fluxmux_config.json` → `LocalProfiles` shows the user's actual modes:
   - `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::vision` → **none**, `OverrideContext` = **262144**
   - `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::max VRAM only context` → **none**, `OverrideContext` = **213056**
   - `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::(defaults)` → **none**, `OverrideContext` = **393216**
   - `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::Test yarn` → **per-profile**, `OverrideContext` = **393216**
   - `Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-MTP-Q8attn.gguf::YaRN test with vision` → **per-profile**, `OverrideContext` = **393216**
   `local_context` is set from `OverrideContext` (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:3245`). So the per-profile profiles have a **393216-token window** (80% trigger = 314572 tokens) while the `none` profiles have **262144 / 213056** (80% = 209715 / 170445). A long chat that fits under 314K tokens compacts silently in the per-profile mode but trips the overflow warning in the `none` mode (209K/170K trigger) — **exactly the user's report.** The settings ARE being applied (the modes are present in the config and `GetLocalAutoCompactSettings` reads them), so this is the "warning only in `none`" scenario, not a key-mismatch bug. The per-profile mode's larger window (393216) is why it doesn't warn; the `none` mode's smaller window + no compaction is why it does.

   **DEFINITIVE ROOT CAUSE — warning at ~20% full after a compaction-mode change (10/11).** The user reports the warning fires when the context is only ~20% full, right after changing the compaction mode. This is NOT a fresh overflow — at 20% the prompt is nowhere near the window, so `PromptExceedsContext` / the 85%/92% watermark genuinely cannot fire. The mechanism is **stale persisted offer state re-surfacing**:
   - The overflow/reload offer is written to `Proxy_Local_Reload_Recommend.json` with `status: "pending"` (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:1665`) and **persists** until the user answers it (Load / Compact / Decline).
   - `ReadLocalReloadRecommend` (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:1289`) returns a snapshot **whenever the file has `status: "pending"`** (only `idle`/`yes`/`no`/blank return null, `:1299`). It does **NOT** re-check whether the context is actually full.
   - `RefreshLocalReloadRecommendBanner` (`MainViewModel.cs:3668`) shows the banner whenever that snapshot is non-null (`:3691`). It does not re-evaluate the prompt size either.
   - **So:** when the chat was genuinely large (e.g. near the window in a `none`/no-compaction mode), the offer was written and the banner shown. If the user did not answer it (or the chat later shrank / a new chat started / the mode changed), the `pending` offer **stays in the file**. Any subsequent banner refresh (including one triggered by changing the compaction mode, which calls `RefreshLocalReloadRecommendBanner` / `RefreshCloudRecommendBanner`) re-reads the stale `pending` file and re-shows the "context is full / chat cannot continue / Headroom is 350" banner — **even though the context is now only ~20% full.** The "350" is `compactHeadroom` from `port_forwarding_rules.json`, named by `FormatContextOverflow` (`PortRulesPostMortem.cs:354`).
   - **Why it correlates with the mode change:** changing the compaction mode triggers a banner refresh (and a config save), which re-reads the stale `pending` offer. The offer is not invalidated by the mode change, so it re-appears. The user perceives "I changed the mode and now I get a context-full warning" when really it's an old, un-answered offer from when the chat *was* large.
   - **Fix direction:** (a) **invalidate the pending local-reload offer when the compaction mode changes** (clear `status` to `idle` in `Proxy_Local_Reload_Recommend.json` on mode change), and/or (b) **re-validate the offer before showing it** — in `RefreshLocalReloadRecommendBanner` / `ReadLocalReloadRecommend`, re-check the *current* prompt size against the window and drop the offer if the context is no longer near the limit (e.g. if the live prompt is now well under the watermark). (c) The offer should carry a `neededContext`/`hotContext` snapshot (it already does, `:1679-1680`) that the banner can compare against the *current* prompt to detect staleness. Option (a) is the minimal, targeted fix for the reported symptom; option (b) is the robust fix that prevents all stale-offer re-surfacing.
   - **BOTH FIXES IMPLEMENTED (10/11, compiled clean, full suite 1583 pass, NOT DEPLOYED):**
     - **(a) Invalidate on mode change:** `MainViewModel.OnSelectedLocalAutoCompactModeOptionChanged` now calls `_runtimeService.ClearLocalReloadRecommend()` + `RefreshLocalReloadRecommendBanner()` when the mode actually changes. `ClearLocalReloadRecommend` is the pre-existing method (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:1619`) that sets the offer's `status` to `idle` (which `ReadLocalReloadRecommend` treats as no offer). This stops the exact reported symptom: changing the compaction mode no longer re-surfaces a stale pending offer.
     - **(b) Robust staleness re-validation:** `ReadLocalReloadRecommend` (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs:1291`) now reads the offer's stored `neededContext`/`hotContext` and, for a `pending` context-overflow/near-limit offer (excluding cloud-failover / spill-compact / document-context offers), calls the new `IsLocalReloadOfferStale(neededContext, hotContext)` helper. If the stored needed context is now **below 70%** of the window (well under the 85% compaction watermark), the offer is stale — it calls `ClearLocalReloadRecommend()` and returns null, so the banner does not re-appear. A genuinely near-limit offer (≥70%) is never dropped. Offers with no usable numbers (older files) keep showing (fail-safe). The `LocalReloadRecommendSnapshot` record gained `NeededContext`/`HotContext` fields.
     - **New test:** `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/LocalReloadOfferStalenessTests.cs` (4 tests) locks in the staleness threshold: 20%/50% → stale; 85%/95%/100% → not stale; exactly 70% → not stale, just under → stale; missing numbers → not stale.
     - *Files: `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (snapshot record + `ReadLocalReloadRecommend` staleness guard + `IsLocalReloadOfferStale`), `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (mode-change invalidation), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/LocalReloadOfferStalenessTests.cs` (new).*

### 2026-10-11 — Headroom error-message wording + dense-tokenizer hint (compiled clean, full suite 1583 pass, NOT DEPLOYED)
- **Headroom is already a visible port-rules field** (`MainWindow.axaml:1490-1494`, "Headroom" NumericUpDown 64–4096, bound to `PortRules.CompactHeadroom`). The "Headroom is 350" in the overflow error was *not* a hidden parameter — it was the value of that visible field. The real problem was the **error message wording**: it named a 350-token buffer right after "this turn was too large for the loaded Context," which reads as if the headroom were the cause.
- **Fix 1 — error message wording** (`Services/PortRulesPostMortem.cs:354` `FormatContextOverflow`): now says "this turn was too large for the loaded Context — the prompt already fills the window" and describes Headroom as "just the small buffer reserved for the reply; it is not what filled the Context." No longer misattributes the overflow to the headroom value.
- **Fix 2 — dense-tokenizer hint** (`Views/MainWindow.axaml:430`): added a small gray hint next to the per-profile "Trigger %" field: "Dense-tokenizer models (e.g. Qwen) may need a lower Trigger % to compensate." with a tooltip explaining why. This turns the model-characteristic question into a documented, user-driven tuning knob rather than hidden magic.
- **Decision: NO automatic model-characteristic threshold tuning.** There is no reliable per-model tokenizer-density signal at decision time, so auto-adjusting thresholds would be guesswork that could make compaction *more* aggressive for the wrong models (the exact bug just fixed). The per-profile manual knobs (`AutoCompactTriggerPercent` etc., `MainWindow.axaml:418-463`) already give full control where it matters; the trigger is computed as `autoContext * triggerPercent` (`FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs:1922`), so a dense-tokenizer model just needs a lower trigger %.
- *Files: `Services/PortRulesPostMortem.cs` (error message), `Views/MainWindow.axaml` (hint).*

8. **llama-server RAM leak: usage creeps up to fill all 64 GB even when FluxMux is idle; causes Windows to crash/reboot.**
   **MITIGATED (memory guard added 9/10, compiles clean, NOT DEPLOYED).**
   **Root cause (confirmed):** llama-server (not the FluxMux gateway — gateway is 0.31 GB, llama-server was 39.77 GB WS / 54.44 GB private) was launched with `--cache-ram -1` because **every profile has `LocalCacheRam=Unlimited`**. That flag means an *unbounded* RAM KV cache, so the server's RAM footprint creeps up over time (KV cache + context + offloaded tensors) and is never released while idle. With `IdleTimeoutEnabled=False`, the server never stops, so it can OOM the 64 GB machine → Windows crash/reboot.
   **Fix (defensive guard):** added a **memory guard** in `FluxMuxRuntimeService` that runs in the existing 30-second idle-timeout loop, *independent* of the idle-timeout setting. When available physical RAM drops below **4 GiB** (`MemoryGuardMinAvailableRamGiB`), it stops llama-server (via the same `StopLocalCoreAsync(preserveParkedForIdleWake: true)` path the idle-timeout uses) and posts a notice explaining why and how to reduce RAM use. A **5-minute cooldown** (`MemoryGuardCooldownMinutes`) prevents stop/restart flapping; the next chat reload re-tests the condition.
   - Files changed: `Services/FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs` (new `_lastMemoryGuardStopUtc` field, `MemoryGuardCooldownMinutes` + `MemoryGuardMinAvailableRamGiB` consts, `ConsiderMemoryGuardStopAsync` method, call at top of `ConsiderIdleUnloadAsync`).
   - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1574 pass.
   - **Deploy:** close app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.
   - **Orphaned-process fix (9/10):** the initial guard only fired when `IsManagedLocalAlive()` was true, which requires `_localServerProcess != null`. If the app was restarted and llama-server was already running from a previous session (or the process handle was lost), `_localServerProcess` would be null and the guard would skip it — so the leak continued. Added `HasOrphanedLlamaServerProcess()` (checks `Process.GetProcessesByName("llama-server").Length > 0`) and the guard now fires when **any** llama-server is running, not just the one this instance launched. `StopLocalCoreAsync` already handles orphaned processes via `StopStaleManagedLocalFromRuntimeState` + `TryStopLocalEndpointOwnerByPort`.
   - **Root-cause fix: capped "Unlimited" RAM cache (9/10):** the real problem was that `LocalCacheRam=Unlimited` passed `--cache-ram -1` (truly unbounded) to llama-server, so the RAM KV cache could creep up to tens of GB. Changed the launch-arg builder so "Unlimited" now caps `--cache-ram` at `max(16 GB, 60% of total physical RAM)` instead of `-1`. On a 64 GB machine that's ~38 GB — enough for the overflow context, but it can never consume the whole machine. This is the actual fix for "memory should never have got this high"; the memory guard above is the safety net if something else still runs away.
   - **Note (user action, recommended):** the guard *prevents the crash* but does not stop the leak. To actually reduce RAM use: (a) enable the idle timeout (Servers → Advanced) so the server stops after N idle minutes, and/or (b) set the profile's **RAM cache** to a fixed value (e.g. 8192) instead of **Unlimited**, and/or (c) lower the context size. The guard's notice message suggests these.

## Compile state (updated 9/10)

- **Reworded first-byte Port-rule hang message to handle "no model was running" (done in source, compiles clean 0 `error CS`, NOT DEPLOYED):**
  - **Problem:** the first-byte hang message assumed llama-server was running and just slow, so it told the user to raise **First-byte seconds** and warned "another message will hit the same stop." When the real cause was that **no local model was running** (or still loading), that advice was wrong — launching a model and retrying the same turn actually works.
  - **Fix (context-aware):** `PortRulesPostMortem.FormatHang` now takes a `bool localModelReady` (default `true`). The gateway passes `LocalTargetReady(state)` at the hang site. For the **first-byte** case:
    - **Model not ready:** lead becomes "Either the model stopped for some reason, or llama-server did not start a reply in time."
    - **Model ready:** lead stays "llama-server did not start a reply in time."
    - Both use the new closer `RetryOrNewChatAdvice` ("With a running model, you can try typing 'retry' to resume the chat, but may have to wait some time. Typically best to start a new chat, and consider setting it a narrower task.") instead of the blanket `PortRuleStopAdvice` ("will hit the same stop") + `NarrowerChatAdvice`.
    - The raise-advice now reads "You can raise **Port rule First-byte seconds** … If the selected model thinks first, raise **Port rule Max think first-byte** instead. Port rule changes can only apply to the next Client-app turn."
  - Scope: first-byte branch only. Stall / hang-quiet / wait-longer keep their current wording (they genuinely imply a live server that went quiet mid-reply).
  - Files changed: `Services/PortRulesPostMortem.cs` (new `RetryOrNewChatAdvice` constant; `FormatHang` + `HangWhatHappened` + `HangChangeAdvice` take `localModelReady`), `Services/FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (passes `LocalTargetReady(LoadState())`), `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/PortRulesPostMortemTests.cs` (updated first-byte test + new `localModelReady: false` test).
  - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. `PortRulesPostMortem` tests 17/17 pass. Full suite: 1571 pass, 3 pre-existing failures in `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests`/`LocalHistoryCompactionTests` (context-compaction area, unrelated — same 3 as before).

- **Fixed context-compaction mode (and sub-settings) reverting after "Save profile" (done in source, compiles clean 0 `error CS`, NOT DEPLOYED):**
  - **Root cause:** `WriteLocalVariantSettingsFields` (the method the **Save profile** button uses via `ApplyLocalVariantSettings`) wrote every local-variant field **except the auto-compact block** (`LocalAutoCompactEnabled`, `LocalAutoCompactMode`, `LocalAutoCompactTriggerPercent`, `LocalAutoCompactKeepTurns`, `LocalAutoCompactKeepToolResults`, `LocalAutoCompactPinUserChars`). So changing the Context compaction mode (or its per-profile sub-settings) and clicking Save never persisted the new value; the next load read the stale value back and the UI reverted. (The sibling `BuildLocalVariantSettingsObject` *did* include the block, which is why Validate/other paths looked fine.)
  - **Fix:** added the 6 auto-compact fields to `WriteLocalVariantSettingsFields`, matching the exact keys used by `BuildLocalVariantSettingsObject` and `LoadLocalVariantSettingsForm`.
  - **YaRN note:** the YaRN fields (`LocalExtendContextIntoRam`, `LocalUseYarn`, `LocalYarnMaxContext`, `LocalRopeScaling`) *were* already written by `WriteLocalVariantSettingsFields` and round-trip cleanly through `CoerceLocalOption`, so they were not part of this bug. If a YaRN setting still appears to revert, it's a separate issue — re-test after this deploy.
  - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (`WriteLocalVariantSettingsFields` now writes the auto-compact block).
  - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Full test suite: 1570 pass, 3 pre-existing failures in `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests`/`LocalHistoryCompactionTests` (context-compaction area, unrelated — same 3 as before this change).

- **Fixed "Stop" button not stopping an in-flight "Validate to endpoint" (done in source, compiles clean 0 `error CS`, NOT DEPLOYED):**
  - **Root cause:** the Quick Select **Stop** button (`StopLocalRouteAsync` → `_runtimeService.StopLocalAsync()`) kills the llama-server process but did **not** cancel the in-flight `ValidateLocalProfileToEndpoint` task. That task is stuck in `WaitUntilLocalPublicEndpointReadyAsync` — a loop that polls the (now-dead) endpoint every 4 s until its 5-minute `CancellationTokenSource` fires. So when validation keeps failing, the app keeps "validating" for up to 5 minutes and Stop appears to do nothing.
  - **Fix:** added a `_localValidateCts` field on `MainViewModel`. `ValidateLocalProfileToEndpoint` now creates/owns it (replacing the local `using var timeout` CTS) and resets it in `finally`. `StopLocalRouteAsync` now calls `_localValidateCts?.Cancel()` (before stopping the process) so the in-flight validation aborts immediately. The `catch (OperationCanceledException)` now distinguishes a user Stop (elapsed < 4 min) from a genuine timeout, so the status message says "Validate to endpoint was stopped." vs "...stopped because it took too long."
  - Files changed: `ViewModels/MainViewModel.cs` (new `_localValidateCts` field; cancel in `StopLocalRouteAsync`; CTS ownership + reset in `ValidateLocalProfileToEndpoint`; user-stop vs timeout message).
  - **Verified:** `dotnet build FluxMux.Avalonia.csproj` → 0 `error CS`. Targeted tests (LocalValidateLaunchPolicy / LocalLaunchStartupRetry / LocalLaunchWarmupProbe / LocalLaunchCountdownEstimate) 21/21 pass. Full suite: 1570 pass, 3 pre-existing failures in `LocalSessionArtifactPolicyTests`/`LocalHistoryCompactionTests` (context-compaction area, unrelated — confirmed still failing with this change stashed).
