New files: `Views/LocalLaunchRecoveryDialog.axaml(.cs)` + `ThemedDialog.LaunchRecovery()`. Hooked into `ApplyLocalLaunchOutcome` via `LooksLikeLocalOomFailure` + `HandleLocalOomRecoveryAsync`. (2) The "How far" dropdown now shows a "⚠ May not fit on this PC" advisory listing which fixed targets the RAM heuristic flags as risky (non-blocking — all options stay enabled). New property `LocalYarnMaxContextAdvisoryText` + `BuildYarnMaxContextAdvisoryText()`.
  - `8c8e1fd` — **Added RAM overflow risk warning.** When a local model's footprint (model + KV cache for the actual context) is likely to overflow into system RAM and trigger Windows paging (which can cause "device failure" messages, massive slowdowns, and reboots), a **red warning** now shows in the profile editor + is stored in the profile settings (`LastRamOverflowWarning`). New file: `Services/LocalRamOverflowAdvisor.cs`. Hooked into the launch path (`FluxMuxRuntimeService.cs`) and the footprint display path (`MainViewModel.cs`). **Works regardless of whether YaRN is on or off** — it's based on the *actual* launch settings (model, context, offload mode), not the cosmetic "How far" dropdown. This was added after the user reported slowdowns → "device failure" → reboots with YaRN *off* and context at 26,314 tokens; the warning will now surface if the effective footprint exceeds available RAM.
  - `522c011` — **Added abort hotkey + RAM overflow watchdog.** Belt-and-suspenders abort strategy for the "KV cache fills VRAM → RAM thrash → freeze" scenario: (1) **Ctrl+Shift+K** kills the local model process tree immediately — a fast-escape that works as long as the AI-FluxMux window is focused and the process is alive, even if the UI is sluggish. New `KillLocalModelNowCommand` in `MainViewModel` + `OnAbortHotkey` in `MainWindow`. (2) **RAM overflow watchdog**: a background timer (every 5s) monitors the llama-server's working set and auto-kills it if it exceeds 85% of total RAM for 30 seconds. This handles the case where the user can't reach any button. New `StartRamWatchdog`/`StopRamWatchdog`/`OnRamWatchdogTick` in `FluxMuxRuntimeService`, started on launch, stopped on kill/stop.
  - `fce5724` — **Added red "Stop model" button** to the right end of the Diagnostics top bar. Triggers `KillLocalModelNowCommand` (same as the Ctrl+Shift+K hotkey). Provides a visible, always-available abort button for the RAM/SSD thrash scenario — the user can see it at a glance and click it without hunting for the hotkey.
  - `25555b7` — **Fixed misleading "Estimated VRAM" label** when YaRN is on. When the context exceeds the native window, most of the KV cache lives in RAM, not VRAM. The footprint text now says "Estimated footprint (VRAM+RAM)" instead of "Estimated VRAM" to avoid the user misreading a 128 GiB footprint as "needs 128 GB VRAM".
  - `eb4b035` — **Fixed DeepSeek Harness "Check for updates" tooltip** in the Environment tab. Was saying "the default GitHub releases" but Harness is updated via npm, not GitHub. Changed to "the default npm registry" to be accurate and consistent with the "Update now" tooltip.
  - `8e8a9b1` — **Fixed wrong text "AI-FluxMux does not install or upgrade DeepSeek Harness"**: AI-FluxMux DOES update Harness via the "Update now" button (standalone updater runs npm). Changed to "AI-FluxMux can update DeepSeek Harness via the 'Update now' button" in 3 places: `DeepSeekHarnessVersion.FormatStatus`, `MainViewModel.HarnessUpdateStatusText`, and the test assertion.
  - `cfccb6a` — **Cleaned up update texts for all three apps** (AI-FluxMux, llama-server, DeepSeek Harness): follow a consistent pattern — "Click Check for updates to look for a newer [APP] on [SOURCE]. Clicking Update now starts the automatic update — AI-FluxMux closes, the updater backs up and replaces the files, then prompts you to restart. Set an update source only if you have a preferred alternate source of updates." For llama-server, added: "If you have recently changed GPU hardware, you may need to manually uninstall and reinstall with the correct version optimised for your hardware — future updates should then work." Shortened the button tooltips to be more action-oriented.
  - `e929534` — **Removed superseded default text from update status properties**: now that each app section has a static help text in the XAML, the default values of `UpdateCheckStatusText`, `LlamaServerUpdateStatusText`, and `HarnessUpdateStatusText` are redundant. Set them to empty strings so the status text only shows dynamic messages (Checking..., Up to date, New version available).
- **Panel: compiled + verified, NOT yet deployed.** The rebuilt panel is in source and the separate-output build compiles clean (0 errors). The *running* app still shows the old UI (its `bin\Debug\net10.0\FluxMux.Avalonia.dll` predates the XAML edit). **Deploy (user action):** close the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart.
- **Regression root cause (7/10):** the update buttons had been built into the ViewModel + `FluxMux.Updates.Core` + the WinForms updater, but the matching `MainWindow.axaml` buttons were never committed — the XAML had reverted to a pre-button version while the compiled DLL kept them, so the buttons appeared to "vanish" on the next build. **The specific "alternative source" layout the user remembered was never persisted anywhere** (not in any build, git commit, or stash) — it was lost in the same revert. The panel was therefore **rebuilt to spec** (three symmetric sections, alternative-source field + inlined buttons per app) rather than restored from a surviving copy.

## What works (verified)

- **Standalone updater + shared update core** (this session): the update/detection logic that was app-only is now a shared, UI-free class library so a standalone updater and the app use the *same* code (so the updater knows the current version and what should replace it, and uses the right install method per type).
  - `FluxMux.Updates.Core/` (new class lib, `net10.0`, no Avalonia): `LlamaServerFamilyFingerprint`, `LlamaCppReleaseMatcher`, `LlamaCppBuildNumber`, `LlamaServerUpdateCheck`, `LlamaServerUpdateService`, `UpdateCheck`, `UpdateManifestParser`, `AppVersionComparer` (+`FluxMuxAppInfo`), `DeepSeekHarnessVersion`, `DeepSeekHarnessUpdateCheck`, plus new `FluxMuxUpdateService` (AI-FluxMux in-place update) and `HarnessUpdateService` (npm update + config backup).
  - `FluxMux.Updates/` (new console exe, `net10.0`): one updater with subcommands `llama` / `fluxmux` / `harness`. Each re-runs the shared detection, refuses to run while AI-FluxMux is running, does a backup-and-replace into a **dedicated backup folder** (wiping previous contents), and prints a "what I did + how to recover" report. Run only while AI-FluxMux is closed; on success it tells you to restart.
    - `llama --server-dir <folder>`: full in-place update (tested).
    - `fluxmux --install-dir <folder> --feed-url <url>`: in-place update from the feed.
    - `harness [--dsh-path <path>]`: backs up `%USERPROFILE%\.dsh` then runs `npm install -g @deepseek-ai/dsh@latest` (guarded — reports clearly if npm is missing).
  - `LlamaServerUpdateService.UpdateAsync` gained an optional `backupIntoFolder` param: when set, the backup goes into that dedicated folder (timestamped subfolder, previous contents wiped) instead of a `<folder>.bak-<stamp>` sibling. In-app update keeps the old default.
  - The app now references `FluxMux.Updates.Core` (moved files are no longer in the app assembly; app csproj excludes `FluxMux.Updates*/**` from its own compile globs). `App.axaml.cs` registers the app's Harness launcher resolver via `DeepSeekHarnessUpdateCheck.SetLauncherResolver`.
  - Tests: `LlamaServerUpdateServiceTests.Update_backs_up_into_dedicated_folder_and_wipes_previous_backups` (new). All moved tests now target the Core namespace.
- **Split "Software updates" into per-software check buttons** (this session): the single "Check for updates" button (which silently checked the AI-FluxMux feed, llama-server nightlies, *and* DeepSeek Harness at once) is now three clearly-labelled buttons, each placed under its own software's description in the Environment tab. Group header renamed `AI-FluxMux updates` → `Software updates`.
  - XAML: `Views/MainWindow.axaml` — three sections (AI-FluxMux / llama-server / DeepSeek Harness), each with its own check button under its paragraph.
  - ViewModel: `MainViewModel.cs` — `CheckForUpdatesAsync` split into `CheckForFluxMuxUpdatesAsync`, `CheckForLlamaServerUpdatesAsync`, `CheckForHarnessUpdatesAsync` (each resets only its own status/availability flags). No other callers of the old combined method existed.
  - Note: this is a **clarity/UX** change only. The llama-server "Update llama-server now" button still only appears when the matcher finds an unambiguous newer zip — when the CUDA minor is ambiguous (multiple `cuda-12.x` zips on a nightly), it still falls back to the release-page link + status text. That underlying ambiguity is a separate open item.
- **In-app llama-server updater** (committed `3650192`):
  - "Check for updates" (Environment tab → "AI-FluxMux updates") also checks llama.cpp nightlies for a newer zip of the same Windows CUDA/Vulkan/CPU family.
  - "Update llama-server now" button appears only when a newer matching zip is found (`LlamaServerUpdateAvailable`). It downloads the matching server zip (+ CUDA DLLs zip), backs up the current folder, and swaps in place.
  - Services: `LlamaServerUpdateCheck.cs`, `LlamaServerUpdateService.cs`, `LlamaCppReleaseMatcher.cs`, `LlamaServerFamilyFingerprint.cs`.
  - Tests: `LlamaServerUpdateCheckTests.cs`, `LlamaServerUpdateServiceTests.cs` (15 passing).
- **Config-save crash fix** (this session): `FluxMuxConfigService.Save` now writes via `WriteAllTextResilient` (temp file + retry on `IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException` + atomic replace, never throws). Fixes the startup crash where a locked `.vscode/fluxmux_config.json` (held by a 2nd instance / editor / watcher) took the whole app down via `SaveWindowGeometry` → `Save`. Test: `FluxMuxConfigServiceTests.cs` (3 passing, incl. a locked-file repro).

## Test suite

- `dotnet test` (separate output dir, per AGENTS.md) → **1550 passing**. **1 pre-existing failure** unrelated to recent work: `FluxMuxUpstreamErrorClassifierTests.Endpoint_error_copy_is_short_and_has_no_hint` (status 400 / "exceeds the available context size" / Local). Fails on clean `main` too — not caused by recent work. 4 new YaRN tests added this session (`LocalPrioritySettingsCalculatorTests.Extend_context_*`).

## YaRN "Extend context into RAM" (this session — compiled + tested, NOT deployed)

A per-profile opt-in that lets AutoTune/wizard exceed the model's native context window by stretching it (YaRN) and letting the overflow live in ordinary memory, plus a manual "compact back onto the graphics card" prompt.

- **New per-profile settings:**
  - `LocalExtendContextIntoRam` (bool, default false) — the "Extend context into RAM (YaRN)" checkbox.
  - `LocalYarnMaxContext` (Auto / 512K / 768K / 1M, default Auto) — how far YaRN may stretch the context. **Auto** assesses installed RAM and picks a safe target; the fixed options are explicit targets (user manages other apps).
  - Both wired through `MainViewModel.cs` (property, options, change handler, save/load, all 3 capture methods, `BuildLocalVariantSettingsObject`), both fingerprints, and `MainWindow.axaml` (checkbox + "Max YaRN context" ComboBox).
- **RAM-bounded extension** — `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator.Calculate` gained `extendContextIntoRam` + `yarnMaxContext`. When on AND context is a top priority:
  - Target = min(user's YarnMaxContext target, RAM-bounded ceiling, 1M YaRN practical limit).
  - `RamBoundedYarnCeiling` computes how many tokens of KV fit in (total RAM − conservative reserve of max(16GB, 25%)), added to the VRAM-resident window.
  - `LocalRopeScaling=yarn` + smallest standard scale reaching the target (options now include 8×).
  - `LocalGpuOffloadMode` forced to `GPU + CPU`; `LocalCacheRam` raised to `Unlimited`; **`LocalFit` disabled** (overflow is meant to live in RAM).
  - The context NumericUpDown maximum rises to ~1M when YaRN is enabled (`LocalVariantContextMax`).
  - `ModelMaxCtx` now uses the model's **true native trained window** (from the GGUF `context_length`), not the editor-capped value — so extension can actually trigger.
- **Manual spill-compact prompt (reuses the existing routing/consent dialog)** — when a YaRN-extended session's live prompt exceeds the VRAM-resident context, `FluxMuxGatewayHost.OfferSpillCompactIfNeeded` surfaces the existing local-reload banner in "compact in place" mode (no auto-compaction). The runtime service stamps `local_extend_context_into_ram` + `local_vram_context` into state at launch; the host reads them. Clicking **Compact** → `AnswerLocalReloadRecommend(enableCompact: true)` → next turn auto-compacts the forwarded history back under the VRAM line.
- **YaRN context stats in model displays:**
  - Profile editor footprint text: `native window N (YaRN extends to M, ≈X GiB overflow in RAM)`.
  - Quick Select slot display: `ctx M (YaRN)` second line.
  - Loaded-model summary row: `ctx M (YaRN)`.
  - Variant-creation status message: "YaRN extends the native N window to M."
- **Effective KV bytes per token (hybrid-KV + Flash Attention aware):** the RAM-bounded YaRN ceiling and the footprint overflow estimate now use the *effective* KV bytes/token rather than the raw profile KV type. `LocalHardwareLaunchAdvice` gained `HybridKv` (populated from the GGUF shape / Qwen3.8 detection). `MainViewModel.ComputeEffectiveKvBytesPerToken()` resolves the profile KV type via `ResolveKvBytesPerToken`, then applies a 0.38 factor for hybrid-KV models and a 0.75 factor when Flash Attention is enabled. This value is passed into `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator.Calculate` (new optional `effectiveKvBytesPerToken` param) and used by `RamBoundedYarnCeiling` (new optional param, falls back to `advice.KvBytesPerToken` when null). The footprint overflow-GiB text uses the same effective value.
- **Tests:** 9 new in `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculatorTests` (Fit disable, YarnTargetFromOption mapping, RamBoundedYarnCeiling, effective-KV ceiling, 1M target, Auto RAM-bounded, scale selection, off keeps native).
- **Deploy:** NOT deployed — requires closing the app → `build-fluxmux.bat clean` → restart. The running app still has the pre-YaRN binary.

## Open backlog

Single source of truth for open work (consolidated from the former `TODO.md`, now deleted). Ordered by priority.

0. **Auto-load selects the WRONG profile on startup** — **FIXED 10/20 (compiled, NOT deployed).** The "last-used profile" now comes from the **most-recent-launch record**, not the UI selection or a write-on-close process. `FluxMuxRuntimeService.RecordLastLaunchedLocal(model, variant)` stamps `FluxMuxMeta.LastLaunchedLocal = { model, variant, utc }` on the launch-success path; `GetLastLaunchedLocal()` reads it; `MainViewModel.AutoLoadSelectedProfileOnStartupAsync` targets that record (falling back to the UI selection only when no launch has been recorded). `autoload_diag.log` now records `source=` and the full `lastLaunched=(...)`. **Verify after deploy:** launch variant A, then variant B, close (gracefully AND by kill), restart → auto-load must pick B.
1. **[TOP PRIORITY] Fix port-rules compaction — behaving badly** (reported 9/10): the port-rules fallback compaction misbehaves (may fire when it shouldn't, fire too early/late, or use wrong thresholds). Trace the full path in `FluxMuxGatewayHost.cs` (`TryCompactLocalPayload`, `TryApplyForwardCompact`) + `FluxMuxGatewayRouting.cs` (`EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext`, `PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact`, `MaxTokensForTurn`) + `LocalRequestOverlayRouting.cs` (`EstimatePromptTokens`, `PromptExceedsContext`). Verify trigger conditions, headroom/watermark math, and interaction with per-profile auto-compact.
   - **Root cause identified (10/11):** the aggressiveness is the **`fillingEstimate` term** in `LocalHistoryCompaction.ShouldForwardCompact` (line 66: `return promptExceeds || fillingEstimate || IsNearLimit(...)`). `fillingEstimate` comes from `FluxMuxGatewayRouting.PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact`, which adds a **pessimistic 2-chars/token** estimate (`LocalRequestOverlayRouting.FillingCharsPerToken = 2`) on top of the standard 4-chars/token + `promptExceeds` checks. For dense tokenizers (Qwen, ~3–4 chars/token) the 2-char estimate **doubles** the perceived token count, so `promptFill > hotContext` trips compaction at roughly **half** the real context fill (e.g. ~50% of a 32K window) instead of the intended ~85% watermark. `ShouldForwardCompact`'s own comment says it "used to wait for 85% of the 4-chars/token count" — the 0.2.1 commit `a476b15` added `fillingEstimate` to stop Cline/Harness 400-ing, but it over-corrected for dense tokenizers. The blocking path (`EvaluatePromptFillsLocalContext`) already *excludes* the 2-char estimate for exactly this reason (comment lines 206–218), but the compact path still uses it. **Fix direction:** gate the `fillingEstimate` term so it only fires when the standard 4-char estimate is already near the window (e.g. `promptStd >= ~0.6*hotContext`), or drop the 2-char term from the compact trigger and rely on `promptExceeds || IsNearLimit` (which is the safe, non-aggressive behavior). Pinned by `LocalHistoryCompactionTests.Forward_compact_only_runs_when_the_window_is_actually_tight` (lines 31–42) and `Compact_before_filling_400_can_clear_a_tool_heavy_history` (lines 46–83) — those tests encode the aggressive behavior and must be updated with the fix.
   - **FIX IMPLEMENTED (10/11, compiled clean, full suite 1579 pass, NOT DEPLOYED):** gated the `fillingEstimate` term in `LocalHistoryCompaction.ShouldForwardCompact` to a "filling zone": `var fillingZone = contextTokens > 0 && promptTokens * 5 >= contextTokens * 3;` (i.e. the standard 4-chars/token estimate is already ≥ 60% of the window). The return is now `promptExceeds || (fillingEstimate && fillingZone) || IsNearLimit(...)`. This keeps early-compact protection for genuinely tight windows (the 2-char estimate still fires once the 4-char estimate is ≥ 60% full) while stopping the over-trip at ~50% for dense tokenizers. `PromptFillsLocalContextForCompact` itself is unchanged (still reports the raw pessimistic signal); only the compact *trigger* gates it. Updated the two pinned tests to encode the corrected behavior: `Forward_compact_only_runs_when_the_window_is_actually_tight` now asserts 58%-full + filling → **False** (was True) and adds a 66%-full + filling → **True** case; `Compact_before_filling_400_can_clear_a_tool_heavy_history` now asserts the 54%-full tool-heavy pack does **not** auto-compact (was True) while force-compaction still works. *Files: `Services/LocalHistoryCompaction.cs`, `FluxMux.Avalonia.Tests/LocalHistoryCompactionTests.cs`.*

12. **YaRN context-extension dropdowns go blank when the user makes a change** (reported 10/10) — **RESOLVED (fixed 10/10, compiled clean, NOT DEPLOYED).** Root cause: both ComboBoxes bound `SelectedItem` to an object property (`SelectedYarnContextOption` / `SelectedKvCacheWizardOption`). When the options list was rebuilt (`Clear()` + `Add()` creates new object instances), the `SelectedItem` binding orphaned (still pointed to the old instance, which was no longer in the list) and displayed blank. The earlier `Equals`/`GetHashCode` fix alone was insufficient because the ComboBox's internal selection state uses reference equality. Fix: switched both ComboBox bindings from `SelectedItem` to `SelectedIndex` (an int, which re-resolves cleanly after the rebuild). Added `SelectedYarnContextWizardIndex` + `SelectedKvCacheWizardIndex` properties and updated the handlers to sync via index. *Files: `MainViewModel.cs` (index properties + handlers + sync logic), `MainWindow.axaml` (binding changes).*
13. **Compact the Auto "How far" display string + reduce ComboBox padding** (reported 10/10) — **RESOLVED (fixed 10/10, compiled clean, NOT DEPLOYED).** (a) Changed the Auto option's display string from `Auto → 384K (256K VRAM+128K RAM)` to `Auto → 384K=256K VRAM+128K RAM` (matching the fixed-target options). (b) Reduced the global `ComboBox` padding from `8,4` to `4,4` in `App.axaml` to tighten the text and reduce the "extra right padding" the user sees. *Files: `MainViewModel.cs` (line ~5218 `options[0].Display`), `App.axaml` (line ~152).*
14. **Reorganize YaRN + Auto-compact controls into a three-axis model** (planned 10/10): separate launch-time specialist settings (YaRN) from runtime coal-face settings (auto-compact), and put each where it belongs. The wizard stays simple (two tickboxes + priority order); the detail lives where it's relevant.
   **Three-axis model:**
   - **Axis 1 — Priority order** (wizard): the high-level intent ranking (Speed/Context/Quality/...). AutoTune tunes the derived settings (threads, context, KV type) to fill leftover room.
   - **Axis 2 — YaRN** (launch-time, specialist, opt-in): tickbox in **both** wizard and detailed settings column (synced). The *detail* (target, guardrails, orange/red warning) lives in the **detailed settings column**, shown when the tickbox is on. AutoTune **ignores** YaRN unless the user explicitly opts in.
   - **Axis 3 — Auto-compact** (runtime, coal-face, dynamic): tickbox in **wizard, detailed settings column, AND Quick Select profile** (three-way synced). The *controls* (3-way mode: Port-rules / Model-specific / None-endpoint-managed + thresholds: keep turns, trigger %, tool-results) live in the **Quick Select profile**, alongside temperature + other dynamic per-turn params. AutoTune configures the thresholds from the priority order (when mode = "Model-specific").
   **Layout:**
   | Control | Wizard | Detailed column | Quick Select profile |
   |---------|:------:|:---------------:|:--------------------:|
   | YaRN tickbox | ✅ | ✅ (synced) | ❌ |
   | YaRN target/guardrails/warning | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
   | Auto-compact tickbox | ✅ | ✅ (synced) | ✅ (synced) |
   | Auto-compact 3-way mode + thresholds | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
   **Sync rules:** (1) YaRN tickbox: wizard ↔ detailed, two-way. (2) YaRN target/guardrails/warning: detailed column only. (3) Auto-compact tickbox: wizard ↔ detailed ↔ Quick Select profile, three-way. (4) Auto-compact 3-way mode + thresholds: Quick Select profile only. (5) Explicit user choices win over inferred values.
   **Phases (each independently testable + deployable):**
   - **Phase 1:** Move YaRN target/guardrails/warning from wizard to detailed column. Keep wizard tickbox; add synced tickbox in detailed column. Wire the two-way sync. *Files: `MainWindow.axaml`, `MainViewModel.cs`.*
   - **Phase 2:** Move auto-compact thresholds + 3-way mode from wizard to Quick Select profile. Keep wizard tickbox; add synced tickbox in detailed column + Quick Select profile. Wire the three-way sync. *Files: `MainWindow.axaml`, `MainViewModel.cs`.*
   - **Phase 3:** Update AutoTune to ignore YaRN (unless opted in) + configure auto-compact thresholds from priority order. *Files: `MainViewModel.cs`, `LocalPrioritySettingsCalculator.cs`.*
   - **Phase 4:** Document the three-axis model + sync rules in STATUS.md.
   **Note:** The per-turn dynamic compacting (`OfferSpillCompactIfNeeded` + `TryApplyForwardCompact`) already exists and is unchanged — this plan only reorganizes the *configuration* UI.
   **Revised approach (10/12) — duplicate, don't move:** Rather than *moving* the auto-compact controls out of the Model Profiles tab, **duplicate** the context-management section into each Quick Select profile panel so the day-to-day tab shows the same controls without stripping them from the profile editor. Within the Model Profiles tab, the **profile-specific** auto-compact sub-settings (Trigger %, Keep turns, Keep tool results, Pin user, Max images) may be shifted over into the **Detailed settings** column, inside the **Fit and memory** section, so the wizard column stays lean. This keeps the three-axis intent (controls live where they're relevant) while avoiding a destructive move.

   **Graph:**
   - X axis label should read `Context used (tok)` not `Tokens`.

## Key invariants / do-NOT traps

- **Two copies exist.** The real git repo is `C:\AI_Workbench\Workspace\FluxMux.Avalonia`. A stale zip extract at `C:\Users\paulk\Downloads\AI-FluxMux-main\AI-FluxMux-main` (no `.git`) **lacks** the updater feature. Always build/run from the Workspace repo.
- The "Update llama-server now" button is intentionally hidden until "Check for updates" finds a newer matching zip — not a bug.
- **Update logic lives in `FluxMux.Updates.Core`, not the app assembly.** The app and the `FluxMux.Updates` exe both reference it. The app csproj excludes `FluxMux.Updates*/**` from its compile globs to avoid duplicate-attribute errors. Don't re-add those types to the app.
- The standalone updater must only run while AI-FluxMux is closed (it checks and refuses otherwise); Windows locks a running app's .exe/.dll so an in-place self-update is impossible while the app is running.
- Config path resolution: dev trees use `.vscode/fluxmux_config.json`; public installs use `%AppData%\AI-FluxMux` (see `FluxMuxConfigPaths.cs`).
- This project is **Avalonia**, not WinForms — the parent `Workspace/AGENTS.md` WinForms layout policy does NOT apply here.

## Context fidelity (how to survive auto-compaction)

The gateway auto-compacts chat history when context nears the window limit. The compaction (`compact_openai_history_payload` in `.vscode/fluxmux.ps1`, ~line 3788) is **lossy by design**:

- Keeps the **last 8 messages** verbatim (`context_compaction_keep_turns: 8`).
- Keeps **all `system` messages** verbatim.
- Everything older is flattened into a single system summary: each old turn → `role: <first 220 chars>`, only the **last 24 such lines** survive, whole thing **hard-capped at 5000 chars**.
- **Tool results are not preserved** — only `user`/`assistant` text is summarized; tool-call payloads are dropped.

Consequences: the original brief is the first thing truncated (220 chars) and eventually dropped from the 24-line window; established decisions survive only if they're in the last-8 tail or land in the per-line summary.

**Pattern to preserve fidelity across a series of tool loops:**

1. **Externalize the brief + key facts to disk early** (this `STATUS.md` is for that). It's a file, not chat history, so compaction can't truncate it.
2. **Re-read `STATUS.md` surgically after each compact.** The summary drops tool results and old text; re-reading the file you need is the cheap way to restore fidelity.
3. **Keep the brief short and self-contained** (ideally <200 chars) so it survives the 220-char-per-line summary *and* the 24-line window.
4. **Re-anchor explicitly at decision points.** Restate the 2–3 governing constraints in your own reply (stays in the verbatim last-8 tail) even if the summary loses them.
5. **Prefer files over chat for anything you'll need later** — decisions, file paths, exact command flags, "do NOT do X" traps. The compaction layer is lossy; the filesystem is not.

The honest limitation: the summary is a fixed 5000-char / 220-char-per-line / last-24-lines window, not a faithful transcript. The only robust answer is externalization: **the brief lives in a file, and the model re-reads it after compaction.**
